# Skia for the JBR 2D Rendering Stack — Project Ledger

*Since 2026-11-02 this file lives at `jb/project/tools/skia-eval/records/ledger.md`, tracked on the shared branch; the kit's `README.md` is the entry point. Earlier: `build/notes/skia-rendering-ledger.md`.*

*Tracking: **[JBR-10654](https://youtrack.jetbrains.com/issue/JBR-10654)** (Project, java2d, In Progress). Branch: `manuel.unterhofer/JBR-10654-skia-backend`.*

*Single-engineer experiment. This document is the single source of truth for the project: plan (§1–§9), decision log (§10), findings (§11), work log (§12), open items (§13). Keep every change here.*

*Goal: establish the concrete benefits of a Skia-based GPU backend for Java2D, and the options for replacing further parts of the Java graphics system with Skia implementations, measured against the team's current plan. Nothing in this document is a commitment to a path; estimates are based on code reading and public information and have not yet been validated with the JBR team, the IDE platform team, or design.*

## 1. Context

The JBR ships several Java2D rendering backends — Metal (macOS), Vulkan (Linux, Windows in progress), OpenGL, Direct3D 9, GDI, XRender, Wayland SHM — on top of a shared software rasterization layer (Marlin, the C/Java "loops", the pipe framework). The team's current direction is to consolidate on Metal and Vulkan and retire the legacy backends.

This experiment asks whether Skia — the 2D library behind Chrome, Android, Flutter's web target, and JetBrains' own Skiko/Compose — should play a role in that consolidation, and if so, which one.

### What we know at this point (to be verified)

- **Windows appears to be software-rendered in the IDE today.** `idea.properties` sets `sun.java2d.d3d=false`, JBR defaults D3D off, and Vulkan on Windows is gated behind an explicit force flag. The team's plan is Vulkan on Windows (IJPL-220582). Based on public driver information, Vulkan support looks weaker on Qualcomm Adreno (Windows on ARM), virtual machines, RDP sessions, and some integrated GPUs; we have no seat data yet to say how many users this affects.
- **Marlin is in the hot path even on accelerated backends.** From code reading: every antialiased non-rectangular shape, every wide stroke at ≥ 150 % scale, and SVG icon rasterization go through Marlin on the CPU, followed by a per-tile mask upload per repaint. How much frame time this costs on real IDE scenes has not been measured.
- **GPU acceleration of AA geometry is already planned.** The team's plan for 2026.3 includes "AA Geometry HW Acceleration (Marlin renderer for GPU)" (Alexey Ushakov, Laurent Bourgès, Nikita Gubarkov): generic Vulkan/Metal shaders that compute per-pixel coverage from edge data, tile-parallel processing, Blinn-style signed-distance coverage for curves, HW-accelerated mask calculation, and bypassing the render queue for tiles. This would remove most of the CPU rasterization and mask-upload cost described above without Skia, and it is the natural baseline for any Skia path-rendering comparison.
- **The shared rasterization layer is large but quiet.** Roughly 90k LOC (Marlin, loops, pipes, image), with low commit activity in the repository history. The font layer (`sun.font`, `libfontmanager`, ~32k LOC) is the most actively changed shared area.
- **A GPU backend in Java2D is relatively thin.** For Metal, roughly half of the native code is platform/presentation glue (layer, drawables, pacing, texture pool) that any renderer would need, and half is primitive rendering that a Skia-based backend would replace.
- **Upstreaming is part of the strategy.** There is a plan to upstream the Wayland toolkit and the Vulkan backend to OpenJDK; Metal already lives upstream (Project Lanai), so changes there can take the same route. A Skia-based backend could not realistically follow: OpenJDK is unlikely to accept a dependency on Skia. Paths a and b would therefore leave the rendering layer permanently JBR-only and would be a deliberate departure from the upstreaming strategy — one that has to be justified on its own terms. Upstreaming also carries its own cost (review cycles with Oracle's client-libs reviewers), which applies to path c.
- **Skia's own trajectory matters.** Chrome has moved to the Graphite backend on macOS; on Windows Graphite runs through Dawn (D3D11 primary, D3D12 functional) and its Stable rollout is not confirmed in public sources. Graphite has no native Direct3D backend and is reported not to run on WARP. The legacy Ganesh Direct3D backend — which Skiko uses on Windows — has been publicly flagged by the Skia team as a removal candidate. Skia does not promise API stability between milestones.

---

## 2. What Skia offers, and why it is worth evaluating

Skia is a complete 2D rendering library with one API (`SkCanvas`) over several interchangeable backends. The layers that are potentially relevant to Java2D:

| Layer | What it provides | Java2D today (from code reading) |
|---|---|---|
| **Canvas API** | Immediate-mode drawing: paths, rects, rrects, images, text, with paint/clip/transform state | `Graphics2D` — a close conceptual match |
| **Raster backend** | CPU rasterization via `SkRasterPipeline` (SIMD, runtime-dispatched), analytic anti-aliasing | Marlin (Java, tuned supersampling) + scalar C loops |
| **GPU backends** | Ganesh (legacy: GL, Vulkan, Metal, D3D12) and Graphite (Metal, Vulkan, Dawn → D3D11/D3D12/WebGPU); pipeline-state management, batching, resource caching | One hand-written backend per API |
| **GPU path rendering** | Tessellation, path atlases, dedicated rrect/oval ops with analytic coverage | AA shapes rasterized on CPU and uploaded as masks today; GPU coverage computation ("Marlin for GPU") planned for 2026.3 |
| **Effects** | Image filters (blur, drop shadow, color matrix), all Porter-Duff and advanced blend modes, gradients, runtime shaders (SkSL) | Porter-Duff only on GPU; blur/shadow via CPU `BufferedImageOp` |
| **Text** | `SkFont`/`SkTypeface` (FreeType or platform scalers), `SkShaper` (HarfBuzz), `SkParagraph` (line breaking, bidi, styling, fallback), glyph atlases, SDF text, COLRv1 color fonts | `sun.font` (FreeType/CoreText scaler, HarfBuzz shaper), `TextLayout`/`LineBreakMeasurer`, per-backend glyph caches |
| **Recording** | `SkPicture` display lists; Graphite multi-threaded `Recorder`s | None (immediate mode, single flusher thread) |
| **Color** | `SkColorSpace`, F16 surfaces, HDR transfer functions | sRGB-centric `ColorModel` |
| **Other modules** | SVG rendering (`SkSVG`), PDF output (`SkPDF`), image codecs | Separate: IJ's SVG renderer, Java printing/ImageIO |

Why this is an interesting consideration as a whole:

- **One implementation across GPU APIs**, maintained by Google for Chrome and Android. A Skia-based Java2D backend would be written once; new GPU APIs (and Windows coverage via Direct3D) would come from Skia rather than from a new in-house backend.
- **Capabilities Java2D does not have on the GPU** — path rendering, effects, blend modes, runtime shaders, a paragraph engine — could be exposed to the IDE through JBR APIs.
- **It is already in the building.** Skiko ships Skia inside IntelliJ for Compose, so the organization has build know-how and a maintained binary.
- **Potential for consistency**: if both CPU and GPU rendering go through Skia, the GPU-vs-software rendering mismatch that exists today disappears by construction.

The open questions are whether these benefits are large enough on real IDE workloads, what the visual and layout differences would be, and what the dependency costs. A further question sits outside the experiment: a Skia dependency is almost certainly incompatible with upstreaming the rendering layer to OpenJDK, so any Skia path has to be worth a deliberate break from that strategy. The experiment can quantify the benefit side of that trade-off; the decision itself is a leadership call.

---

## 3. The three paths

| | **a) Skia as the renderer** | **b) Skia as a GPU backend** | **c) Current plan (baseline)** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Scope** | Skia replaces the GPU backends, the software loops (where pixel formats allow), Marlin, `ProcessPath`, and the `sun.font` scaler/shaper/metrics. Java keeps font discovery/fallback and the `java.awt` / `javax.swing.text` API surface; `TextLayout` / `LineBreakMeasurer` backed by SkParagraph. All Swing text moves implicitly. | `SkiaSurfaceData` + pipes replace the Metal and Vulkan backends. Marlin, loops, `ProcessPath`, and the font stack unchanged. Routing rrects/paths to Skia's GPU path renderer optional. | The team's plan as it stands: finish Vulkan (Linux, then Windows); retire OpenGL, D3D9, GDI, XRender, X11; GPU-accelerate Marlin's AA geometry (2026.3). Small additions the experiment may suggest: GPU-blocklist data for Vulkan eligibility, `SharedTextures` for Vulkan, a blur/shadow JBR API. No new backend. |
| **Net LOC (from today, rough)** | ≈ −110k | ≈ −95k | ≈ −70k |
| **Rendering output** | Changes product-wide; text re-flow expected | Preserved for everything Marlin/loops produce | Preserved |
| **Windows coverage** | D3D11/12 via Graphite + Dawn; Skia raster as CPU fallback | Same | Vulkan-capable GPUs; software elsewhere |
| **Performance thesis** | Faster CPU rasterization; no CPU raster or upload for GPU shapes | No CPU raster or upload for shapes routed to Skia | Planned GPU Marlin removes CPU coverage computation and mask upload for all AA shapes |
| **New capabilities** | Full Skia surface incl. paragraph API | Everything except Skia text/raster; exposable via JBR APIs | blur/shadow API if added |
| **New external dependency** | Skia (rendering and text) | Skia (backend only) | None |
| **Fit with upstreaming strategy** | Break: rendering and text layers become permanently JBR-only; no upstream path for Skia | Break for the GPU backends; shared layers remain upstreamable | Aligned: Vulkan, Wayland, and GPU Marlin can go upstream (subject to Oracle review capacity) |
| **Effort to production (rough)** | 2–3 engineer-years + stabilization release | ≈ 1 engineer-year to Metal parity + build integration + transition | Already planned and staffed; additions ≈ 2–4 engineer-months |

Note on the LOC column: most of the ~40k that separates a from c is code with low change frequency. The size reduction is real; whether it translates into maintenance relief is not established.

---

## 4. Experiment plan (single engineer)

**Time box:** roughly three quarters for the full scope at prototype/measurement depth, sequenced so that each phase can end the experiment early. Production work for the chosen path follows separately. Hardware access (Windows ARM, VMs) and analytics support are external dependencies that may shift the schedule.

**Execution order.** Work that depends on code currently in flux (the team's GPU-Marlin research, JBR-10520/10521) or on coordination with its owners is deferred until that work lands; the experiment starts with the independent items and the Skia backend prototype:

1. WP 1.3 (dependency and build) — prerequisite for everything Skia-side.
2. WP 2.1 (Skia backend prototype on macOS), with WP 1.1 folded in as its baseline: profile the *shipped* Metal pipeline, not anything in development.
3. WP 1.4 (Skia raster vs. Marlin + loops) once a Skia library is available.
4. WP 2.2 (Windows) when hardware and a Dawn-enabled build are available.
5. WP 1.2 (alignment with GPU-Marlin) only after that work lands; re-run the WP 2.1b comparison against it then.

**Implementation choice for the prototype:** native C++ against `SkCanvas` from a new on-demand `libawt_skia`, interpreting the existing render-queue opcodes on the flusher thread and wrapping the existing presentation textures as `SkSurface`s — so the prototype measures the architecture that would ship. Skia binaries from the JetBrains/skia prebuilt releases (same milestone Skiko pins). Skiko is used as reference code (Metal/Vulkan/Graphite context setup) and for Java-level capability demos, not as the backend's binding: it cannot pin Java heap arrays for raster surfaces, its AWT module depends on `java.desktop` (dependency cycle), and per-primitive JNI would confound the comparison.

### Phase 1 — Where does the time go, and what will the current plan already fix? (≈ 8–10 weeks)

**WP 1.1 — Profile the current pipelines.** JFR + native profiling on three IDE scenes (editor scroll, islands hover/animation, settings dialog) on Metal, Vulkan, and the software path. Share of frame time in Marlin (`Renderer`, `Stroker`), `AAShapePipe`, mask upload, text, blits. *2 weeks.*

**WP 1.2 — Align with the AA-geometry HW acceleration work** (control arm; **deferred until JBR-10520/10521 lands**). Coordinate with its owners; agree on shared scenes and metrics so their GPU-Marlin prototype can serve as the comparison baseline for Skia's GPU path rendering in Phase 2. Contribute WP 1.1 results (share of frame time in coverage computation vs. mask upload, tile statistics, custom-paint/composite frequency on AA tiles) as input to their design. Where their prototype is not yet available, estimate from their design and record the assumption. *2 weeks, then ongoing.*

**WP 1.3 — Dependency and build cost.** *(Started 2026-10-13; findings in §11.1: the JetBrains/skia m155 prebuilt links and runs Graphite-on-Metal without a custom build; Windows prebuilts include Graphite + Dawn and an arm64 variant; the prebuilts use platform font hosts, so a FreeType-everywhere policy would require owning a Skia build.)* Skia in the JDK build vs. consuming Skiko's prebuilt binary; binary size; whether one Skia binary could be shared with the Compose plugin; API diff between two consecutive Skiko-pinned Skia milestones as an upgrade-cost sample. *2–3 weeks.*

**WP 1.4 — Skia raster vs. Marlin + loops** on `BufferedImage` scene replays and SVG icon rasterization (MapBench/J2DBench as micro-benchmarks). *2 weeks.*

*Checkpoint:* if Marlin + upload is a small share of frame time, or the planned GPU Marlin is expected to remove most of it, Skia's performance case on accelerated platforms is weak and Phase 2 can concentrate on Windows coverage, capabilities, and the software path.

### Phase 2 — Skia as a backend, and Windows (≈ 12–14 weeks)

**WP 2.1 — Skia-as-backend prototype on macOS.** `SkiaSurfaceData` + pipes reusing the existing `MTLLayer` presentation (wrap the layer texture as an `SkSurface`). Variant 1: Marlin-preserving (AA via `MASK_FILL`). Variant 2: Skia-rasterizing (`drawRRect`/`drawPath`). Frame time and power vs. Metal; pixel diff per variant. *8–10 weeks.*

**WP 2.1b — Skia GPU rrect/path vs. Marlin + mask upload** on the islands scenes, using WP 2.1 variant 2 against the Phase 1 baseline and, where available, the GPU-Marlin prototype. *Included in WP 2.1.*

**WP 2.2 — Windows coverage.** WP 2.1 prototype on Graphite + Dawn D3D11 (D3D12 where available) vs. the Vulkan backend forced on, across available hardware: discrete NVIDIA/AMD, Intel iGPU, Snapdragon X, a VM, an RDP session. Initializes? frame time; correctness; pipeline-compile startup cost. In parallel, request seat telemetry (GPU vendor/driver, Vulkan availability) from analytics. *4 weeks + hardware access.*

*Checkpoint:* b vs. c is decidable here if telemetry arrives.

### Phase 3 — Rendering differences and text (≈ 10–12 weeks)

**WP 3.1 — Rendering-difference corpora.** A/B today's pipeline vs. Skia-rasterized, classified by cause rather than pixel count: (1) synthetic primitive matrix (shape × stroke × AA × transform × composite × paint × clip) with diff heatmaps bucketed by cause — coverage drift, thin-stroke pixel selection, .5 tie-rounding, stroke geometry, clip edges, gradient dithering, image sampling, gamma/premultiplication; (2) Int UI component sheet, every state, 100/125/150/200 %, light/dark, headless; (3) a fixed Starter/Driver script through real screens with perceptual metrics (SSIM, ΔE) plus structural flags (bounding-box moves); (4) all SVG icons at each scale. Output: a catalogue of difference kinds with representative images. **We do not yet know whether any of these differences are visible in practice; the corpora exist to find out.** Design review is only requested if the catalogue contains differences that are perceptible at normal viewing. *6 weeks.*

**WP 3.2 — Text on Skia feasibility.** Prototype `FontDesignMetrics`/`GlyphVector` over SkFont/SkShaper on FreeType. Measure metrics drift vs. today across bundled fonts (JetBrains Mono, Inter) and common system fonts × sizes × scales; count components on the WP 3.1 sheet whose preferred size or truncation changes; collect appearance differences (hinting, gamma, LCD); verify determinism across machines (Lux projects server-rendered Swing pixel-exactly). The design intent is internal consistency — layout and rasterization from the same engine — not matching today's numbers exactly; the measurement tells us how large the one-time re-flow would be. *4–6 weeks.*

### Follow-on (outside the experiment; additions to the current plan)

GPU-blocklist data + matcher behind `VKEnv` (≈ 1 month); `SharedTextures` for Vulkan and accelerated paint in remote JCEF (≈ 2 months); blur/shadow JBR API scoping.

---

## 5. Text track

**Execution plan (started 2026-10-22, font engine = the prebuilt's platform hosts, user decision):**
- **T1 — scaler core and metrics drift.** `sun.font.SKFontScaler extends FontScaler` in `libawt_skia` (`SKFontScaler.cpp`): a typeface from the platform host (`SKPlatform_FontManager()` / `SKPlatform_TypefaceFromNativeFont()`: CoreText on macOS over the CTFontRef that `CFont` already holds, DirectWrite on Windows later, file-based elsewhere), one `SkFont` per strike context (size from the glyph transform's determinant, the remaining matrix applied to outlines and images; design metrics scaled linearly, no hinting, sub-pixel positions with fractional metrics, advances rounded without them as `CStrike` does). Probe `build/skia-smoke/FontMetricsDrift.java`: Skia scaler vs the platform strike, ascent/descent/leading and ASCII advances, fonts × sizes 8–32 × scales 1–2 × fractional on/off, whole-pixel advance flips. Gate: rounding-level drift.
  **T1 result (2026-10-22):** `SKFontScaler` on the CoreText host, probe over Dialog, Helvetica Neue, Menlo (CoreText `CFont`s) and JetBrains Mono, Inter (bundled-style `Font.createFont` fonts, which on macOS are **FreeType-rendered `TrueTypeFont`s**, not CoreText), sizes 8–32 × scales 1/1.25/1.5/2 × fractional metrics on/off, ASCII 32–126 (480 rows, 45,600 glyph advances): **ascent/descent/leading identical** everywhere (both engines scale design metrics linearly); **fractional advances identical** everywhere; whole-pixel advances revealed two conventions inside today's JDK — `CStrike` rounds in user space (`AWTStrike.m`: advance through `fFontTx`, then `round`), `FileFontStrike`/FreeType rounds in device space — and the Skia scaler follows either rule on request (`createScalerContext(matrix, deviceScale, …)` for CoreText-style strikes); with the rule matched: **0 misses** for the three CoreText fonts and JetBrains Mono, **41 of 4,560 (0.9 %) one-device-pixel misses for Inter** at sizes 8–20, FreeType's CFF hinting moving the advance where CoreText does not hint (an engine difference, the only drift of the kind the plan feared). Gate passed; committed as `03bd323e5c28` with `SkiaFontScalerMetricsTest` (168 strikes of the logical fonts, exact). Found on the way: the `nativeFontPtr` of a `CFont` is libawt_lwawt's `AWTFont` wrapper (its `fFont` NSFont is the CTFontRef), read through the Objective-C runtime because libawt_skia does not link libawt_lwawt. Probe: `build/skia-smoke/FontMetricsDrift.java`.
- **T2 — glyph images.** `getGlyphImage` fills StrikeCache's `GlyphInfo` (A8 grayscale, RGB for LCD, sub-pixel variants) by drawing the glyph with the `SkFont` into a raster; the existing Skia text pipe (`SKTextRenderer` → `SKGlyphCache`) consumes them unchanged, so text renders on Skia end to end with `-Dsun.java2d.font.scaler=skia`. Plug points: `CFont.createStrike` (macOS: a `PhysicalStrike` over the scaler), `FontScaler.getScaler` (Windows/Linux: `FileFontStrike` unchanged). Compare glyph bitmaps with the platform's (CoreText) per glyph: coverage differences, LCD filter.
  **T2 state (2026-10-22, evening):** glyph images from the scaler (`getGlyphImageNative`: A8 for grayscale/aliased, RGB coverage triplets for LCD from Skia's sub-pixel edging on an opaque RGB canvas, sub-pixel variants with fractional metrics as the FreeType scaler's), `sun.font.SKFontStrike` (a `PhysicalStrike` over the scaler, `CStrike`'s user-space rounding, images carry the advance), hooks `FontUtilities.useSkiaScaler` (`-Dsun.java2d.font.scaler=skia`), `CFont.createStrike` → `SKFontStrike`, `FontScaler.getScaler` → `SKFontScaler.forFile` for file fonts. **Text renders end to end** on the Skia GPU pipe with the Skia scaler; `GlyphAtlasEvictionTest`, `LcdTextBackgroundsTest`, `LcdTextDisposeTest`, `LcdTextLayersTest`, `SkiaFontScalerMetricsTest` pass with the property (the LCD backgrounds test needed Skia's text contrast/gamma off: `SkSurfaceProps(…, contrast 0, gamma 1)`, otherwise the masks carried Skia's 0.5 contrast boost). **Glyph bitmaps vs the platform's (software loops, probe `build/skia-smoke/TextCompare.java`, 40 rows: Dialog, Menlo, Helvetica Neue, JetBrains Mono, Inter × gray / gray+fm / LCD / aliased × 1x, 2x):** aliased text of the CoreText fonts **identical** (positions and outlines exact); **LCD within 28 levels, 0 px beyond 32, ink +2…5 %** (Skia's CoreText LCD ≈ the JDK's); **grayscale is the open point:** the JDK renders gray glyphs with CoreGraphics font smoothing but without sub-pixels and averages the channels, a mode Skia's host does not expose — Skia's plain coverage has 14–15 % less ink (max 44–143, mostly edge pixels: stems identical in a scanline, edges 124 vs 75), the sub-pixel render averaged 13–25 % more (max 57 at every edge), a stroke-and-fill darkening of 0.3 px lands at ink 0.98–0.99 at 2x but 1.03–1.11 at 1x, which says CoreGraphics' widening is proportional to the size (≈ 0.013 em: 0.17 px at 13 px, 0.35 px at 26 px); knob `sun.java2d.skia.text.gray=coverage|smoothed|darkened` with `sun.java2d.skia.text.grayDarkening` (em fraction, default 0.013) for the design triage; **darkened at 0.013 em: ink 0.999 (Dialog, both scales), 0.974/0.994 (Menlo 1x/2x), 0.965/0.998 (Helvetica Neue), max 72–131 and 88–832 px beyond 32 per row from the edge pixels of Skia's path rasterizer vs CoreGraphics' (coverage: 130–1946 px beyond 32).** Default stays `coverage` (platform-consistent, user leaning 2026-10-22); the four macOS text quirks are in §18. Committed as `8788b1c0b33e` (T1 `03bd323e5c28`, strokes `a72176186cc6` before it; 28 commits on `beee138cfe19`), pushed to `origin` and the fork; the full Graphics2D suite (16 tests) passes with the property off, the text tests also with it on. **Bundled fonts (FreeType today) change engine:** JetBrains Mono and Inter differ in every mode including aliased (hinting: FreeType hints, CoreText does not; Inter 13 px aliased visibly heavier on Skia), except gray + fractional metrics at 2x (max 48–49, ink 0.985–0.996: no hinting on either side there); their LCD ink +28…63 % (FreeType's light LCD filter vs CoreText's). This is the WP4 "appearance" item for design; the alternative is FreeType-everywhere (custom Skia build).
- **T3 — IDE text.** Editor and UI scenes with the Skia scaler: pixel comparison against today's text, re-flow check (component preferred sizes), performance (strike creation, glyph upload), fallback fonts and emoji (colour glyphs: `GlyphRenderData` path).
  **T3, first step (2026-10-22, evening): Swing component sheet** (`build/skia-smoke/ComponentSheet.java`, cross-platform L&F, 14 components incl. JTree/JTable/JTabbedPane/HTML label, painted headless at 1x and 2x, platform scaler vs Skia scaler): **no preferred-size difference on any component (no re-flow)**; pixels max 29 (1x) / 42 (2x) levels, 0 / 2 px beyond 32, ink ratio 1.002 / 1.001 — Swing draws through the desktop text hint, which is LCD here, the mode where the two paths agree.
  **T3, IDE smoke run (2026-10-22, late morning):** IntelliJ 2026.3 EAP on the Skia image through `run-eap.sh` with `-Dsun.java2d.font.scaler=skia`: UI text (menus, project tree, notifications) renders correctly; **the editor failed to open** with a `ClassCastException` from `SKFontScaler.getGlyphOutlineBounds` (`getBounds2D()` is a `Rectangle2D.Double`, cast to `Float`) reached from `EditorView.createNewMetrics` → `GlyphVector.getVisualBounds` (40 stack traces, one IDE error balloon); the platform-scaler run on the same image: 0 errors. Red test first: `GlyphVectorBoundsTest` (visual bounds of a glyph vector vs the bounds of its outline, logical fonts × 9/13/24 pt × 1x/2x, platform and Skia run lines). It caught a second defect right after the first fix: `SKFontStrike.getGlyphOutlineBounds` mapped the bounds to user space while the glyph vector expects device space (as `CStrike`/`FileFontStrike` return them), so visual bounds were half-size at 2x. Both fixed, folded into the T2 commit (`5de299e41330`, pushed); **IDE rerun with the Skia scaler: 0 log errors, 0 exceptions, the editor opens** (`/tmp/sheet/idea-skia-text2.png`). T3 next steps: pixel comparison of editor and UI text at 2x against the platform scaler on identical IDE state (the two captures differ in IDE state, so no pixel diff yet), strike-creation and glyph-upload cost (JFR on the scaler's natives vs CStrike), fallback and colour glyphs (CoreText fallback codes are negative in `CFont`'s mapper and come back empty from `SKFontStrike`: an open functional gap before any wider use).
  **T3, fallback and colour glyphs (2026-10-22, midday):** red test `FallbackGlyphsTest` (CJK, Greek, Cyrillic, Hebrew, Arabic, emoji with Dialog, ink per character ≥ ⅓ of Latin's and non-empty visual bounds, platform and Skia run lines): the Skia line had 0 ink for CJK. Implemented: `SKPlatform_FallbackTypeface` (macOS: CoreText's `CTFontCreateForString` from the `fFallbackBase`/primary font as `CTS_CopyCTFallbackFontAndGlyphForUnicode` does, one `SkTypeface` per distinct fallback CTFont through a `CFEqual` cache), `resolveGlyph` in the scaler for negative codes with a per-scaler code-point cache, `SKFontStrike` fallback image cache with its own disposer record; glyphs without an outline (Apple Color Emoji) are drawn in colour into **premultiplied BGRA records** (the JDK's colour glyph format, the Skia pipe composites them as images) — emoji ink matches the platform's exactly (100,597 per character); glyph bounds from the outline where there is one (exact, as CoreText reports) and from the host for bitmap glyphs (whole pixels). Green: fallback, bounds, metrics tests. Scripts' ink vs platform: CJK 49,966 vs 59,301, Greek 33,645 vs 39,380, Cyrillic 34,382 vs 39,723, Hebrew 24,978 vs 28,989, Arabic 18,713 vs 22,753 — the 14–16 % grayscale coverage gap again, consistent across scripts. Committed as `309b521cf1e3` (29 commits), pushed; Graphics2D suite 18/18 with the property off, TextRender 11/11 with it on. **Cold cost (`/tmp/sheet/ScalerColdBench.java`, 171 new strikes × 95 ASCII glyphs, first draw into a BufferedImage, fractional metrics so grayscale makes 4 sub-pixel images per glyph; M1 Max, two runs each):** platform 19.7–19.9 µs/glyph gray, 8.2–8.3 LCD; Skia scaler 21.2–21.4 gray (+8 %), 9.9–10.1 LCD (+20 %); warm redraw of the same strikes 14.8 ms on both (identical, the images are cached either way). Strike creation and rasterization are not a performance concern.

  **T3, IDE scene pipeline on the Mac (2026-10-22, afternoon):** the VM's instrument 3b was handed over in full (`build/notes/text-quality/2026-10-20`: `IdeScenes` (ten Robot-driven scenes), `SceneDiff`, `DeviationClusters`, the seed configuration with the nondeterminism removed, the `text-scene` project, run scripts, and the Windows results). Ported to `build/text-quality-mac/`: `run-ide.sh` after `run-eap.sh` with arms **M0** Metal, **T0** Skia pipe + platform scaler, **T1** Skia scaler (coverage), **T2** darkened, **T3** smoothed; `run-scenes.sh` with `pgrep`/`osascript` instead of PowerShell; `fg.sh` (frontmost process and activation through System Events); `IdeScenes` platform-aware (Command for the primary modifier, Cmd+Up for document start, F1 for quick documentation, Cmd+, for settings, Cmd+O / Cmd+Shift+O for class / file navigation, the hamburger "Main Menu" action instead of the Alt key, `ls` in the terminal); the seed's SDK table points at the EAP's bundled JBR. No request to the VM needed for this. **First runs (afternoon):** three runs opened a `man` window in the user's Terminal. Cause (found by the user's observation that the window is new each time): **Shift+Cmd+A is the default system-wide shortcut of the macOS Service "Search man Page Index in Terminal"**, which fires in any application while text is selected; Search Everywhere reopens with its last query selected ("Clear All Notifications;type=a"), so the driver's Find Action chord went to the Service. Probes with a fresh IDE (nothing selected) kept focus through the same chord, which first pointed me at activation, wrongly. Fixes: Search Everywhere is opened with double Shift on macOS (the all tab finds an action by its exact name); a foreground check before every chord and typed string aborts the run instead of sending keys elsewhere; the driver JVM runs as `-Dapple.awt.UIElement=true`. The same conflict hits IntelliJ users on macOS, which is why the IDE suggests disabling that Service shortcut. **With double Shift the single arm runs through: T0 dark 2x, all ten scenes captured at 3456×2234 (`/tmp/scenes/T0-skia-platform/dark-2x`), valid run, editor and quick-documentation scenes as designed.** Remaining difference to the VM: the frame was not maximized (the Windows helper maximized it), so the runner now sizes the frame to the usable screen area through System Events and the driver crops captures to that area (no menu-bar clock, no desktop around the frame). Verified: captures are 3456×2168 of the frame alone (`/tmp/scenes2/T0-skia-platform/dark-2x`). **The macOS pipeline is ready; the matrix (M0/T0/T1/T2, dark + light, 2x; ≈ 25 min of untouched screen) and `SceneDiff` against T0 as the baseline are the next step, to schedule with the user.** **Matrix run (2026-10-22, 12:03–12:39, user present, screen untouched):** 8 runs × 10 scenes, all valid; reports `build/text-quality-mac/out/2026-10-22/scenes/scenes-{dark,light}-2x.md` with hot-spot crops. **Skia scaler vs platform scaler on the same Skia pipe (T1, T2 vs T0), editor scene, frame of 7.5 M px:** dark — T1 172,869 px differ / 40,268 beyond 32 / max 166 / mean 24.3; T2 (darkened) 165,501 / 19,232 / 166 / 15.3; light — T1 193,117 / 81,639 / 251 / 31.2; T2 165,699 / 32,447 / 251 / 20.3. The darkened variant halves the pixels beyond 32 and lowers the mean by a third in both themes, consistent with the glyph sheets (the editor font is JetBrains Mono, i.e. FreeType → CoreText-host engine change plus the smoothing difference). Other scenes scale with their text amount (markdown 5–9 k beyond 32, tooltip 48–98 k). Visually in the crops: T1 text slightly lighter than T0, T2 close to T0, same layout in all three Skia-pipe arms. **M0 (Metal) is not comparable in this run:** its captures show a different frame layout (text columns and rows shifted against T0), so its 350 k px beyond 32 are layout, not rendering; to repeat after finding the layout difference (tool-window state or frame placement in the Metal arm). Heat maps (`review-*-2x/<scene>-<arm>-heat.png`): the T1/T2 differences sit on every glyph edge uniformly (coverage), nothing structural; the one hot spot is the symbol run `∑∂Δ → ⇒ ≠ ≤ ≥` in the editor (fallback glyphs: the fallback font Skia's path picks through `CTFontCreateForString` may differ from the one the JDK's `JRSFontCreateFallbackFontForCharacters` picks; glyph weights visibly differ in the crops) — a parity item for the fallback resolution. **Metal arm layout, resolved (13:00–13:40):** the editor strip of M0 is shifted by exactly 164 device px (82 pt) against T0 in both themes (column-profile cross-correlation), T1/T2 by 0; a rerun of M0 reproduces it. First suspect was the explicit `-Dsun.java2d.metal=true` (M0 is the only arm that sets it; the installed EAP sets it in `idea.vmoptions`, so M0 is the real IDE), because a new arm **M1 = no Java2D property** laid out like the Skia arms, and so did **T0m = Skia + the Metal property**. But nothing reads the property in a layout-relevant way: in the JDK only `MacOSFlags` (pipeline choice, verbose only for the spelling `True`), in the IDE and plugin jars only `UIUtil.isMetalRendering` (AboutDialog, `SystemRuntimeCollector`), `EnableMetalRenderingAction`, `UpdateInstaller`, `EmbeddedClientLauncher`; Skiko's `AcceleratedSwingPainter` checks the graphics-configuration class name, not the property. **Actual cause: the frame opened at the IDE's default 1400×1000 pt (no stored bounds for the project), the runner then enlarged it to 1728×1084 through System Events, and the Project tool window keeps its pixel width across a frame resize: 0.25 × (1400 − 38) ≈ 340 pt in the arms whose tool window was laid out before the resize, 0.25 × (1728 − 38) ≈ 423 pt in M0, where it happened to come after.** The property only correlated with that race. Fix: the seed now carries `options/recentProjects.xml` with a `<frame x="0" y="33" width="1728" height="1084"/>` entry for the project (where the IDE stores frame bounds per project path), so the frame opens at full size and the placement is a no-op; with it, M1 and M0 show shift 0 against each other and 164 px against the old T0 captures. So the old T0/T1/T2 captures carry the narrow layout and M0 the real one: **the matrix is rerun on the seeded bounds (`out/2026-10-22/scenes-v2`)**; the M1-vs-old-T0 numbers above are layout-confounded and void. **Matrix v2 on the seeded bounds (2026-10-22, 13:15–13:45; `out/2026-10-22/scenes-v2`, reports `scenes-{dark,light}-2x.md`, title-bar corner 0,0,240,150 excluded because the project badge colour is random per run):** editor strip shift 0 for every arm in both themes. Light, complete (10 scenes × 4 arms): **M0 Metal vs T0 Skia pipe, both on the platform scaler: editor 168,189 px differ / 16 beyond 32 / max 33 / mean 10.2; the other scenes 0–46 beyond 32, max 33, except settings 161 / 58 and presentation 4,313 / 208, the latter being the badge again (moved by the taller title bar: cluster (250,40) 70×70)**. So the Skia pipe renders the IDE within 33 levels of Metal everywhere in the light theme, the differences sitting on every glyph edge uniformly (heat map `review-light-2x/editor-M0-metal-heat.png`; the LCD text differences of §16/§18) — against which the Skia scaler arms are T1 80,083 / max 251 / mean 30.1 and T2 (darkened) 30,951 / 251 / 19.8 in the editor: **the engine change is three orders of magnitude more visible than the pipe change**. Dark: M0 vs T0 editor 138,068 / 1,272 / max 63 / mean 9.7, where the 1,272 are the label of the selected editor tab "Hello.java" (`clusters-dark-2x/editor-M0-metal`: glyph-shaped clusters at y 116–141, max 61–63 — text over the rounded selected-tab background, to look at on the pipe side); T1 38,673 / 166 / 21.1, T2 17,713 / 166 / 14.3. **The dark T0 run was aborted after three scenes (editor, selection, tree) when a Google Meet window took the foreground (the driver's focus guard stopped it as designed); the dark report therefore has three scenes, the remaining seven need one more T0 dark run (≈ 4 min, screen untouched).** M1-vs-old-T0 numbers above are void (layout). Fallback symbol run `∑∂Δ`: M0 and T0 identical (same platform fallback), T1/T2 differ (the parity item). **Dark matrix completed (2026-10-22, 13:55):** the seventh T0 dark run went through; dark report now 10 scenes. M0 vs T0 dark: editor 138,272 / 1,272 / 63 / 9.7 (the selected tab's label), markdown 22,384 / 814 / 34, menu 135,097 / 1,272 / 63, search 90,934 / 1,272 / 63, terminal 105,957 / 6,048 / 63, tooltip 161,543 / 1,272 / 63, tree 138,208 / 1,272 / 63; **settings 362,912 / 115,768 / 194 and presentation 65,448 / 10,434 / 195 are layout, not rendering**: the Settings dialog opened with a different width/position in the M0 dark run (its labels appear twice, shifted, in the heat map; the dialog size comes from `DimensionService`, which the seed does not pin), and presentation mode moves the project badge out of the excluded corner. To pin: seed the Settings dialog's `DimensionService` size and extend the excluded region in presentation mode. **Comparison viewer and platform text reference (2026-10-22, 14:00–14:20; user request: switching/blending instead of side by side, integer zoom, LCD judgement on a 1x screen, a platform reference for the dedicated renders):** `build/compare-viewer/` — `index.html` + `viewer.js` (canvas, software-composited in device pixels: switch 1–9, flip while Space is held, blend, flicker 2 Hz, |A−B| × 1/4/8/16, sub-pixel RGB stripes, zoom 1/2/4/8/16 device px per image px around the cursor, RGB readout with Δmax under the cursor, view state in the URL hash for exact repro), `make-viewer.py` (nested scene layout or flat sheets → `<root>/viewer/manifest.js`), `serve.sh` (http origin, needed for pixel access; port derived from the root). Installed over the scene matrix (`scenes-v2/viewer`, 20 sets × 4 arms) and over the text sheets. Verified with headless Chrome screenshots (diff outlines every glyph edge between Metal and the Skia pipe; blend and stripes work). `build/text-reference/` — `ctref.swift` (CoreText/CoreGraphics draws the same 40 rows as `TextReference.java`: Inter 13, JetBrains Mono 13 from the JDK image's fonts, Helvetica Neue 13, Menlo 12 × 1x/2x × gray, gray-fm, lcd, lcd-fm, aliased; opaque RGB bitmap so font smoothing is allowed; sub-pixel positioning on for the `-fm` rows, quantization otherwise) and `TextReference.java` (software loops or, with `--volatile`, the default screen configuration's pipeline into a VolatileImage, `-Dsun.java2d.uiScale=1`); `run.sh` renders coretext, software, metal-platform, skia-platform, skia-scaler-coverage/darkened/smoothed and builds the viewer (`out/2026-10-22/viewer`). **Findings from the first sheets:** (1) CoreGraphics font smoothing on an opaque bitmap yields no colour sub-pixels on this macOS — the platform's "LCD" is dilated grayscale, so the JDK's LCD glyphs (colour fringes at 1x and 2x on Metal) have no native counterpart (§18's JDK-made-LCD quirk, now with the direct reference next to it); (2) the fractional-metrics rows lay out within ≈ 1 px of CoreText over 40 characters, the whole-pixel rows run wider (Java's advance rounding); (3) CoreText's antialias-off rendering is heavy and smeared compared with Java's aliased glyphs (not an IDE case); (4) `-Dsun.java2d.font.scaler=skia` without the Skia pipe silently falls back to the platform scaler (`requireAvailable`) — the arm was byte-identical to Metal and was dropped; a warning belongs there (§13). Handover request for the Windows counterpart (DirectWrite and GDI reference sheets, Java arms, viewers): `build/notes/handover-vm-2026-10-22.md`. **Finding from the sheet reproducibility check (2026-10-22, 14:30):** running `run.sh` twice, the CoreText sheet and the smoothed sheet reproduce byte for byte, but the other sheets differ in 1–4 isolated pixels, always in `lcd-fm` rows (LCD + fractional metrics; rows 3, 8, 13, 18 = Inter 1x/2x, JetBrains Mono 2x, Helvetica Neue 2x), always a single coloured pixel with white neighbours on both sides, with a different value each run (e.g. (276,746) `ffe9d0`, `fff773`, …, max Δ 84–158), on the software loops, the Metal pipe and the Skia pipe alike, with the platform scaler **and** the Skia scaler (coverage: (219,192) `ffffad`). So it is neither the pipe nor the rasterizer: the common part is the JDK's LCD sub-pixel-variant glyph image path (JBR's sub-pixel glyph resolution for LCD with fractional metrics) — reading past a glyph row or an uninitialised byte in the image padding is the obvious suspect. Pre-existing in the platform path, so not a Skia regression, but it must be understood before the sheets are used for a pixel-exact LCD judgement; reproducer: the software arm of `build/text-reference/run.sh` twice, `java /tmp/ImgDiff.java a.png b.png`. Needs the red test first (§13). **Viewer, second round (2026-10-22, 15:00; user: plain form controls for discovery, a documentation panel, platform-native references in the mix, per-dimension selection of arms, smooth trackpad zoom without interpolation):** `index.html` now has fieldsets with the shortcut next to each control (set, arms, view as radio buttons, blend slider + number field, difference gain, zoom presets 1/2/3/4/6/8/12/16 + fit + snap, panels); arms carry **dimensions** (renderer, font scaler, grayscale route, theme) from `descriptions.json`, so A and B are chosen through **columns of radio buttons, one per dimension, each column appearing only once the columns before it still leave a choice** (native shows nothing further, the platform scaler no grayscale column; arms that share every dimension get a final by-name column; user's correction of a first dropdown version that let one select change the others) or by the name select; the **documentation panel** (`I`) explains the set (text-sheet rows, hints, fonts, geometry), each selected arm's dimension values and arm description, and lists the dimensions present in the set; the native reference arms (coretext; directwrite/gdi prepared for Windows) are described as "renderer: native". Zoom is continuous on the wheel/pinch (factor per event, around the cursor) and stays nearest-neighbour at every zoom — fractional zooms show uneven pixel sizes, flagged in the overlay, `N` snaps to a whole number; keys step through the presets. The text-sheet viewer shows the row label under the cursor (`rows.txt`, 56 px rows). Verified with headless Chrome. `make-viewer.py` reports arms without dimensions so new arm names get added to `descriptions.json`. **Inventory answers (2026-10-22, 16:00; user questions on the sheets and the viewer):** (1) **LCD rows of the Skia scaler:** `lcd` without fractional metrics comes out as grayscale (0 % of ink pixels with channel spread > 48 in rows 2, 7, 12, versus 66 % / 54 % / 70 % for the platform scaler), `lcd-fm` with fully saturated fringes (49 %, max spread 224 against the platform's 164 at a similar mean of 72 vs 75) — Skia's CoreText host delivers sub-pixel coverage only together with sub-pixel positioning (`SkFont::setSubpixel`, which our context ties to fractional metrics), and our LCD path exports that coverage raw, while the JDK runs CoreGraphics' LCD output through a reverse-gamma LUT (`J2D_LCD_REVERSE_GAMMA`, default 1.8, `CGGlyphImages.m`) and no FIR filter either; FreeType (Windows/Linux host) applies its own LCD filter, so the Windows sheets decide that platform. **Moot on macOS for the IDE:** `AntialiasingType.canUseSubpixelAAForIDE/Editor` return false on macOS (Big Sur+) unless `-Denable.macos.ide.subpixelAA` / `-Denable.macos.editor.subpixelAA` are set, and the scene captures confirm grayscale text (456 px with spread > 48 in the dark project tree, identical in M0/T0/T1: icons). (2) **Fractional metrics in IntelliJ:** registry `ide.text.fractional.metrics` and `editor.text.fractional.metrics` are `Auto*|Disabled|Enabled`; `UISettings.Companion.getPreferredFractionalMetricsValue` resolves Auto to **on** when `SystemInfo.isMacOSCatalina` (always, today) or when JBR's sub-pixel glyph resolution is enabled and the scope's AA is grayscale; the editor rule is the latter alone — so **on macOS the IDE already runs UI and editor text with fractional metrics and sub-pixel glyph positions** (the `-fm` rows are the IDE-relevant rows there; the layout is already CoreText-like), while on Windows/Linux with LCD default it is off and switching it on would be the layout-shift path. (3) **Lighter coverage:** confirmed in `SKFontScaler.cpp`: `SkSurfaceProps(…, contrast 0, gamma 1)` on purpose; Skia's own text path bakes a luminance-dependent preblend (contrast 0.5, sRGB gamma) into the mask at rasterization, not at compositing, so Skia-drawn black text on white is darker than our linear coverage; Java2D caches colour-independent glyph images, so only a fixed preblend can be offered — proposed as a fourth grayscale route `gamma` (Skia defaults, black luminance) next to coverage/darkened/smoothed, and an LCD route option (`raw` / reverse gamma like the JDK / FIR like FreeType). (4) **Viewer, third round:** two-finger scroll pans, pinch (ctrl-wheel) or Shift-wheel zooms, still nearest-neighbour; the contextual controls live with their view option (blend slider and number, flicker interval 1/0.5/0.25 s, difference gain) and are disabled unless that option is selected. (5) **Light on dark:** both sheet tools take `--dark` (IDE dark editor colours: paper #1E1F22, ink #BCBEC4); `run.sh` now writes `<out>/<arm>/on-white.png` and `on-dark.png` with `rows.txt` at the root (nested layout, two sets in the viewer: `out/2026-10-22-v2`, served on port 8705); the IDE dark scenes are real light-on-dark samples already. **Sheets restructured, Skia source read (2026-10-22, 17:00; user: AA and positioning as dimensions, italics, underline, dark paper on native too, no pointer cursor):** rows are now family × {regular, italic, bold, bold italic, regular underlined} (Inter 13 with the bundled SemiBold as bold, JetBrains Mono 13, Helvetica Neue 13, Menlo 12), and each rendering configuration is one sheet under `<arm>/<aa>/<positioning>/<scale>/<paper>.png` (3 × 2 × 2 × 2 = 24 per arm, 168 images in `out/2026-10-22-v3`, port 8726); `make-viewer.py --set-dims aa,positioning,scale,paper` turns the path into set dimensions and the viewer offers them as radio columns like the arms (scenes: `--set-dims theme-scale,scene`). Both tools render all sheets in one process; CoreText draws the dark sheets on the same (30, 31, 34) paper. Underline: Java's `TextAttribute.UNDERLINE` (a rectangle from `CoreMetrics.underlineOffset/Thickness`, derived from the strike metrics, drawn by `Decoration` as a fill → renderer-dependent at most) against CoreText's underline attribute (font `post` table values) — a cheap parity comparison, so it stayed in as one row per family; strikethrough has no CoreText equivalent at `CTLineDraw` level and was left out. **Skia's CoreText host, from the source (`SkScalerContext_mac_ct.cpp`, `SkTypeface_mac_ct.cpp`, `SkScalerContext.cpp`):** (a) LCD is kept only when `SkCTFontGetSmoothBehavior() == subpixel`, otherwise the rec is downgraded to A8 (with hinting Normal, i.e. A8 generated from CG's smoothed masks); (b) CG's sub-pixel output is linearised with `gLinearCoverageFromCGLCDValue` = x²/255 ("CG obscurely defaults to 2.0 as the subpixel coverage gamma") before inversion and the preblend — the JDK instead inverts first and applies `J2D_LCD_REVERSE_GAMMA` (x^(1/1.8)) to the coverage; both boost mid coverage, Skia's curve more at both ends (our lcd-fm fringes: max spread 224–227 vs the JDK's 162–166, mean alike); (c) for A8 with hinting None Skia itself calls `rec->ignorePreBlend()` and draws **without** CG smoothing (`requestSmooth = hinting != kNone`), so our grayscale = exactly Skia's own unhinted macOS text; with hinting set, Skia generates A8 from CG's smoothed masks with the x² linearisation and luminance — a Skia-native "smoothed" route (`font.setHinting(kNormal)`) worth offering instead of our channel-averaging emulation; (d) the sub-pixel positioning flag never enters the mask-format decision in Skia core or the Mac host — yet **our LCD without fractional metrics is grayscale from the scaler itself** (software loops + Skia scaler: lcd/whole-pixel 0 % fringe, lcd/fractional 38 %, max 227; the platform scaler 27 % / 164 in both), so the cause is still open (follow-up). **Fractional metrics (user question):** on macOS the IDE already runs UI and editor text with FM on (Auto rule above), so the `fractional` sheets are the IDE-relevant ones there; making FM an IDE-wide switch elsewhere is the layout-shift path, not a JBR dimension. **CoreText reference corrected (2026-10-22, 17:45; user noticed lighter dark paper and non-integer "whole-pixel" advances):** (1) `CGColor(red:green:blue:alpha:)` makes a Generic-RGB colour that CoreGraphics converts into the DeviceRGB bitmap: the dark paper came out 28292d instead of 1e1f22 and the ink 282935 / c8cacf instead of 1e1e28 / bcbec4 — every colour is now created from DeviceRGB components (`CGColor(colorSpace:components:)`), and the sheets match Java's values exactly. (2) CoreGraphics' "sub-pixel quantization" only snaps where a glyph is drawn; CoreText's advances stay fractional, so the native "whole-pixel" line of JetBrains Mono 13 was 411 px against Java's 422 (7.8 px advances rounded to 8 by Java). **The platform has no integer-advance mode.** The native whole-pixel sheets now place the glyphs at Java's positions (every advance rounded, in device pixels for the file fonts like the FreeType strike and in points for the system fonts like `CStrike`, summed; `CTFontDrawGlyphs` per run so fallback fonts keep theirs; the underline as a rectangle from `CTFontGetUnderlinePosition/Thickness`) and the fractional sheets stay CoreText's own layout. Result: JetBrains Mono 1x whole-pixel 424 px vs Java 422, fractional 412 vs 412; Helvetica Neue 2x whole-pixel 621 vs 627 (the system-font rule leaves ≈ 1 % — `CStrike`'s advances differ slightly from `CTRunGetAdvances`; residual noted), fractional 624 vs 625. (3) Cells are now W·scale × H·scale (460 × 28 px at 1x), so 1x rows are no longer spread out; the viewer derives the row height from the image height. Sheets `out/2026-10-22-v4`, port 8787. **Then a placement bug in the whole-pixel native rows (v4):** `CTFontDrawGlyphs` takes positions in text space — relative to `textPosition` and through the (flipped) text matrix — so passing y = BASE put every baseline at the cell top and the rows bled into each other (ink y 0..27 in every row, the underline through the next family's ascenders). Fixed with the baseline in `textPosition` and y = 0 in the points (`out/2026-10-22-v5`): rows ink y 7/8..21/22 like Java's 7/9..21, underline at y 21–22 vs Java's 21, right edges within 1–3 px (JetBrains Mono 426/427 both). **AppleFontSmoothing and what "native" means on macOS (2026-10-22, 18:30; user question):** ink per regular row (Σ|pixel − paper|, Inter 13, fractional): native with smoothing **off** (`gray` sheet) 175,928 on white 1x / 125,334 on dark 1x — **equal to the Skia coverage route within 0.5 %** (175,174 / 126,196): plain coverage is exactly what CoreText delivers without smoothing, i.e. what Skia and Chrome draw. Native with smoothing **on** (`lcd` sheet; AppKit's default, governed by the `AppleFontSmoothing` default, unset on this machine = medium) 207,802 on white 1x, 827,899 at 2x, 168,037 on dark 1x, 672,495 at 2x. Our `smoothed` route (CG smoothing through Skia's sub-pixel edging, channels averaged): 263,003 (+27 %), 939,622 (+13 %), 189,455 (+13 %), 676,848 (+0.6 %) — it picks up the system's smoothing (Skia asks CoreGraphics for it, so `AppleFontSmoothing` applies to the IDE process like to AppKit) but overshoots on light paper and at 1x, because CG smooths against the real text and background colours and the Java mask is rendered black-on-white, colour-independent, then composited by the pipe. JBR's platform scaler today: FreeType for the file fonts, 154,587 (−26 % against AppKit's look) and `CStrike` for the system fonts, Helvetica 183,205 vs native smoothed 204,101 (−10 %), on dark +3 %. **Consequence:** to match AppKit, glyph masks would have to depend on the text/background colour class (at least dark-on-light vs light-on-dark, as Skia's own preblend does per luminance) — a strike-cache design change (image variants per luminance bucket in `SKFontStrike`); smoothing alone does not suffice. Skia's hinted-A8 route is the natural implementation of "smoothing on" and should replace the channel-averaging emulation; the native `lcd` sheet is the macOS reference for grayscale UI text, the native `gray` sheet the reference for the coverage route. **Who owns `AppleFontSmoothing`, probed (2026-10-22, 19:00; user: match AppKit by construction, reuse as much as possible):** CoreGraphics' glyph rasterizer implements font smoothing and reads the preference; CoreText delegates glyph drawing to it; AppKit only sets `shouldSmoothFonts` per context (off for non-opaque layers) and, through the private JavaRuntimeSupport/CG style API, the smoothing style — which is what JBR's `CGGlyphImages.m` also does (`JRSFontSetRenderingStyleOnContext`), and where its colour-fringed LCD glyphs come from. Skia's CoreText host uses only public API (`CGContextSetShouldSmoothFonts`), so it inherits the system's smoothing setting by construction when smoothing is requested. **Probe `/tmp/fmtprobe2.swift` (Helvetica Neue 13, every bitmap format Skia or a window backing store uses, both polarities):** CoreGraphics emits **no colour sub-pixels at all** on this macOS (max channel spread 0 in every case), so Skia's `SkCTFontGetSmoothBehavior` cannot be `subpixel` here and the colour in our `lcd`/`fractional` output is made by Skia's raster path, not by CoreGraphics (narrows the open LCD items); smoothing adds **+15 % ink for black on white but +40 % for white on black** (67,359 → 77,528 vs 94,491) — CG's smoothing is polarity-dependent, which is exactly the part no colour-independent glyph mask can reproduce (our black-on-white mask gives light-on-dark text the +15 % dilation, AppKit gives it +40 %; the earlier ink ratios dark/white 0.81 native vs 0.72 for every mask route say the same). **So "by construction" decomposes as:** rasterizer and preference — yes, already (Skia → CG → `AppleFontSmoothing`), provided Skia's x² linearisation is bypassed or CG is called directly as `CStrike` does; polarity — only by rasterizing per polarity class (at least dark-on-light vs light-on-dark glyph images; the pipes pick by the text/background luminance; Skia's own preblend does the same per luminance bucket); compositing gamma — a pipe matter. Proposed next: an `appkit` arm rendered by AppKit itself (an `NSTextField`/`NSAttributedString` drawn into an on-screen window and captured) as the final reference, and a polarity-aware variant of the Skia strike as the experiment. **Skia scaler on every pipe (2026-10-22, 19:30; user request):** red test `SkiaScalerWithoutBackendTest` (`-Dsun.java2d.font.scaler=skia` alone must make `SKFontScaler.isAvailable()` true, construct a scaler over `lib/fonts/Inter-Regular.otf` and put ink on a BufferedImage) failed with "not available without the Skia backend"; fix: `SKEnvironment.loadLibrary()` loads `libawt_skia` independently of the backend and `SKFontScaler.isAvailable()` uses it (commit on the branch, TextRender suite green). New sheet arms `software-skia-scaler` and `metal-skia-scaler` (`out/2026-10-22-v6`, 9 arms × 24 sheets, port derived). **Finding from them: the Metal pipe draws grayscale text ≈ 12 % lighter than the software loops and the Skia pipe with the identical glyph images** (Inter 13 fractional 1x on white: software 174,796 / Skia pipe 175,174 / Metal 154,587 with the platform scaler; with the Skia scaler software 175,178 / Skia 175,174 / Metal 154,979; Helvetica via `CStrike` software 203,358 / Metal 183,205). That is the pipe-level text difference seen in the IDE scenes (M0 vs T0, mean ≈ 10 levels on every glyph edge): Metal's AA text blending, not the glyphs — a Metal candidate (§16): check `MTLTextRenderer`'s grayscale shader for the coverage-to-alpha mapping (sRGB sampling of the glyph texture, or a non-linear blend). **CoreGraphics smoothing follows the text colour only (2026-10-22, 19:50; probe `/tmp/polprobe.swift`, Helvetica Neue 13, Σ|pixel − paper|, smoothed vs unsmoothed):** black text +15 % on white and +15 % on mid-gray; white text +40 % on black and +41 % on mid-gray; mid-gray text +21 % on white and +22 % on black; 0.2 gray on 0.8 gray +15 %, 0.8 on 0.2 +28 %. So the extra dilation is a function of the **foreground luminance alone**, independent of the destination: CoreGraphics applies a luminance-dependent transfer to the glyph coverage before a normal composite — exactly a mask-level preblend keyed by text luminance, which is what Skia models with `fLumBits` in the strike key and its preblend LUT (but Skia renders the glyph once, black on white, and applies its *own* gamma model: `onFilterRec` scales the luminance colour by ½ on this smooth behaviour and leaves the sRGB preblend to approximate CG — a model, not CG). **Consequence for Java2D:** because the dependence is on the text colour only, CG's exact result is cacheable: render the glyph with CoreGraphics, smoothing on, fill = the text's luminance L on a far paper (white for dark text, black for light text), recover the coverage as (paper − pixel)/(paper − L), and cache it per luminance bucket in `SKFontStrike`; the pipe passes the colour's luminance (a strike-side key, as Skia does; a few buckets suffice). That is by construction for the rasterizer, the preference and the luminance transfer; only the pipes' blending (plain sRGB alpha vs CG's) remains, and the probe says CG's composite itself is linear in the destination. Going "full Skia" including its compositing would give Skia's model of CG (Chrome's look), not CG — the hybrid is closer to native than full Skia. Proposed experiment: a `cg` grayscale route in the Skia scaler with the luminance taken from a property for the sheets (30 on white, 188 on dark), compared against the native smoothed sheets; then the pipe-side luminance key. **Metal's lighter text explained (2026-10-22, 20:20; user hypothesis: the Metal shader was tuned to macOS's look):** confirmed and sharper — with identical glyph images the Metal pipe is −12 % against the software loops and Skia on white but **+18 % on dark** (Inter 1x: 154,587 vs 174,796 on white, 148,710 vs 125,909 on dark; 2x the same sign). The grayscale text shader (`frag_gmc_text`, `MTLPaints.m`, JBR-3820 "Gamma correction for grayscale text in Metal rendering pipeline", `830b5c09e4c5`) raises the coverage to an exponent interpolated by the text's brightness: 1.4 for dark text (thinner), 0.454 for light text (bolder) — a shader-side imitation of CoreGraphics' luminance-dependent smoothing, needed because the platform scaler's images for file fonts are plain FreeType coverage. Against AppKit's smoothed look (207,802 / 168,037): software −16 % / −25 %, Metal −26 % / −12 % — Metal trades the light theme for the dark one; for `CStrike` fonts (already CG-smoothed images) the software loops match native on white within 0.4 % and Metal on dark within 3 %. So **the JBR already contains a luminance-dependent text model, in the Metal shader; the Skia route moves it to the glyph image where CoreGraphics does it exactly.** **The `cg` route works by construction (2026-10-22, 21:00):** red test `CoreGraphicsGrayRouteTest` (white text ≥ 1.25× the plain coverage ink, black text 1.03–1.25×) → implementation: `SKPlatform_SmoothedGlyphMask` (macOS: `CTFontDrawGlyphs` into an RGBX `CGBitmapContext` with smoothing on, unit-size CTFont with the glyph-to-device matrix (size, synthetic italic, remainder; y flipped) as the text matrix, the origin in the text position, fill = text luminance on white (dark text) or black (light text), coverage = |paper − pixel| / |paper − luminance|; Windows false), `sun.java2d.skia.text.gray=cg` + `sun.java2d.skia.text.cgLuminance` (per process for now), synthetic bold keeps Skia's coverage. Two build mistakes on the way (`SkFont::getTypefaceOrDefault` does not exist in m156; glyph positions go through the text matrix, so the origin must be the text position — the same lesson as in `ctref`). Test green (ratios 1.43 white, 1.16 black — CoreGraphics' own +40 % / +15 %), TextRender suite green, commit on the branch. **Sheets (`out/2026-10-22-v7`, 11 arms): with the matching luminance the route equals the native smoothed sheet within 0.3 % ink everywhere** — Inter 1x on white 207,252 vs 207,802, 2x 826,529 vs 827,899; on dark 1x 167,636 vs 168,037, 2x 671,526 vs 672,495 (the mismatched-luminance arm shows the size of the luminance effect: dark-ink masks on dark paper 149,267, −11 %); the `smoothed` route stays +13–27 %. Pixel statistics in the next entry. **Architecture (user question):** this route lives in the glyph image, so it needs Java2D (or anyone) to own the glyph cache and the blend with a luminance-keyed strike — in a full-Skia text path it would need a custom `SkScalerContext` (Skia's `src/` API) rendering per `fLumBits`, which Skia's strike key already supports; Skia's own CoreText host would not do it (its model, not CG). Blending: Skia's and Java2D's pipes both composite the preblended mask linearly, so either is fine; Metal's JBR-3820 exponent would have to be switched off for these images (it would double-apply the luminance model). **Pixel identity of the `cg` route (2026-10-22, 21:20):** on the whole-pixel sheets, where both sides place glyphs at the same rounded positions, the route equals the native smoothed sheet **pixel for pixel: max 1–2 levels, no pixel beyond 8, for JetBrains Mono, Helvetica Neue and Menlo, on white and on dark** (4 rows each, 1x; mean of the differing pixels 1.0). Inter differed after any whole-pixel alignment (best dx = 2, mean 63–81): **kerning** — CoreText applies the font's kern pairs by default and Java's `drawString` does not; Inter is the proportional file font with pairs in the sample string (`ctref` now sets `kCTKernAttributeName` 0; line lengths then agree, 341 = 341 px, and Inter's regular rows match pixel for pixel too; the residual (≈ 240 px beyond 32 per row, every style alike) sits in two column bands only: the brackets `{}[]();:,.` (x 189–216: a few advances that the two sides round to different whole pixels) and the symbol run `∑∂Δ → ≠ ≤ ≥` (x 269–317: fallback glyphs, the fallback-font parity item) — layout, not rasterization; everywhere else Inter matches like the other families). The fractional sheets cannot be compared pixel-wise: Java quantizes sub-pixel positions to JBR's 4×1 resolution, CoreText positions exactly — a layout matter, the ink totals (within 0.3 %) are the metric there.
