(4) *Per-luminance masks in Skia's strike* — custom `SkScalerContext` over CoreGraphics, keyed by Skia's `fLumBits`. Each stage against the same sheets and scenes. **Stage 1 of the full-Skia text route, first result (2026-10-23, 15:00):** `sun.java2d.skia.text=skia` draws glyph lists through `SkCanvas::drawGlyphs` (m156 takes `SkSpan`s; one build failed on the old signature, one on an accessor that slipped under an `@Override`). `SkiaTextPathTest` green for the system font: 40 glyphs through Skia's path, ink bounds identical to the glyph-image path, ink ratio 0.79 for black Helvetica Neue on white — **Skia's gamma model draws dark text about 21 % lighter than plain coverage**. Sheets (`out/2026-10-23-v11`): Helvetica Neue 13 on white 1x 136,001 (native 204,101, coverage 173,400, cg 203,475; −33 % against native), on dark 146,491 (native 163,820, coverage 124,919; +17 % over coverage, −11 % against native) — Skia's model moves in the platform's direction on dark paper but far from it on light paper, as Chrome's text does. **The file fonts (Inter, JetBrains Mono) did not take the path** in that run: their strikes are `FileFontStrike`s over the Skia scaler, not `SKFontStrike`s — found by the sheets (ink identical to coverage), test extended (file font must count its glyphs too, red), and the renderer now accepts any `SkiaDrawable` strike (`SKFontStrike`, `FileFontStrike` on the Skia scaler); rerun in progress (`v12`). **Stage 1 complete (2026-10-23, 16:00):** red (file font: 0 glyphs through the path) → `SkiaDrawable` (`SKFontStrike`, `FileFontStrike` on the Skia scaler) → green for both strike kinds (40 glyphs each, identical ink bounds, ink ratio 0.79 / 0.79 against the glyph-image path for black text on white); TextRender suite green; commit pushed. **Sheets `out/2026-10-23-v12` (Inter 13, fractional, Σ ink): Skia's own text path 136,057 on white 1x (native 207,754, coverage 175,174, cg 207,207 → −35 % against native, −22 % against plain coverage), 148,636 on dark 1x (native 168,073, coverage 126,196 → −12 % against native, +18 % over coverage); 2x the same shape (617,467 / 547,612).** So Skia's gamma model (contrast 0.5, sRGB device gamma, the CoreText host's halved luminance) moves light-on-dark text towards the platform but takes dark-on-light text further away than plain coverage — Chrome's look on macOS, measured: the "full Skia" rasterization and compositing does not approach native text, while the CoreGraphics-per-luminance route does (within 0.3 %). What the full route can still bring is layout (stages 2–3) and one engine for both; fidelity would need stage 4 (a custom scaler context feeding CoreGraphics masks into Skia's strike). Catalog regenerated with the arm and two presets. **Chrome measured, the "Chrome look" claim corrected (2026-10-23, 16:40; user asked to verify):** headless Chrome 156 drawing the same 20 rows on a canvas (`build/compare-viewer/chrome-ref/index.html`: same fonts from the JDK image via `FontFace`, same sizes, baseline, colours, `fontKerning = 'none'`; `render.sh` writes the `chrome` arm) gives Inter 13 on white 1x **200,917** (native 207,754, −3 %), on dark **169,970** (native 168,073, +1 %), 2x 813,876 / 636,644 (−2 % / −5 %) — **Chrome is near native; our Skia text path (136,057 / 148,636) is not Chrome's look.** The difference must be the one setting Skia's CoreText host keys its smoothing on: `requestSmooth = hinting != kNone`; our scaler context sets `SkFontHinting::kNone` ("like CStrike"), Chrome leaves hinting on, so Chrome gets CoreGraphics' smoothed masks plus Skia's luminance model, which lands within a few percent of AppKit. New knob under test: `sun.java2d.skia.text.smoothing=true` (the Skia text path draws with hinting normal), arm `skia-text-smoothed`; red test line first. **Chrome parity confirmed (2026-10-23, 17:30):** red line (smoothing requested → ratio 0.79 < 1.0) → `sun.java2d.skia.text.smoothing=true` sets `SkFontHinting::kNormal` on the runs the Skia text path draws → green, suite green, commit pushed. **Sheets `out/2026-10-23-v13` (Inter 13, fractional): Skia text path with smoothing 200,217 / 170,955 / 812,587 / 638,580 (white 1x, dark 1x, white 2x, dark 2x) against Chrome 156's 200,917 / 169,970 / 813,876 / 636,644 — within 0.6 % everywhere.** So the Chrome look is exactly "Skia's CoreText host with hinting on (CoreGraphics' smoothed masks) plus Skia's luminance model", and the full-Skia rasterization and compositing can reach it with one flag. Against native: Chrome and that path −3 % / +1 % / −2 % / −5 %; the CoreGraphics-per-luminance route −0.3 % everywhere. Stage 1 conclusion: **a full-Skia text path gets Chrome's fidelity (within 2–5 % of AppKit), not AppKit's; the per-luminance CoreGraphics masks remain the only exact route, and inside Skia's architecture they would need stage 4.** The `chrome` arm is rendered by `compare-viewer/chrome-ref/render.sh` from `run.sh` (headless Chrome, canvas, fonts via file URLs), two presets added. Consequence as on the VM: a scene run needs the screen untouched (≈ 3 min per arm × theme × scale, incl. ~1 min indexing); the matrix (M0/T0/T1/T2 × dark/light × 2x) is ≈ 25 min of screen time, to schedule.
- **T4 — shaping and layout.** HarfBuzz stays (JDK callbacks feed advances from the Skia scaler by construction); SkShaper/SkParagraph only as an opt-in JBR API later.

Under path a, Swing text would move to Skia **implicitly** through a `sun.font` re-implementation (scaler + shaper + metrics on SkFont/SkShaper); no per-component opt-in. `JLabel`, `JTextArea`, HTML panes, `SimpleColoredComponent`, and the editor (via `FontLayoutService`) consume `FontMetrics`/`GlyphVector` and would move automatically. Layout and rasterization would then come from one engine, so they stay consistent with each other; the cost is a one-time product-wide re-flow of text widths, comparable in kind to a font-version update. Its magnitude is what WP 3.2 measures.

SkParagraph would back `TextLayout`/`LineBreakMeasurer` and could additionally be exposed as a JBR rich-text API for `JBHtmlPane`, tooltips, documentation popups, inlays, notifications, and chat UIs. Under paths b or c, that API alone could still be offered on Skia as an opt-in primitive.

Engine policy — FreeType everywhere (consistent across OSes, deterministic) vs. platform-native scalers per OS — is a decision to take before WP 3.2 if path a stays in scope; FreeType everywhere is the simpler default for the experiment.

Rough effort for the `sun.font` re-implementation itself: 8–12k LOC, 1–1.5 engineer-years, plus stabilization.

---

## 6. Decision rule

- **The current plan (c) proceeds regardless and is the baseline**, not a competing proposal: it is staffed, aligned with upstreaming, and already addresses AA geometry on the GPU. The experiment's job is to establish what Skia adds *on top of* it.
- **Recommend b if** WP 2.2 shows Vulkan cannot serve a material share of Windows seats (threshold to agree once telemetry exists), **and** WP 1.3 shows an acceptable dependency/build cost, **and** WP 2.1 shows Skia at least on par with Metal with no output differences in the Marlin-preserving variant. New capabilities are a tiebreaker, weighted by whether the platform would adopt them. Because b means the GPU backends stop being upstreamable, choosing it also requires leadership to accept that departure explicitly.
- **Keep a in scope only if** WP 1.4 / 2.1b show a performance gain large enough to justify a product-wide rendering change, **and** WP 3.1 / 3.2 show that the resulting differences are either imperceptible or acceptable after review. Only at that point should design and IDE UI leadership be asked to weigh in; a stabilization release would need their commitment, and JBR leadership would need to accept a permanent divergence from OpenJDK in rendering and text.
- **If neither condition is met**, the recommendation is not to adopt Skia for Java2D at this time, with the measured capability gaps and the conditions under which to revisit documented for later.

---

## 7. Key risks and unknowns

| Risk / unknown | Affects | How the experiment addresses it |
|---|---|---|
| Rendering differences may or may not be visible; magnitude unknown | a (b only if Skia rasterizes shapes) | WP 3.1 catalogue by cause; design involved only if perceptible differences appear |
| Text re-flow magnitude and appearance change | a | WP 3.2 drift and re-flow measurement; FreeType everywhere for determinism |
| Graphite + Dawn on Windows maturity; no WARP path; Ganesh D3D removal | a, b | WP 2.2 on the actual hardware classes; Graphite-only from the start; Skia raster as CPU fallback |
| Skia API churn between milestones; Google priorities | a, b | WP 1.3 upgrade-cost sample |
| Transition period with old and new backends coexisting | a, b | Staged deletion plan per backend |
| Vulkan coverage gaps on Windows | c | WP 2.2 telemetry; GPU-blocklist fallback; software remains where Vulkan is absent |
| Power regression from more GPU work | all | Power measured in WP 1.1 / 2.1 |
| Lux determinism; third-party screenshot-based tests | a | WP 3.1 corpora; re-baseline notice if needed |
| Skia backend cannot be upstreamed; permanent divergence from OpenJDK in the rendering layer | a, b | Not mitigable by the experiment; stated as a strategic break for leadership to accept or reject |
| Upstream review cost and timeline for Vulkan, Wayland, and GPU Marlin | c | Outside the experiment |
| GPU-Marlin prototype not available in time to serve as baseline | experiment | Estimate from its design; record the assumption; re-run the comparison when it lands |
| Single-engineer schedule; hardware and analytics dependencies | all | Phased plan with early-exit checkpoints |

---

## 8. Deliverables

1. Profile of where frame time goes today (WP 1.1), shared with the AA-geometry acceleration owners.
2. Performance table: Marlin (CPU) vs. GPU Marlin (where available) vs. Skia raster vs. Skia GPU, per scene and platform, including power.
3. Windows coverage matrix and, if available, seat telemetry.
4. Dependency and build cost report.
5. Catalogue of rendering-difference kinds with representative images.
6. Text metrics drift / re-flow / appearance report.
7. Recommendation memo: one path, with estimates and the conditions under which it holds.

---

## 9. Inputs needed from others (when relevant, not up front)

- **Analytics:** GPU vendor/driver and Vulkan availability across Windows seats (Phase 2).
- **JBR team:** coordination with the AA-geometry HW acceleration owners on scenes, metrics, and prototype availability; confirmation of the Vulkan-on-Windows timeline and the upstreaming plan.
- **JBR leadership:** whether a deliberate break from the upstreaming strategy for the rendering layer is acceptable at all (only if a or b remain in scope after Phase 2).
- **IDE platform:** interest in GPU effects, path rendering, or a paragraph API (only if b or a remain in scope after Phase 2).
- **Design / UI leadership:** review of WP 3.1 / 3.2 differences (only if perceptible differences are found).

---

## 10. Decision log

- **2026-10-22 — Strokes: Skia's analytic stroker stays the default** (user, after the side-by-side sheet and the timing): the visible gain of Java's stroker outline (rotated 1 px round rects at 2x from 88 to 46 levels, otherwise a wash) does not justify 8.5× the cost on stroke-heavy frames (2.4 → 20.4 ms per 1,000 stroked ovals). `sun.java2d.skia.strokes=java` stays as the comparison knob.
- **2026-10-22 — Font engine for the text track: the prebuilt's platform hosts** (CoreText on macOS, DirectWrite on Windows, FreeType+fontconfig on Linux; user). No custom Skia build; FreeType-everywhere only if the metrics measurements demand it.

| Date | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-12 | Frame the work as *testing the benefits of a Skia backend* and *options to replace more of the Java graphics system with Skia*, measured against the team's current plan — not as "status quo vs. Skia". | The current plan is staffed and proceeding; the experiment adds information on top of it. Avoids a territorial framing with the backend owners. |
| 2026-10-12 | Path c = the current plan (incl. the planned GPU-Marlin work), no new D3D backend. | The team has chosen Vulkan for Windows; a D3D12 backend would compete with their roadmap and needs coverage data first. |
| 2026-10-12 | Path a includes moving *all* Swing text to Skia via a `sun.font` re-implementation (scaler + shaper + metrics), not a glyph-rasterizer-only swap; SkParagraph backs `TextLayout`/`LineBreakMeasurer` and a new rich-text API. | Layout and rasterization from one engine are consistent by construction; the risk becomes a measurable one-time re-flow rather than a mismatch. No metrics-compat mode required. |
| 2026-10-13 | Defer WP 1.2 (alignment with JBR-10520/10521) until that work lands; start with 1.3 → 2.1. | Comparing against code in flux is not meaningful and coordination adds overhead for everyone; the shipped Metal pipeline is a stable baseline. |
| 2026-10-13 | Backend prototype is **native C++ against `SkCanvas`** in a new on-demand `libawt_skia`, interpreting the existing render-queue opcodes on the flusher thread and wrapping existing presentation textures. Skiko only as reference code and for Java-level capability demos. | Measures the architecture that would ship; raster-over-pinned-arrays and texture wrapping are native-only; Skiko's AWT module depends on `java.desktop` (cycle); per-primitive JNI would confound the comparison. |
| 2026-10-13 | Use the JetBrains/skia prebuilt rather than building Skia. **Graphite only**, no Ganesh. | Prebuilt links and runs Graphite-on-Metal unmodified (see §11.1); Ganesh is being deprecated. |
| 2026-10-13 | **Pin `m156-24300fbbcb`** (not m155). | m155 was only "what the first probes used"; m156 is the newest release, passes the same probes, and nothing requires matching the Skiko milestone IJ bundles (see 11.2 on in-process coexistence). |
| 2026-10-13 | Acquisition: pin file `jb/project/tools/common/skia_prebuilt.conf` (tag + SHA-256 per OS/arch, from GitHub asset digests, verified locally) + `jb/project/tools/common/scripts/fetch_skia.sh` + `configure --with-skia=<dir>` (`make/autoconf/lib-skia.m4`). Configure never downloads. | Mirrors the Vulkan/JCEF "path to a prebuilt" pattern; adds the checksum verification JBR tooling lacked; switching publisher later is a one-line pin change. |
| 2026-10-13 | Publisher stage 1 = JetBrains/skia releases; stage 2 (if b or a proceeds) = JBR-owned build job from `google/skia@chrome/mNNN` using the same `tools/skia_release` recipe, published like JCEF. Rejected: HumbleUI/SkiaBuild (shared libs, Ganesh, m143), rust-skia binaries (third-party cadence, glibc baseline, bindings baggage). | Google publishes no desktop binaries; JetBrains/skia artifacts are plain release assets built by a visible recipe, so the cross-team dependency is bounded to "download their zip" with a known exit. |
| 2026-10-14 | **Non-AA geometry never goes through `drawRect`/`drawPath`/`drawImageRect`; it is drawn as meshes (`SkVertices`): spans, scanlines, pixels, rect edges, parallelograms, and now every image blit (textured quad with a half-texel coordinate clamp).** | Graphite anti-aliases everything that is not pixel-aligned in device space regardless of `SkPaint::setAntiAlias(false)` (`Device::drawRect` snaps only axis-aligned rects; paths and image rects get analytic or MSAA coverage). Java2D non-AA semantics are pixel-centre inclusion, which is exactly what the GPU rasterizer does for a plain mesh — the same way the Metal pipeline draws its quads. Verified: rotated/sheared non-AA fills, rects and lines are pixel-identical to Metal (§11.13). |
| 2026-10-14 | **Graphite runs without internal multisampling** (`ContextOptions::fInternalMultisampleCount = k1`). AA paths (AA parallelograms, variant 2 later) use Graphite's path atlas. | Graphite renders a *whole pass* with 4× MSAA as soon as one draw in it needs it, so every non-AA edge recorded in the same frame got 4-level coverage (25 %/75 % blends in the matrix) — the mesh rule above is useless with MSAA on. With MSAA off, AA paths get full 8-bit coverage from the atlas, closer to Metal's analytic shader than 5 MSAA levels were. Cost to re-check: path-atlas rasterization is CPU (raster atlas) or compute work; only AA parallelograms use it in variant 1. |
| 2026-10-13 | **Do not attempt to share one Skia binary with Skiko/Compose.** Design `libawt_skia` for isolation instead (hidden visibility, no exported Skia symbols, `-Bsymbolic` on Linux). | Sharing needs Skiko to consume a JBR-provided shared Skia at a matching milestone — lockstep upgrades and Compose-side changes, not worth it now. Two isolated copies are the normal case (see 11.2). |
| 2026-10-15 | **Platform halves of the Skia backend carry the platform prefix first: `MTLSK*`** (`MTLSKPlatform.mm`, `MTLSKEnvironment.mm`, `MTLSKInterop.h`, identifiers `MTLSKGraphicsConfigInfo`, `MTLSK_PrivOps`); shared code stays `SK*`. | Precedent in this repo: the Vulkan backend's platform-bound classes are `WLVKGraphicsConfig`, `X11VKWindowSurfaceData`, `Win32VKGraphicsConfig` (platform, then API); OpenGL replaces the prefix (`CGL*`/`WGL*`/`GLX*`). A `Metal` suffix has no precedent outside Swing's Metal L&F. Linux and Windows halves follow as `VKSK*`/`WLSK*` or `DawnSK*`/`Win32SK*` by the same rule. |
| 2026-10-15 | **Java host-surface seam.** `SKSurfaceData` is abstract in `share/classes` (pipes, `validatePipe`, LCD rule, flush/dispose protocol); host-owned bounds, destination, transparency, scales, replacement, native resource and `isOnScreen` live in the platform subclasses; `SKGraphicsConfig` interface (precedent `OGLGraphicsConfig`) implemented by `MTLGraphicsConfig` and `Win32SKGraphicsConfig`; the 13 portable classes live in `share/classes/sun/java2d/skia/`. | One Java protocol for every host; what differs per host is how the window surface is owned and presented, not how it is drawn. |
| 2026-10-15 | **Names per half: Java = windowing layer, native = GPU API.** macOS `MTLSKSurfaceData` + `MTLSKPlatform.mm`; Windows `Win32SKGraphicsConfig`/`Win32SKSurfaceData` (like `Win32VKGraphicsConfig`) + `DawnSKPlatform.cpp`/`DawnSKEnvironment.cpp`. | Dawn is the API Skia runs on; D3D11 is Dawn's backend and invisible to the Java side. |
| 2026-10-15 | **Windows: the swap chain belongs to the HWND.** A replacing window surface takes over the `wgpu::Surface` of the surface it replaces (`Win32SKScreenUpdateManager`, D3D9 pattern, under the render-queue lock) and reconfigures it. | DXGI allows one flip-model swap chain per HWND; AWT replaces the window `SurfaceData` on every bounds change, and a second swap chain fails with `E_ACCESSDENIED`, which Dawn escalates to a lost device. |
| 2026-10-16 | **`sun.java2d.skia.shapes` defaults to `analytic`** (user decision; closes the §17 default question). **`msaa=4` rejected** as a default on Dawn as on Metal. | Analytic shapes run at display rate on both Metal and Dawn with 256-level coverage; general paths stay on Marlin at parity. MSAA would make general paths display-rate on Dawn but quantises coverage on Skia-rasterised paths (§11.7d). |
| 2026-10-19 | **One Dawn device per backend, shared by every configuration** (VM round 4, `06e94c106071`); a lost device is dropped so recovery still gets a new one. | Each display change created a device, a Graphite context and new atlases and made every texture of the previous configuration foreign (`SKPlatform_SameDevice` false); the D3D pipeline keeps its device across configurations too. Verified with `DisplayChangeProbe`: three configurations on one device, cross-configuration blit draws. |
| 2026-10-19 | **LCD text: `sun.java2d.skia.lcd=blend|twopass`, default `twopass` where the GPU has no framebuffer fetch (Dawn, Vulkan until measured), `blend` on Metal** (user decision after the VM's measurements, `098ad934afa5`). **Two-pass batches split on overlap** like blender batches (Mac fix 2026-10-19, §11.20): the two passes of one mesh are not sequential, overlapping glyphs of one batch got the colour added twice (light text on white turned white at the overlap). | The blender reads the destination; without framebuffer fetch every such draw ends the render pass and copies the destination (≈ 85 µs on the VM) and adjacent LCD glyphs overlap by the filter padding, so correct LCD text was ≈ 100× slower than GDI (§21). Two fixed-function passes (kModulate with the inverse coverage, kPlus with the colour times the coverage, DirectWrite's ClearType decomposition) need no destination read; the gamma correction folded into the coverage is exact on white and black and ≤ 15 levels off at glyph edges on mid-tones. The split's draw count on Dawn is **unmeasured** (hand-over round 5, §13). |
| 2026-10-21 | **`accumulate` is the default LCD mode where there is no framebuffer fetch** (Dawn, Metal on Intel/AMD, Vulkan until measured); `blend` stays the default under framebuffer fetch (user decision after the VM's IDE scene matrix, `6c8621d0d7a`). | Faster (3.0 / 8.5 / 14.5 ms vs two-pass 4.1 / 13.7 / 29.6 at 100 / 500 / 1000 lines on Dawn), closer at overlaps (max 15 vs 25), indistinguishable from two-pass in the IDE scenes (within 2–3 levels mean of the loops, 0 px beyond 32 in text scenes at 200 %). Cost: a destination-sized RGBA scratch per context (§13 F6 note stands). |
| 2026-10-21 | **Antialiased fills are not normalized** (VM finding from the IDE scene captures, `d2789679020d` + pill clamp fixup, `AnalyticShapeEdgeTest`): the analytic shape pipe snapped fill edges to pixel centres like strokes; Java normalizes strokes only (`AAShapePipe.renderPath`: `adjust = bs != null && !PURE`). | Every rounded panel and tab spilled a 50 % pixel over its right and bottom edge at 200 % (5–14 levels in the IDE). The render matrix did not see it because it sets `STROKE_PURE` for every cell; the Mac window scene at 1x went from 551 px beyond 32 levels against the loops to 0 (max 29). |

---
- **2026-10-13 — Naming: `SK*` prefix, `SKEnvironment`.** Classes and native symbols follow the backend family convention (`MTL*`, `VK*`, `WL*` + shared noun): `SKSurfaceData`, `SKRenderQueue`, `SKBackend_*`, `SKSDOps`. Measured precedent in `java.desktop`: acronyms are ALLCAPS without exception (431 : 0), so `SK` not `Sk`; the near-collision with Skia's own `Sk*` types is the same one Metal (`MTLContext` vs. Apple's `MTLDevice`) and Vulkan (`VKDevice` vs. `VkDevice`) already live with. Abbreviations in *new* names are avoided where no shared noun forces them (`Environment`, not `Env`; the only precedent for `Env` is `VKEnv`). Properties keep camelCase leaves and real namespaces only (`sun.java2d.skia.verbose`), matching the majority of `sun.java2d.*`. Doc comments only where they add thread/lifecycle/why information. Package `sun.java2d.skia`, `libawt_skia`, JNI exports unchanged.
- **2026-10-13 — Second branch on the JBR 25 line for IDE runs.** `main` is JDK 28 and has dropped `sun.awt.AppContext` (8385347); IntelliJ 2026.2.x (JBR 25) fails at startup on it, so no shipping IDE can run on the `main`-based prototype. For step 2 and all IDE measurements the four Skia commits are cherry-picked onto `jbr-release-25.0.4b508.27` — the exact JBR bundled with IDEA 2026.2.3 — as `manuel.unterhofer/JBR-10654-skia-backend-jbr25` (worktree `build/wt-jbr25`, config `macosx-aarch64-skia`, boot JDK Temurin 25 in `build/boot-jdk/jdk-25`). Development stays on `main`; the jbr25 branch is rebuilt from cherry-picks, never edited directly. Cost: one more full build per sync; benefit: A/B against the IDE's own runtime with everything else identical.

## 11. Findings

### 11.1 WP 1.3 — Skia prebuilt (2026-10-13)

Source: JetBrains/skia release `m155-3945378c4a` (published 2026-10-05), the same prebuilts Skiko consumes. Build recipe: `tools/skia_release/build.py` on branch `skiko-m155`. Archives unpacked under `build/skia-prebuilt/` (git-ignored); probes under `build/skia-prebuilt/probe/`.

#### What the release provides

- 32 assets; desktop targets: macOS/Linux/Windows × x64/arm64 × Debug/Release. **A `windows-Release-arm64` build exists** (55.6 MB) — relevant for WP 2.2.
- Build flags (all desktop): `is_official_build=true`, bundled expat/libjpeg/libpng/libwebp/zlib/icu/harfbuzz (no system libs), `--gpu-as-extension --enable-graphite`; **Dawn enabled on macOS and Windows** (`dawn_enable_metal`, `dawn_enable_d3d11`, `dawn_enable_d3d12`), not on Linux. `-fvisibility=default`.
- macOS: `skia_use_metal=true`, `skia_use_vulkan=true`, `-frtti`, `-stdlib=libc++`, `-mmacosx-version-min=10.13` (binary reports `minos 11.0`, `sdk 15.5`), **`skia_use_fonthost_mac=true` → CoreText; FreeType is not in the macOS build** (0 FreeType symbols, 132 CoreText symbols). Windows: `skia_use_direct3d=true`, `skia_use_vulkan=true`, platform font host (DirectWrite). Linux: gcc-10, `-D_GLIBCXX_USE_CXX11_ABI=0`, `-fno-exceptions -fno-rtti`, FreeType/fontconfig.
- Static libraries, macOS arm64 Release (MB): `libskia` 17.8, `skia_graphite_ext` 2.5, `skia_graphite_dawn_ext` 2.3, `dawn_combined` 21.1, `skia_ganesh_ext` 4.9, `skshaper` 6.1, `skparagraph` 0.5, `skunicode_icu` 11.6, `icu` 11.6, `harfbuzz` 6.0, `svg` 4.4, codecs/deps ~3.
  - Grouped: core + Graphite + codecs ≈ **24 MB** static; + Dawn ≈ 24 MB; + text stack (shaper/paragraph/ICU/HarfBuzz) ≈ 36 MB; Ganesh ext (not needed) 5 MB. Static-lib sizes overstate final shared-library size (dead-stripping); measure after building `libawt_skia`.
- Headers: `include/gpu/graphite/{mtl,vk,dawn}` all present; Dawn/webgpu headers under `out/.../gen/third_party/dawn/include`. Modules: skparagraph, skshaper, skunicode, svg, skottie, sksg.
- Consumer defines needed when compiling against the headers: `SK_GRAPHITE`, `SK_METAL` (and `SK_VULKAN` / `SK_DAWN` per backend); `SK_GANESH` only for legacy. `SK_SHAPER_HARFBUZZ_AVAILABLE` for the shaper.
- API in transition inside this milestone: both `skgpu::graphite::ContextFactory::MakeMetal` (older) and `SkContexts::MakeGraphite` (newer) exist; `MtlGraphiteUtils.h` is marked deprecated. Expect this kind of churn at every milestone bump.

#### Toolchain compatibility with the JDK build (macOS)

| | Skia prebuilt | JBR (`make/autoconf/flags*.m4`) | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| C++ standard | C++17 | `-std=c++17` | match |
| stdlib | libc++ | libc++ (Xcode clang) | match |
| Deployment target | minos 11.0 | `MACOSX_VERSION_MIN=11.00.00` | match |
| RTTI / exceptions | `-frtti`, some exception symbols | JDK libs: no `-fno-rtti`/`-fno-exceptions` (those are JVM-only flags) | compatible; compile `libawt_skia` with RTTI |
| Visibility | default | `-fvisibility=hidden` for JDK libs | fine for static linking into our own dylib |

#### Probes (all passed)

1. `raster_probe.cpp` — compile + link against `libskia.a` + codecs/deps; draws an AA rounded rect to a raster surface. Edge pixel at x = 8.5 → `0x7f7f7f` (50 % coverage). **Raster path works with the prebuilt as-is.**
2. `graphite_probe.cpp` — Graphite + Metal headers compile standalone with `-DSK_GRAPHITE -DSK_METAL` as Objective-C++.
3. `graphite_mtl_probe.mm` — creates `MTLDevice`/queue → Graphite context → `Recorder` → GPU `SkSurface`; records 50 AA rrects per frame, `snap()` → `insertRecording` → `submit`, 2000 frames; reads back. On Apple M1 Max: **0.276 ms/frame for 50 AA rounded rects (record + insert + submit, no present)**. Edge pixel → `0x808080` (vs `0x7f` on raster — Skia's own GPU/CPU paths differ by 1/255 here; note for WP 3.1). **Graphite-on-Metal works with the prebuilt, no custom Skia build needed for the macOS prototype.**

#### Implications

- **No custom Skia build is required for WP 2.1 on macOS.** Link `libskia.a + libskia_graphite_ext.a + codecs/deps` into a new `libawt_skia`; add text libs only when the text path is attempted.
- **Font engine policy conflict:** the prebuilts use platform font hosts (CoreText / DirectWrite / FreeType-on-Linux). A "FreeType everywhere" policy (deterministic, Lux-friendly) would need a custom Skia build (`skia_use_freetype=true` on macOS/Windows), i.e. owning a Skia build pipeline — a cost for path a to carry, not for b.
- **Windows path is in the box:** Windows prebuilts ship Graphite + Dawn (D3D11/D3D12) and an arm64 variant; WP 2.2 does not need a custom build either.
- **Sharing one binary with Skiko:** same milestone, same flags — feasible in principle, but Skiko links its own copy into `libskiko-*.dylib`; sharing would require Skiko to consume a JBR-provided shared Skia or vice versa. Treat as a later question.
- **Size budget:** expect a `libawt_skia` in the ~10–20 MB range on macOS before text; measure once built.

### 11.2 Two Skias in one process (2026-10-13, estimate — to verify in WP 2.1 on Linux)

Situation if the Skia backend ships: `libawt_skia` (static Skia, our milestone) and Skiko's `libskiko-*` (static Skia, Compose's milestone, built with `-fvisibility=default`) are both loaded into the IDE process via `System.load`, which uses `dlopen(RTLD_LAZY)` → `RTLD_LOCAL`.

| Platform | Risk | Assessment |
|---|---|---|
| macOS | Two-level namespace binds each dylib's references to its own definitions. | No clash expected. |
| Windows | Per-DLL symbol namespaces. | No clash expected. |
| Linux | ELF interposition: if either library's Skia symbols were in the *global* scope, the other's internal references could bind to the wrong version (ODR violation → memory corruption). With both loaded `RTLD_LOCAL` this does not happen, but it is fragile against anything loading one of them `RTLD_GLOBAL`. | Mitigate on our side: build `libawt_skia` with `-fvisibility=hidden` (JDK default), export only the JNI entry points, link with `-Wl,-Bsymbolic` (or `-Bsymbolic-functions`) and a version script. Verify by running IJ with the Compose plugin enabled and the Skia backend on Linux. |

Other consequences of two copies: two GPU contexts on one device (fine; `SharedTextures` interop already crosses contexts), two resource caches and glyph atlases (~tens of MB), two copies of ICU/HarfBuzz if text is linked. Acceptable for the experiment; a shared binary is a later optimisation, if ever.

---

### 11.3 WP 2.1 — first renders (2026-10-13)

Test: `build/skia-smoke/SkiaRender.java` paints a primitive mix (fills, `drawRect`, lines, AA rounded rects fill/stroke, 2-stop/linear/radial gradients, `BufferedImage` blits plain and scaled, plain and AA text, 50 % `AlphaComposite`, elliptical clip, `copyArea`) to (a) a `BufferedImage` (software loops = canonical), (b) a `VolatileImage` on Metal, (c) a `VolatileImage` on Skia — each read back to PNG — and to a window with Swing components.

| Comparison (620×300, off-screen) | Differing pixels | > 32 levels | max level |
|---|---|---|---|
| software vs. Metal | 10.06 % | 4,191 | 240 |
| software vs. Skia (before review fixes) | 14.90 % | 5,905 | — |
| software vs. Skia (after review fixes) | 15.07 % | 4,710 | 240 |
| Metal vs. Skia (before review fixes) | 11.07 % | 2,784 | — |
| Metal vs. Skia (after review fixes) | 10.34 % | 978 | 88 |

(The "differing pixels" share is dominated by ±1–4 level differences in gradients and AA coverage; the "> 32" column is the structural one. After the fixes Metal and Skia differ structurally only in AA coverage along edges and glyphs; the max of 88 is on a radial gradient.)

Observations:
- All elements render through Skia; the window path (back buffer → window texture → `MTLLayer` presentation) works with Aqua Swing components. Screen capture via `screencapture` confirms it.
- Pixel-identical at sampled points: fills, `drawRect`, 50 % alpha (after fixing a double application of extra alpha — Java folds it into the color pixel), `BufferedImage` blits (±1 level), AA rrect interior.
- Differences are where expected: AA coverage values (Marlin tiles composited by Skia vs. Metal shader; vs. software exact), radial gradient interpolation (±3 levels), text glyph placement, and **lines at HiDPI**: at scale 2 lines become `DRAW_PARALLELOGRAM` with fractional edges; Metal's shader and Skia's non-AA fill pick different rows, visible after the 2×→1× snapshot downsample. Graphite does honor non-AA (probe: crisp rows for fractional-edge fills), so this is an edge-rule difference, not AA leakage. Plain 1× lines use Bresenham runs and match.
- Graphite does not draw raster `SkImage`s directly; every coverage tile, glyph and software blit is uploaded with `SkImages::TextureFromImage` per draw. Functional, not fast — the glyph/tile caches are the obvious next optimization and the reason text performance must not be measured on this variant yet.
- `Robot.createScreenCapture` returns the desktop *behind* the Skia-presented window even while it is visibly on screen (Metal windows capture fine). Not understood yet; could be benign (capture path) or a real presentation difference visible to screen-sharing tools. Open item.
- Build/debug lessons: `J2dTraceLn` is debug-only (use `J2dRlsTraceLn` in release builds); `-Werror` in javac treats JEP 472 restricted-method warnings as errors (`@SuppressWarnings("restricted")`); `SkPath` is immutable in m156 (`SkPathBuilder`).

### 11.5 WP 2.1 — the black-window bug (2026-10-13)

Symptom: `RenderPerfTest` (robot mode) hung on its first test with an entirely black window on Skia, while every smoke-test scene rendered. Not a presentation problem: the Swing back buffer read back black *inside* `paint`, all opcodes arrived with correct parameters, Graphite's `insertRecording`/`submit` succeeded, a standalone Graphite probe of the same sequence passed.

Cause: `SKBackend_SetDestination` captured `baseSaveCount = canvas->getSaveCount()` from a canvas that still held the `save()` + clip `applyCanvasState()` had left on it the last time that surface was the destination. The base ratcheted up on every switch, `restoreToCount(base)` could never pop the stale clip, and every later draw on that surface stayed clipped to **whatever clip was last used on it**. RenderPerfTest's frame contains an Aqua `JLabel` whose 135×16 text clip was the last draw into the back buffer before the blit → the back buffer stayed clipped to that strip forever → black window and an unseen Robot marker. The smoke scenes happened to end every paint with a full clip.

Fix: `restoreToCount(1)` before taking the base count (commit "reset the canvas save stack when switching destinations"). Lessons for the backend: (1) per-surface canvas state must be reset on every destination switch — Metal has no equivalent because its clip is re-applied per draw from `MTLContext`; (2) the bug was invisible to every scene that ended with a wide clip, so 3.1's corpus needs cases where a *clipped* draw is the last op before a surface switch; (3) debugging toolkit that worked: `-Dsun.java2d.trace=log` (which loops run), release-level opcode tracing (`J2D_TRACE_LEVEL=4`), back-buffer readback from inside `paint` (`RepaintManager.getVolatileOffscreenBuffer(...).getSnapshot()`), the minimal Swing reproducer in `build/skia-bench/repro/Repro.java` (toggles: `-Drep.firsttext` etc.), and the standalone Graphite probe `build/skia-prebuilt/probe/a8_probe.mm`. The old open item "Robot does not see Skia windows" was almost certainly this bug.

### 11.6 WP 2.1 — JVM crash in RenderPerfTest `Image_XOR` (2026-10-13)

`SIGSEGV` in `_platform_memmove` ← `Context::finalizeAsyncReadPixels` callback ← `~GpuWorkSubmission` ← `Context::submit` ← `SKBackend_EndOfFlush`, preceded by Graphite's `** ERROR ** Unfinished command buffer status: 3` (`MTLCommandBufferStatusError`). Two defects:
1. **GPU error**: with an XOR composite every `drawImage` is routed through `SKAnyCompositeBlit` (read destination back → composite in software → upload). `SKBackend_SurfaceToSwBlit` passed the source rect to `asyncRescaleAndReadPixels` *unclamped* to the surface; images partially outside the surface produced a rect leaving the texture, which Metal rejects at execution time (API validation is silent — it is not an API misuse). `MTLBlitLoops_SurfaceToSwBlit` clamps; now so do we.
2. **Use-after-free**: a failed command buffer stays "unfinished" in Graphite's queue, and the readback callback is delivered from a *later* `submit()` — into the stack-allocated target of a call that had long returned. The readback request is now heap-allocated, reference-counted between caller and callback, and owns its pixel copy; a late or missing callback is harmless and reported.
Lesson: Graphite's async readback is only synchronous in the happy path; anything passed as callback context must survive the call. Also: the XOR "performance" numbers are not comparable — Metal implements XOR; the Skia path draws Src (ledger §13) and the read-modify-write blit fallback makes XOR image draws ~1.5 FPS on both.

### 11.7 Step 1 → Step 3 — RenderPerfTest, Metal vs. Skia variant 1 (2026-10-13/14)

Setup: `test/jdk/performance/client/RenderPerfTest`, default robot mode (on-screen, Robot-timed, display-bound at ~52–56 FPS), 1 repeat, Apple M1 Max, same `macosx-aarch64-skia` build for both (`-Dsun.java2d.skia=true` for Skia), idle machine. Logs: `build/skia-bench/rpt-metal.log`, `rpt-skia-all.log` (prototype before the caches), `rpt-skia-final.log` (after §11.9–§11.11); `python3 compare.py <a> <b>` prints the per-test table. XOR tests excluded from ratios (Skia draws Src where Metal does XOR). **Geomean Skia/Metal over 59 tests: 0.50× before the caches → 1.04× after.**

| Group | n | prototype | with caches |
|---|---|---|---|
| Shape-clipped fills | 2 | 2.60–2.85× | **2.27–2.54×** |
| Mixed VolatileImage + fills / text | 5 | 0.24–2.11× | **1.16–2.18×** |
| Radial-gradient ovals | 1 | 0.76–0.76× | **1.67–1.67×** |
| Software-source blits | 2 | 1.20–1.42× | **1.18–1.30×** |
| Text (all sizes/modes) | 14 | 0.07–1.01× | **0.89–1.11×** |
| Managed BufferedImage draws | 4 | 0.17–0.24× | **0.85–0.97×** |
| Gradient AA ovals | 3 | 1.12–1.27× | **0.99–1.12×** |
| AA shapes (Marlin tiles) | 6 | 0.47–0.67× | **0.70–1.06×** |
| Non-AA shapes (spans/lines) | 8 | 0.29–0.51× | **0.84–1.03×** |
| Display-bound rect/line/box/VolImage tests | 14 | 0.90–1.03× | **0.80–1.04×** |

Reading:
- The structural wins are unchanged by the caches and are the actual Skia-vs-Metal signal: shape clips (`clipPath` vs. stencil) 2.3–2.5×, mixed image/text/fill scenes (batching across pipeline-state changes) 2.0–2.2×, radial gradients 1.7×.
- Everything that was 0.07–0.3× was a missing cache in the prototype, now at or near parity; the remaining systematic gaps are the AA/non-AA shape paths at 0.70–0.85× (per-op CPU in Java + mesh build; Metal's vertex cache is leaner) and a **0.80–0.86× pattern on display-bound tests** (`FlatBox` 45 vs. 56) that does not exist off screen (Skia ≥ Metal there, §11.10) — a presentation-latency effect of the Robot-timed loop (when the frame becomes visible relative to the sync), not throughput. To examine with `-DREPORT_OVERALL_FPS=true`/`-e=volatile` before any conclusion; it also decides whether on-screen robot mode is the right instrument for the IDE-scene measurements.
- RenderPerfTest's primitives are synthetic; the IDE-scene measurements (editor scroll, popups, islands) remain the decisive ones for the plan.

#### 11.7b Re-run after the correctness pass and MSAA off (2026-10-14, `rpt-skia-msaaoff.log`, `rpt-*-s10b/s15.log`, `rpt-skia-msaa{1,4}-aa.log`)

Full suite on Skia with commits `7cd8569a3d32`+`a04a6ddbf581`: **geomean Skia/Metal 1.02× over the same 59 tests** (1.04× before the pass). With the six XOR tests included it is 1.26×: Metal's XOR runs at 1.2–3.5 FPS, Skia's blender at 26–51 FPS (`Image_XOR` 1.6 on both — bound elsewhere). Display-bound tests moved from ~45 to ~51 FPS (0.91–0.96× of Metal instead of 0.8×). Structural wins unchanged (shape clips 2.4–2.8×, mixed scenes 1.9–2.1×, `RadGrad3RotatedOval` 1.7×).

**MSAA A/B (§14 D2), 8 AA tests, same binary, `sun.java2d.skia.msaa=4` vs default off:** geomean 0.97×; only the AA-parallelogram tests pay — `RotatedBoxAA` 46.7 → 34.9 FPS (0.75×, Metal 50), `LinesAA` 47.4 → 40.5 (0.85×, Metal 51); ovals, bubbles, quads unchanged or slightly better with the atlas (`WiredBubblesAA` 1.14×). So the cost of "no MSAA" is confined to the one primitive variant 1 sends through Skia path rendering, about 25 % there, and it is the only remaining ≥ 20 % deficit against Metal in the suite.

**Fractional scale (§14 D1), 14-test subset at `uiScale` 1.0 vs 1.5 on both pipelines:** scale cost geomean Metal 1.01×, Skia 1.00×; Skia/Metal 0.95× at 1.0 and 0.94× at 1.5. The mesh path has no scale-dependent cost; nothing in the subset changes rank between scales.

**Memory (asked while watching the suite):** GC is not involved — 135 young collections totalling 0.6 s in 6 min, no full GC, none during a 20 s window at 45 FPS. RSS is step-wise per test and flat within tests on both pipelines (Metal 380 → 449 MB over the subset, Skia 383 → 725 MB with one ≈285 MB step early in the run and flat afterwards; the AA-only run sat at 656 MB for 3 minutes). No leak signature; Skia's footprint is ≈280 MB higher — Graphite's resource-cache budget defaults to 256 MB (`ContextOptions::fGpuBudgetInBytes`), the likely explanation, to verify (§13). The visible "dips" during the suite were the non-AA → AA test transitions (e.g. `FlatOval` 40 → `FlatOvalAA` 14–23 FPS), present on both pipelines. **Skipped frames** (harness waited > 1 s for its marker pixel): Skia 2–12 per run in 1–2 tests, Metal 0–1; open item.

#### 11.7c Variant 2 performance — AA subset, 17 tests (2026-10-14, `rpt-v1-aa.log`, `rpt-v2-aa.log`, `rpt-v2-msaa4-aa.log`)

| Test | variant 1 | variant 2 | variant 2 + MSAA 4 | Metal |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FlatOvalAA | 18.4 | **50.6** | 51.0 | 23.4 |
| WiredBubblesAA | 17.5 | **50.8** | 50.5 | 19.8 |
| ClipFlatOvalAA | 43.9 | 48.0 | 48.6 | 17.5 |
| LinGradRotatedOvalAA / LinGrad3 / RadGrad3 | 13.9 / 14.1 / 14.1 | 33.3 / 33.2 / 33.6 | 48.6 / 48.7 / 48.6 | 13.3 / 11.8 / 12.6 |
| RotatedOvalAA | 21.1 | 34.1 | 49.4 | 24.1 |
| FlatQuadAA | 10.2 | 14.0 | **51.4** | 14.5 |
| WiredQuadAA | 18.3 | 14.8 | **50.9** | 18.0 |
| RotatedBoxAA / LinesAA (AA parallelograms, not the shape pipe) | 35.5 / 40.3 | 35.3 / 40.1 | 51.0 / 50.9 | 53.1 / 54.1 |
| TextWiredQuadAAMix / VolImageWiredQuadAAMix | 26.1 / 26.7 | 26.6 / 28.2 | 47.1 / 50.9 | 23.0 / 23.7 |
| geomean vs variant 1 | 1.00× | **1.37×** | 1.93× | — |
| geomean vs Metal | — | **1.42×** | — | 1.00× |

Reading: (1) **Analytic shapes (ovals, round rects, rects) are the win of variant 2** — 2.2–2.9× over both variant 1 and Metal, display-bound now; this is the rounded-UI case. (2) **General paths and stroked general paths are atlas-bound without MSAA** (FlatQuadAA 14, WiredQuadAA 15 FPS, below variant 1's 18 for stroked quads); with `sun.java2d.skia.msaa=4` the same tests reach the display rate (3.4–3.7×), and AA parallelograms/rotated ovals gain 1.3–1.5× as well. So §14 D2 is now a real trade: MSAA buys general-path performance and costs the hard edges of the two non-pixel-aligned non-AA cases (rotated/sheared parallelograms, rotated image blits). (3) No skipped frames in the variant 2 runs; variant 1 had 39 in this run (`ClipFlatOvalAA` 28) — the mask-tile path is where the >1 s stalls come from (§13 item).

**After `analytic shapes under any transform` (`47c3128b9882`, jbr25 cherry-picked; `rpt-hybrid2-aa.log`, `rpt-v2b-aa.log`):** rotated/sheared ovals and round rects reach the analytic renderer in both modes — `RotatedOvalAA` 50.0, `LinGradRotatedOvalAA` 48.2, `LinGrad3`/`RadGrad3` 49.4/48.8 FPS (variant 1: 14–21, Metal: 12–24, the path route: 33). The **analytic mode now gives 2.15× over variant 1 on the 9 shape tests** with general paths at Marlin parity (`FlatQuadAA` 11.3 vs 10.2, `WiredQuadAA` 18.5 vs 18.3) and no skipped frames; the all-Skia mode keeps the atlas penalty on general paths (14–15 FPS) until D2 is resolved.

#### 11.7d Option C measured (2026-10-14, `5cf00fba7bab`): MSAA on + pixel-exact non-AA via span decomposition

Non-AA parallelograms (fills and rings) and fractional/rotated image blits are decomposed into integer spans by the pixel-centre rule (half-open, like `ParallelogramUtils.h`) when `sun.java2d.skia.msaa=4` is set; blit spans carry texture coordinates mapped back through the canvas matrix. **Non-AA exactness holds under MSAA**: EABlitTest reproducer 3 colours, octant lines and polylines identical to software. **But MSAA quantizes AA coverage to four levels**, and that costs fidelity on every AA edge Skia rasterizes: matrix cells within 32 levels of software with MSAA on — marlin mode 826 (835 off), analytic 769 (798), skia 663 (779); the flagged cells are AA lines, rects, dashes and parallelograms with 4-level edges (max 47–66 vs ≤ 26 without MSAA). So option C trades general-path speed (display rate, §11.7c) against AA quality on all Skia-rasterized edges; `SampleCount::k8` would halve the step but not remove it. Qualitatively (`build/skia-smoke/msaa-compare.png`, software | Skia | Skia+MSAA at 6×): edges stay in place, nothing blurs; the coverage ramp along thin AA lines and outlines collapses to 5 values (10 distinct pixel values per cell vs Marlin's 61–94 and the atlas' 76–125), which reads as beaded/ropey lines — the classic 4× MSAA look. The per-pixel metric captured it correctly (max error = one quarter step ≈ 64 levels); a perceptual metric would rank it the same. 8× would halve the beading, still 4× coarser than Marlin. Why MSAA changes the *algorithm*: with a multisampled target Graphite renders paths by GPU tessellation/stencil-and-cover and lets the hardware's per-sample inside/outside test provide the AA (k/N coverage); without it paths go through the atlas with analytic/scanline coverage (8-bit). Coverage-based draws (analytic rrect/oval, Marlin tiles, glyphs) keep their 256-level alpha under MSAA, which is why the hybrid's ovals do not degrade — only what Skia draws as a *path* (AA parallelograms, general shapes in `skia` mode) does. Graphite offers `ContextOptions::fMinimumPathSizeForMSAA` (paths smaller than N device px avoid MSAA even when it is on): a candidate fourth knob, `sun.java2d.skia.msaaMinPathSize`, to keep small UI paths analytic and let only large paths use the MSAA renderer — untested. **In the wild (2026-10-14, sources in the session log):** Skia's default for both Ganesh and Graphite is internal MSAA 4; Chrome keeps Graphite's default except where `msaa_is_slow` (older Intel), where it falls back to `SampleCount::k1` with the comment that the single-sampled fallback is the CPU raster path atlas ("still a little slow and buggy"); for Ganesh/canvas Chrome asks for 8. Flutter's Impeller uses 4× MSAA for everything plus SDF shapes, relying on tile-based mobile GPUs where MSAA is nearly free. Android HWUI leaves Skia's default. Skia engineers' own advice for quality-first clients: do not enable MSAA, shader/analytic AA is higher quality ("4× MSAA can only account for five degrees of pixel coverage"), and 16× is only partially supported by GPUs. Several embedded/Android drivers (Adreno Vulkan, Vivante) have MSAA path-rendering bugs that forced clients to k1. So: browsers/Flutter accept 4× MSAA edges on paths for speed and memory; a pixel-fidelity-first 2D backend is the odd one out, and that is a deliberate choice, not a misconfiguration — but it also means our `k1` path is the less-travelled one in Skia (Chrome's comment about the atlas being "slow and buggy" is a risk to track). **Recommendation: keep MSAA off and the hybrid (`analytic`) as the candidate default**; MSAA stays a knob for backends where the atlas is slower or multisampling cheaper (tile-based GPUs) — see §17 on per-backend configuration.

Options for D2 recorded in §17: (A) **hybrid** — Skia for analytic shapes, Marlin tiles for general paths, MSAA off (captures the IDE win, no new visual deltas); (B) MSAA on, accept 4-level edges on rotated non-AA geometry; (C) MSAA on **and** pixel-exact non-AA via span decomposition of rotated parallelograms and rotated blits (spans are axis-aligned integer rects and therefore MSAA-safe) — best of both if the decomposition stays cheap; rotated non-AA geometry is rare in UIs.

### 11.12 Rendering comparison matrix — first WP 3.1 corpus (2026-10-14)

Trigger: the user asked whether the differences are really AA-only (XOR was still drawn as Src; an IDE header gradient looked truncated). Built `build/skia-smoke/RenderMatrix.java` + `MatrixDiff.java` (+ `MatrixList`, `MatrixCells` for triage): **1152 cells** = 13 paints × 19 shapes × AA on/off, plus composites (Src, SrcOver α, SrcIn, Clear, XOR) × shapes and × paints, transforms (½-px translate, 1.5×, 2×, rotate, shear) × shapes and × paints, clips (rect, ellipse, two rects) × shapes and × paints. Each cell is rendered identically through the software loops (`BufferedImage`), Metal and Skia (`VolatileImage`), 64×64 px, over a background with a dark band so alpha/XOR/Clear are visible. Per cell: differing / >32-level / max for software–Metal, software–Skia, Metal–Skia; classifier: *pass* (Metal–Skia ≤ 32), *coverage-level* (Metal deviates from software comparably), *suspect* (Skia deviates where Metal matches software). `matrix-suspects.png` is a software | Metal | Skia contact sheet of the worst cells.

Found and fixed (commit "real XOR mode, premultiplied gradient interpolation, texture paints from cached textures"):
1. **Gradients with translucent stops** faded through grey (every `LIN_ALPHA` cell, ~57 levels across the fill, Metal = software). Java and the Metal shaders interpolate the *premultiplied* stop pixels; I had unpremultiplied them (losing a transparent stop's colour) and let Skia interpolate straight. Fix: `SkGradient::Interpolation::fInPremul = kYes`. This is almost certainly the IDE header gradient (project colour fading to transparent).
2. **XOR** really implemented: runtime blender with the Metal shaders' semantics (`dst ^ ((color ^ xorPixel)·coverage)`, images `dst ^ pixel ^ xorPixel`), bit-by-bit in arithmetic because runtime blenders are ES2 SkSL. First version XORed uncovered pixels too (text became boxes) — caught by the matrix. AA + XOR installs the solid pipes like Metal. **Result: XOR fills match the software loops exactly (0 differing pixels); Metal does not** (whole-fill differences up to 127 levels). XOR text ≈ Metal; both differ from software's (binary-coverage) XOR text.
3. **Texture paint from a cached texture**: `BufferedPaints.setTexturePaint` goes through `getSourceSurfaceData` → with §11.10's proxy it hands over the cached `SKTexture`; the native side locked it as a raster → `InternalError` on the flusher → **IDEA failed at startup** ("Start Failed … SKSD_Lock not implemented"). Now sampled as a texture; a Skia surface reaching the software-blit path is sampled too.
Also hardened (previous commit): batches are flushed before a surface is re-wrapped or its context disposed (the IDE crash in `RectBatch::flush` with a dangling canvas; the matrix did not reproduce that one — IDE lifecycles did).

After the fixes: 1152 cells → **835 pass, 307 coverage-level, 10 suspect**; the 10 were all sheared non-AA fills (26–34 edge pixels, max ≤ 49) — not an edge rule after all but Graphite's MSAA pass (§11.13); after §11.13: **949 pass, 203 coverage-level, 0 suspect**, and rotated/sheared non-AA `FILL_RECT`/`DRAW_RECT`/`DRAW_LINE` are identical to Metal (0 differing pixels). Other observations the matrix made explicit: on `Src`/`SrcIn` with translucent colours Skia matches software (0–1 level) while Metal writes premultiplied values (~96 levels off); `CLEAR_RECT` differs from software on *both* GPU backends identically (VolatileImage graphics default background); Metal's `copyArea` under a non-SrcOver composite throws `RasterFormatException` (`MTLAnyCompositeBlit`, pre-existing, cells excluded). Java itself rejects `copyArea` under non-translate transforms.

IDE confirmation: the truncated header gradient reported from a Skia IDE run (spring-petclinic-kotlin, red project colour) was fix 1 — the islands header fades the project colour to transparent. Same-config A/B on the rebuilt jbr25 image: both runs start and run (no crash, no startup error); header red-channel profile within 3–7 levels of Metal at every sample across the full width (`build/skia-bench/idea/pc-header-skia-vs-metal.png`).

Lesson: the benchmark suite could not see any of 1–3; a cheap, systematic three-way matrix found all of them in one run and classified the rest. It is the seed of the WP 3.1 corpus; next additions: more paints under transforms, strokes under transforms, text under transforms/composites, image types (gray, indexed, 3-byte), HiDPI-scaled sheets.

### 11.13 jtreg java2d/awt subset on Skia, and two Graphite rules (2026-10-14)

Suite: `make run-test TEST="jdk/sun/java2d jdk/java/awt/Graphics2D jdk/java/awt/image" JTREG="JAVA_OPTIONS=-Dsun.java2d.skia=true;JOBS=1;TIMEOUT_FACTOR=2"` (≈ 250 tests executed, ~100 headful; the rest excluded/keyword/platform-filtered). First run: 13 failures. Each re-run on Metal (`-Dsun.java2d.metal=true`, same build): **5 fail on Metal too** — `CopyAreaOOB`, `DrawString/GposTest`, `multiresolution/MenuMultiresolutionIconTest`, `GdiRendering/GdiBlitOffscreenTest`, `PolyVertTest#opengl` — pre-existing on this machine/branch, not ours; `OpenGL/SrcMaskOps#id1` flaky on both. The **6 Skia-specific** failures, all fixed (commit `7cd8569a3d32` on `main`, `92ceabd32aec` on jbr25):

| Test | Cause on Skia | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `VolatileImage/GradientPaints` | `LinearGradientPaint`/`RadialGradientPaint` with `LINEAR_RGB` interpolated in sRGB | `SkGradient::Interpolation::fColorSpace = kSRGBLinear` (stops converted like `BufferedPaints` does) |
| `DrawImage/IncorrectClipXorModeSurface2Surface` | surface→surface blit under XOR had no `AnyCompositeBlit` for `SKSurface`/`SKTexture` sources | registered both; converts through `IntArgbPre` like Metal |
| `DrawImage/IncorrectAlphaSurface2SW` | readback unpremultiplied with float division; Java expects `div8table` rounding | `unpremulJava` replicates `AlphaMath.c` |
| `VolatileImage/DrawHugeImageTest` | a 46k-px-wide software image uploaded as one texture (> `maxTextureSize`) → nothing drawn | software-source blits clipped to destination/clip, mapped back to source texels, chunked by `Recorder::maxTextureSize()` |
| `DrawImage/EABlitTest` | rotated image blits produced 37 colours instead of 3: Graphite AA-edges every non-axis-aligned `drawImageRect` (and `drawRect`) | blits are textured meshes (`drawImageQuad`), sample coordinates clamped half a texel inside the source rect |
| `OpenGL/SrcMaskOps#id1` | a solid-colour AA shape vanished when a gradient-painted AA shape followed in the same frame: the mask cache drew its colour-tile mesh before the staged atlas row was uploaded (Graphite rule 3 below) | `SKSpriteBatch::beforeDraw` hook, mask cache uploads its staging row there (`a04a6ddbf581`, jbr25 `3a84d6d02328`) |
| sheared non-AA fills (matrix, not jtreg) | 25 %/75 % blends on edges = 4× MSAA: once any draw in a pass needs MSAA, Graphite multisamples the whole pass | non-AA parallelograms as meshes **and** `fInternalMultisampleCount = k1` |

Two Graphite rules worth remembering for any Java2D-on-Skia work (both are documented only in Skia source comments, `src/gpu/graphite/Device.cpp`):
1. *"Graphite assumes everything is anti-aliased."* Non-AA is emulated only for axis-aligned rects by snapping to pixels; anything else (rotated rects, paths, image rects) gets coverage. Java2D's non-AA primitives therefore have to be meshes, which the GPU rasterizes with the pixel-centre rule — the rule the software loops and Metal's quads use. This is now uniform in `SKBackend.mm` (rect batch, parallelograms, blits).
2. MSAA is a per-pass property: a pass containing one MSAA draw multisamples every draw in it. With the default `k4`, non-AA edges only looked right in frames without paths — which is why the EABlitTest reproducer passed in isolation while the matrix still failed.
3. Upload ordering: `SkSurface::writePixels` on an atlas surface is recorded as a pending upload of *that* device; it becomes a task ahead of the destination's pass only when a draw sampling the atlas is recorded afterwards (or at snap, in device-tracking order — i.e. undefined for our purposes). Every atlas write must therefore be followed by a sampling draw before anything can snap the destination. The glyph cache does this by construction (upload, then mesh); the mask cache now does it through the batch's `beforeDraw` hook. The failure was deterministic but depended on whether the destination surface was created before the atlas (`SrcMaskOps` yes, my first reproducer no).

Side findings: Metal has no `MTLSwToSurfaceTransform` for `IntRgb` (it goes through `TransformHelper` in software and a plain blit; Skia has native sw→surface transform loops for all int types — faster, same pixels now). With MSAA off, AA parallelograms (`FILL_RECT/AA` under rotate/shear) deviate from Metal's analytic shader by ≤ 34 levels on edge pixels (was ≤ 104 with MSAA), everything else unchanged. Remaining matrix deviations > 32 vs. Metal are coverage-level by construction: AA edges, LCD text (≤ 34), and the seam pixels of `REPEAT` gradients (≈ 40 px per cell at the cycle boundary, max 192 — a sampling-phase question, §13).

Final state (run 7 after the fixes, `build/jtreg-skia-7.log`): **256 passed, 7 failed** → `SrcMaskOps#id1` (real, fixed above, passes now), `ScaleParamsOOB#id1` (passed on immediate re-run, flaky), `SimplePrimQuality#opengl` and the four Metal-also tests (`GposTest`, `CopyAreaOOB`, `MenuMultiresolutionIconTest`, `PolyVertTest#opengl`; `GdiBlitOffscreenTest` passed this time — flaky). `SimplePrimQuality`: the diff is a 1/8-pixel blur of the whole capture (isolated 1-px lines at 75 %, neighbours at 12.5 %, a 1-px lighter border) = bilinear 2× window-server upscaling of a `uiScale=1` window on this Retina display, then Robot's 2:1 downsample; a direct Robot capture (`/tmp/jt/BlurTest.java`) shows the identical pixels on Metal. Its `#default` id is excluded on macOS upstream for that reason (JDK-8392800/8392802); the `#opengl` id passing in the Metal baseline most likely ran the OpenGL pipeline (`-Dsun.java2d.opengl=True` is on its command line and CGL is still in this tree). **Net: zero Skia-specific failures left in the subset.**

### 11.14 Independent review of variant 1 (2026-10-14)

Full report: `build/notes/review-2026-10-14-variant1.md` (Fable agent, read-only, all 7 commits, Metal/Vulkan/Marlin/shared pipes as references). My verification of the top findings:

| # | Finding | My check | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| H1 ✔ `b864d1becb49` | `SKSurfaceData` lacks `canRenderLCDText`; LCD hint is downgraded to gray on Skia surfaces | **Runtime-confirmed**: `/tmp/jt/LcdProbe2.java` — on Skia, LCD text is pixel-identical to gray AA (0 differing pixels); on Metal LCD differs from gray (232 px, max 55). macOS produces neutral-valued 3-byte LCD glyphs (no colour fringes on either pipeline), so the difference is the LCD gamma blend, not subpixel colour. | **Correction to this ledger:** the LCD blender, LCD atlas and sub-pixel LCD paths of §11.9 have never executed in any measurement; §11.12's "XOR text ≈ Metal" and the matrix's `TEXT_LCD` rows (≤ 34 levels) compared Skia *gray* against Metal *LCD*. RenderPerfTest `*LCD` numbers on Skia are gray-AA numbers. |
| H2 ✔ `b864d1becb49` | Strike disposal runs on the Metal flusher via `MTLGraphicsConfig.getContext()` while the Skia flusher dereferences `GlyphInfo` | `getContext()` returns the `MTLContext` (`MTLGraphicsConfig.java:168`) — confirmed statically; race window not reproduced | real, latent |
| H3 ✔ `b864d1becb49` | Lock-order inversion MTL→SK (`MTLLayer.drawInMTLContext`) vs SK→MTL (texture-paint proxy creation inside `validate`), plus nested `validateContext` mid-validation | lock order confirmed (`MTLLayer.java:148-163`); `SKPaints.isTextureValid` indeed lacks `MTLPaints`' out-of-lock proxy pre-caching | real; deadlock needs the display callback to coincide, mis-targeted primitive not yet reproduced |
| H4 ✔ `b864d1becb49` | Texture paint reads every source as `INT_ARGB_PRE` (`SKBackend.mm:690`) | confirmed statically | real for INT_ARGB with α<255 and INT_RGB sources until the proxy texture takes over |
| H5 ✔ `b864d1becb49` (delegate kept reachable until the Skia side disposed) | `SKSDOps.mtl` may dangle between the two Disposer records; `IsoBlit` adds no source reference | not verified | plausible |
| M1 ✔ `978bd2a8ea3a` (confirmed at 1×: 67 XOR pixels) | Polyline joints drawn twice (`DrawPoly` segments inclusive) | not verified; visible under XOR/translucent only | plausible |
| M2 ✔ `b864d1becb49` | `SYNC` opcode does not wait for the GPU (`EndOfFlush` → `Submit(cc, false)`) | confirmed (`SKRenderQueue.mm:286`, `SKBackend.mm:1525`) | real; `Toolkit.sync()` semantics |
| M3 ✔ `978bd2a8ea3a` | Cross-context images wrapped with the source's recorder | not verified; multi-display only | plausible |
