- **2026-11-02 (Mac, afternoon 3) — the capture routine's second round: 420 s → 28 s for an arm's light and dark (user: "shave off even more"; "a lot of waits"; Air must stay).** Profiled first (`profile (s / calls)` at the end of every run): of a 34 s scene phase, 15 s were 184 Robot screen captures at 82 ms each — every wait is built on them — 8.6 s the settle before each of ten captures (two samples 300 ms apart, 600 ms floor), and the plugin's exit never ran: the IDE's `RestService` base class counts requests per client and answered "429 Too Many Requests" after a few dozen, which the driver had taken for success. **Changes:** (1) `SceneService` is a plain `HttpRequestHandler` (no rate limit; keep-alive, a reconnect per command cost 30 ms) and the driver fails on any non-200. (2) Sampling through the Swift helper (`fgfast sample x y w h cols rows`: the frame at nominal resolution, 1440 grid pixels) — `CGWindowListCreateImage` and `CGDisplayCreateImage` are *unavailable* in the macOS 26 SDK (ScreenCaptureKit is the replacement) but the symbol is still in the framework: a `dlsym` lookup; 29 ms per frame instead of 82. (3) Settle 150 ms apart, 300 ms floor; the trailing quiet after a detected change has no floor; scene minimums halved. (4) Both themes in one IDE session (`run-scenes.sh <arm> both <scale>`): a plugin `theme` command sets the UI theme id and the editor colour scheme (`ExperimentalLight`/`Light`, `ExperimentalDark`/`Dark`, the seeds' values), the workspace scene closes the terminal tab so the second theme opens a fresh shell (a hidden shell left two tabs once). (5) The index snapshot: `ide/snapshot/<IDE build>/system`, an APFS clone (`cp -c`) per run; the first run without one indexes and is ended with SIGTERM — the IDE's shutdown hooks flush the indexes, the next run reaches smart mode 3.6 s after launch with no "App fingerprint changed" and nothing scheduled — the plugin's exit (ApplicationEx.exit, then the Exit action) never ended the process. (6) Heavy unrelated bundled plugins disabled in the seed (Kotlin, full-line completion, databases, REST client, JavaScript, Grazie, Kubernetes, Docker, Vue, Swagger, Qodana, Gateway, remote development, Gradle, Maven); Air stays and loads. (7) The wrapper: `caffeinate -dimsu` for the run (the window list went empty when the screen locked while the user was away), the smart-mode rule as exits ≥ entries for 0.6 s (a `grep -c … || echo 0` doubled a zero and hung one run 240 s), the IDE build from `Contents/Resources/product-info.json`, the frame placement with retries, the output root per arm with `<theme>-<scale>x` beneath. **Result** (M0, light + dark, MacBook panel): 28 s end to end, scenes 18.3 s (workspace 2.7 / 3.2, menu 0.9, settings 1.4, search 1.3, markdown 1.0 per theme, the theme switch ≈ 1.5 s); profile: awaitQuiet 7.3 s/36, expect 7.5/19, settle 3.7/10, sample 5.5/183 (30 ms), ide 1.0/35, capture 0.9/10. Against the morning's 210 s per theme: 15×. The floor now: IDE launch to frame 4 s, smart mode 3.6 s, quiet 1.7 s, ≈ 1 s per capture (settle + capture + change detection), the shell and the documentation fetch in the workspace scene. Sticky balloons ("Automatic error reporting is now on") are cleared right before each capture. Windows: the same changes in the kit, unverified (the plain `cp -R` snapshot, `fg.ps1 -Serve`, the plugin build with `IDE_HOME`, the `--exit` attempt kept since there is no SIGTERM).
- **2026-11-02 (Mac, afternoon 2) — the IDE scene capture routine rebuilt (user: too much waiting for the IDE to settle; collapse the scenes; fixed window size; native scale; no typed searches).** Measured first: the old routine took ≈ 210 s per arm × theme on the Mac — 15 s per scene of fixed sleeps (Clear All Notifications and the navigation popup before every scene, 1.5 s popup waits, the Robot's 40 ms per key event), plus a 120 s fixed sleep on the Windows kit after indexing. **Now:** (1) a `scene-driver` plugin inside the isolated IDE (`text-quality/scene-driver/SceneService.java`, a `RestService` on the built-in server: `GET /api/scene?cmd=open|select|action|settings|search|menu|terminal|terminalExec|toolwindow|key|front|title`) executes every step on the EDT — no keystrokes typed into popups, no focus guessing (the first Robot-only rewrite lost the selection scene to the 2026.3 terminal, which keeps Escape for the shell and shows a balloon about it); the Robot only captures. Built per machine against its IDE (`build-plugin.sh`, `IDE_HOME=` on Windows), copied by the wrapper into the isolated IDE's plugins directory. (2) Five scenes instead of ten: `workspace` (project tree, editor with the selection of lines 15–18 and the quick documentation popup of `Comparable`, terminal with `ls`), `menu` (the MainMenu group as a popup under the header — the hamburger action does nothing without its button's context), `settings` (Editor › Font, configurable `editor.preferences.fonts.default`), `search` (classes tab, "Point"), `markdown`; presentation and the separate editor/selection/tree/tooltip/terminal scenes are gone. (3) The frame at a fixed 1728×980 logical pixels — the common ground of a 16-inch MacBook panel's default 2× mode and a 1080p screen under its taskbar (user) — placed through the helpers (`fgfast place` via the accessibility API, `fg.ps1 -Serve` place/bounds via SetWindowPos and DWM's extended frame bounds, device pixels) and captured as the frame's own bounds with native decorations: 3456×1960 at 2×, no menu bar, dock or taskbar. (4) The scale is the display's: `ScreenBounds --require <scale> <w> <h>` waits until some attached display runs at the scale with the room and tells the user what to set (a MacBook's "more space" modes are downsampled — not pixel-exact; 1920×1080 on a 4K for 2×); `sun.java2d.uiScale` is no longer set by `run-ide.sh`; the driver picks that display and makes its Robot for it. (5) Waits are screen-driven: `expect` (change, then quiet), `awaitQuiet`, `quiesce` at the start (unchanged for 2 s on the Mac, 4 s on Windows, max 150 s; a report of the busiest grid cells after 20 s — the first run sat 122 s on the Air plugin's status spinner under exact comparison), all tolerating two changed cells of the 48×30 grid (a caret, a spinner). Air stays enabled (user: it is part of the UX): with the tolerance the start is quiet after 4.7 s, its widget settles into a static badge, and its "New Session" tab and header button are part of every arm's frame. (6) The wrapper aborts when its launcher dies (a failed first launch once adopted the second run's IDE and two drivers typed into it), rewrites every SDK path in the seed's `jdk.table.xml` (58 jrt roots, not only the home path — else red symbols and a "Project JDK is misconfigured" banner, seen by the user), finds the IDE app whatever the Toolbox renamed it to (EAP → Release Candidate on 2026-11-02), reports a failed driver, kills the IDE hard (the state is discarded anyway). **Result** (M0 light 2×, MacBook panel, five runs while fixing): the final run 36 s end to end (IDE start to dumb-mode exit ≈ 16 s, quiet after 4.7 s, five scenes in 22 s: workspace 5.7, menu 2.2, settings 3.2, search 3.1, markdown 2.6) against ≈ 210 s — 6×; the contact sheet shows every scene as intended (file header with the CJK/ligature lines in view, selection 15–18, the `Comparable` popup, the terminal's `ls`, the main menu popup, Editor › Font with its sample text, the class search, the Markdown preview), no SDK banner, no red symbols, no pointer. Fixed on the way: the Font page's id is not registered (`settings&class=…AppEditorFontConfigurable`), the hamburger action needs its button (the plugin pops the `MainMenu` group), `terminalExec`'s parameter clashed with `cmd`, the frame placement needs retries right after indexing (a run 13 s in found no window). Not yet verified on Windows (the `fg.ps1` changes and `build-plugin.sh` with `IDE_HOME`: the VM's first task). Pitfalls met: editing a bash script while it runs breaks the running instance (the teardown of one run); macOS notifications of the user's own calendar land in the captures (a Focus mode during rounds); the user's own windows coming to the front mid-run are captured too (the foreground check is before the capture, not atomic). Old captures (ten scenes, full usable area) and the new ones are not comparable; the catalog's scenes pages need a full re-capture on both platforms with the new routine.
- **2026-11-02 (Mac, night) — the text settings redesigned and implemented (§22 fourth form; user: "delete the emulations, rename and split the properties with aliases, fold the two hooks into one", and "I explicitly do _not_ want to align the correction curves").** Red test first: `SkiaTextSettingsTest` (13 run lines: the defaults, each new property, each retired spelling and a new one overriding an old one; red because `SKEnvironment.textHinting()/textDarkening()/textBlend()` did not exist). **What changed.** `SKEnvironment`: `sun.java2d.skia.text.{strike,shaping,paragraphs}=java2d|skia` (a skia shaping or paragraphs value implies the skia strike), `text.hinting=system|none|slight|normal|full`, `text.darkening=system|none`, `text.blend=system|plain|corrected`, `text.lcdOrder` as an alias of `lcdOrder`; retired spellings resolve (`text=skia|shaper|paragraph`; `text.gray=cg|platform` → blend system, `coverage` → corrected + darkening none, `platformcoverage` → plain + darkening none, `smoothed`/`darkened` → read as coverage; `text.smoothing=true` → darkening system), a new spelling wins. `textSmoothing()` and the raster pipe's flag bit 25 are gone. `SKFontScaler`: `BLEND_CORRECTED/PLAIN/SYSTEM` replace the five `GRAY_*` modes; `createContextNative(…, blend, darkening, hinting, luminance)`; the smoothed (sub-pixel render, channels averaged) and darkened (stroke-and-fill by 1.3 % em) emulations deleted from `SKFontScaler.cpp`. Natives: `SKPlatform_TextDefaults()` → `{hinting, darkening, lcdOrderBGR}` read once per process (macOS: kNone, `AppleFontSmoothing` as AppKit reads it, false; Windows: kNormal, true, `SPI_GETFONTSMOOTHINGORIENTATION`) replaces `SKPlatform_FontSmoothingEnabled()`, `SKPlatform_LcdOrderIsBGR()` and the VM's no-argument `SKPlatform_FontHinting()`; `SKPlatform_FontHinting(request, darkening)` (macOS: darkening decides, kNormal/kNone; Windows: the request, −1 = kNormal); `SKPlatform_SmoothedGlyphMask(…, darkening, …)` sets `CGContextSetShouldSmoothFonts` from the dimension; `SKFontScaler_BlendMode()` and a process-wide `SKFontScaler_TextDarkening()` for the luminance typeface; the three `if (smoothing) setHinting(kNormal)` blocks in `SKBackend.cpp` gone. `FontUtilities.platformRoute = blend == SYSTEM`, one luminance class on macOS when darkening is off, `plainTextBlend` → LCD contrast default 100 under blend plain. **A finding on the way:** with darkening on the context's own SkFont, the strike's glyph images under blend corrected (`SkiaTextPathTest`'s reference) came out 45 % heavier than coverage — Skia's CoreText host renders A8 at kNormal from an LCD pass through its luminance pre-blend for the strike's white paint: the retired smoothed emulation by another door. Rule, now in code (`textPathFont`): the strike's own glyph images take no darkening from the host — their masks are coverage, or the platform engine's through `SKPlatform_SmoothedGlyphMask`, which takes the darkening itself; only Skia's text path's font (strike skia: `SKFontScaler_Font`, `SKFontScaler_ResolveGlyph`) carries it. **The default moved:** blend was `coverage`, is `system` (the design's `system` everywhere). Two run lines pinned to their intent: `LuminanceClassCountTest`'s non-cg line (`blend=corrected`) and `SubpixelVariantTieTest`'s Skia-scaler line (`blend=corrected`: under blend system the variants follow CoreGraphics' placement grid, which floors, `PlatformSmoothingSampleGridTest`'s rule, not DirectWrite's half-up). 20 tests, the kit's bench and IDE scripts and `HANDOVER.md`'s property table now use the new spellings; the catalog's four emulation arms (`T2-skia-darkened`, `T3-skia-smoothed`, `skia-scaler-darkened`, `skia-scaler-smoothed`) are listed as retired in `descriptions.json` (not republished yet). **Results** (macOS, `macosx-aarch64-skia`): `SkiaTextSettingsTest` 13/13 green; `FontSmoothingPreferenceTest` unchanged (jdk 138320 → 119293 at 0; cg route 138343 → 119293 with 1 class; Skia's text path 134166 → 95086); `SkiaTextPathTest` coverage 0.79, darkening 1.13 (system font) / 1.14 (file font) of the glyph images' coverage — the bound widened 1.15 → 1.25 (the old comment's 1.0–1.05 was never the measurement); `SkiaTextPathQueuedTest` now picks its counter from the new properties (12000 glyphs / 300 paragraphs, 2 flushes); TextRender directory + every `SkiaRaster*` test: 43 of 44 pass, the one failure `GlyphAtlasEvictionTest` is the open Metal atlas eviction race (§13, not this change). Commit on the shared branch, pushed to origin and the fork. Not done: republishing the catalog without the four arms; the Windows VM's verification of the DirectWrite host under the new defaults.
- **2026-11-02 (Mac, evening) — the font smoothing preference honoured (`2958023cd952`; user: "implement it as a fix for both the new routes and the existing ones").** Red test first: `FontSmoothingPreferenceTest` (macOS; child JVMs per route with `-AppleFontSmoothing` unset / 0 / 2 in their argument domain): every route rendered the same ink under all three. **The fix:** `SKPlatform_FontSmoothingEnabled()` (macOS: `NSUserDefaults` `AppleFontSmoothing`, unset or non-zero on, 0 off — AppKit's reading; Windows: true), surfaced as `SKEnvironment.platformFontSmoothing()`; `SKPlatform_SmoothedGlyphMask` sets `CGContextSetShouldSmoothFonts` from it instead of forcing true; `SKEnvironment.textSmoothing()` (Skia's text path requesting `kNormal` hinting) is gated by it; `FontUtilities` collapses `textLuminanceClasses` to 1 and `textLuminanceDependent` to false on macOS with smoothing off (user's choice); and `CGGlyphImages.m` — today's shipped pipeline — sets the switch after each `JRSFontSetRenderingStyleOnContext` (`CGGI_FontSmoothingEnabled`, read once as AppKit does). **Green:** JDK CStrike 138,320 → 119,293 at 0 (exactly CoreGraphics' unsmoothed coverage, the Skia coverage figure), the cg route the same with **one** luminance class, Skia's text path with the smoothing request 134,166 → 95,086 (its plain route's figure); `=2` renders as unset on all three. The TextRender directory: 32 passed, `GlyphAtlasEvictionTest` failed once and passed on rerun (the known §13.5 flake). For users with the preference unset nothing changes; users who turned smoothing off get AppKit's rendering from every Java text route for the first time. Windows unchanged (true). Still not read: a change of the preference while the process runs (AppKit re-reads on its notification; we read once per process).
- **2026-11-02 (Mac, afternoon) — font smoothing on macOS: who applies it, and whether `AppleFontSmoothing` is honoured (user's question from a VM finding).** Probe `build/text-reference/probes/smoothprobe.swift` (AppKit bitmap-rep context, an offscreen `NSTextField`, a plain CoreGraphics ARGB bitmap context as the JDK's glyph canvas starts out, contexts with smoothing forced on / off, the private `CGContextSet/GetFontSmoothingStyle` and `CGContextGetAllowsFontSmoothing` by `dlsym`) and `build/skia-smoke/SmoothInk.java` (the JDK's CStrike path, the Skia scaler's coverage and cg routes, Skia's text path plain / with `text.smoothing` / cg), one line of Helvetica Neue 13 pt black on white, ink = Σ(255 − gray); `AppleFontSmoothing` set per process through the argument domain (`-AppleFontSmoothing N`; the system default stays untouched — an attempt to write it was declined by the harness). **Findings (macOS 26, this Mac):** (1) a CoreGraphics bitmap context has smoothing **on by default** whatever the setting (`shouldSmoothFonts` true, `allowsFontSmoothing` true, style 48), so everything rendering through plain CG (the JDK's `CGGlyphImages`, our `SKPlatform_SmoothedGlyphMask`, Skia's CoreText host once it asks) smooths regardless of the user's preference; (2) **AppKit honours `AppleFontSmoothing = 0`**: its bitmap-rep context's ink drops from 138,392 to 119,322 — *exactly* the forced-off CG rendering — and the text field by the same share at 2×, while the context's `shouldSmoothFonts` and `allowsFontSmoothing` still read true: AppKit turns it off per draw, not through the context switches; (3) **the levels 1, 2, 3 are dead**: AppKit's ink is identical for unset / 1 / 2 / 3, and the private style setter ignores 1–3 (style stays 48; 16 is accepted and changes nothing) — on current macOS the preference is on / off, the historic light / medium / strong strengths no longer render differently; (4) the Java paths under the same argument domain: JDK CStrike 138,320 ≈ CG smoothed, Skia scaler cg 138,343 and Skia text path cg 138,344 (the platform route *is* the JDK's rendering), Skia scaler coverage 119,293 ≈ CG unsmoothed, Skia text path plain 95,086 (Skia's own gamma on unsmoothed coverage, −31 %), Skia text path with `text.smoothing` 134,166 (Skia's hinting normal makes its host smooth, then Skia's gamma, −3 %) — **none of them moves with the setting**: the JDK ignores it (JRS / CG defaults), our cg route forces it on, Skia's host decides by probing CG (always "smoothing available" here). **Judgment:** the cg route needs no emulation — it is CoreGraphics' genuine smoothing — but like today's JDK it does not respect the user's choice; the fix is one switch, `CGContextSetShouldSmoothFonts(cg, enabled)` with `enabled = AppleFontSmoothing != 0` read as AppKit reads it (`NSUserDefaults`, which also makes it testable per process), and with smoothing off the luminance classes collapse to one (unsmoothed CG output is plain coverage). The same switch belongs in `CGGlyphImages.m` for today's pipeline (a behaviour change for users who turned smoothing off: their text would finally match AppKit's). Skia's own text path should request `kNormal` hinting only when the setting allows. The `smoothed` and `darkened` gray modes stay what they were: emulations for comparison, not candidates to ship. Artifacts: `build/notes/smoothing-2026-11-02/` (one PNG per context and setting). **Decision (user): analysis only for now; when the switch is implemented, the cg route's luminance classes collapse to one while smoothing is off.** Recorded as an open item in §13.2.

- **2026-10-29 (Mac, midday) — host items 2, 3 and 4 before the Windows round (`d166dd1447e2`): the jtreg sweep, the INT_ARGB mirror kept, the JNI census.** **Item 2, the sweep:** the headless Java2D suites (`java/awt/image`, `java/awt/Graphics2D`, `java/awt/geom`, `java/awt/font`, `sun/java2d`; `-k:!headful`, headless, `-conc:4`) with the raster flag on against off, 334 tests each: 287 / 292 passed, the rest the same headless failures in both arms; **five flag-specific failures, three of them bugs, fixed test-first in `SkiaRasterNonAATest` and verified on the JDK tests:** (1) `DrawRoundRect0Bug` — the loops' `drawOval / drawRoundRect / drawArc` go through `sg2d.shapepipe.draw` even for a thin non-AA stroke, and the raster converter stroked them with Marlin's outline instead of the loops' one-pixel `DrawPath` (a zero-width round rectangle vanished; `LoopPipe.draw`'s thin branch is now the pipe's too); (2) `CrashNaNTest` — Skia draws nothing of a path with a NaN or infinite point where Marlin skips the point (`PathData.of` applies Marlin's rule: a move to such a point ends the subpath, a line to one is dropped, a curve with such a control point becomes a line, the first usable point after a dropped move starts the subpath); (3) `StrokedLinePerf` — a dashed line billions of pixels long had its outline built by `createStrokedShape` unclipped (OutOfMemoryError); Java outlines now come from `RenderingEngine.strokeTo(shape, at, clip, …)` into a path collector, as `LoopPipe.getStrokeSpans`, 69 ms. Zero-length segments are dropped before Skia's stroker, as Marlin drops them, which removed half of (4) `JoinMiterRedundantLineSegmentsTest`'s differences; what remains (two pixels at 3–4 levels, paths with a redundant collinear segment) is Skia's stroker shaping a join Marlin ignores — **known difference Q26**; (5) `TestCreateStrokedShapeJoins` counts the non-blank pixels of AA fills against Marlin-tuned constants, 4792 vs 4790 — the coverage model (Q11), **known**. **Item 3, D10 resolved — the mirror kept between operations:** `SKRasterSurfaceData.trackWrites` wraps the buffered image's native `Lock` (`BufImgSDOps.sdOps.Lock`, the same function for every buffered image) so every `SD_LOCK_WRITE` by any pipe on any thread bumps a global serial; a thread's `RasterTarget` remembers the serial its own last lock produced and the mirror is current while the serial has not moved; Java-side writes (`setRGB`, the raster's setters) are caught through the state tracker — **but `SunGraphics2D.markDirty` invalidates that same tracker before every operation**, so the tracker is read inside `markDirty` (stale there = Java wrote since the last operation) and renewed; `SurfaceData.markDirty` lost its `final` for that, the one shared-code touch; a stolen array (untrackable buffer) keeps the mirror off. Imports are 16-px tiles on first touch; the colour-only rectangle store writes the mirror as well. The first attempt (tracker read in the pipe) kept nothing and made INT_ARGB 4–7× *slower* (every operation re-imported whole 64-px tiles) — the fixed-cost test caught it. `SkiaRasterMirrorTest`: a `setRGB`, the loops' thin line, a copy blit, a raster store on another thread and the store on this thread are each seen by the next antialiased fill. **Numbers (INT_ARGB, loops → raster): AA rects ratio 1.9 → 1.11** (limit 1.5 now), non-AA integer rects 0.99; bench rows round-rects 11.0 → 7.8 ms, ovals 21.9 → 12.2, gradient round-rects 18.9 → 14.3, stroked round-rects 12.4 → 11.0; the mirror's *out* conversion per operation remains (Java memory must be current for any reader), which is why INT_ARGB non-AA curves (3.7 → 6.1) and non-AA strokes (3.3 → 8.9) stay behind the loops where the rasterizer itself gains nothing. Accepted gap: two threads drawing the same image concurrently (a data race under Java2D's contract anyway). **Item 4, the JNI census:** §16. **Item 5, the Windows hand-over (`244f32e7ff26`):** `build/notes/handover-vm-2026-10-29.md`, copied into the kit's `HANDOVER.md` as §11 — the nine raster tests with the LCD sub-pixel order check as the Windows-specific part, the five JDK tests the sweep flagged, the sweep on Windows, the metrics census still owed from 2026-10-28, the bench and RenderPerfTest software vs raster, the IDE with the GPU off and with the Skia text route, the round's pitfalls (14 slots, 16-bit points, `markDirty`). The host has done what it can; the next step is the VM / x64 round.
- **2026-10-29 (Mac, morning) — host work before the Windows round, item 1 of 5: composite strikes on Skia's text path (`24fbe9a05076`; user: "all five, start with the composite strikes").** The question first (user): the Windows window-surface slice is mostly verification — Swing's back buffer on Windows without Direct3D is a BufferedImage through `BufImgSurfaceData.createData`, the raster surface's own hook, so the slices already apply there; macOS has only the Metal and OpenGL configurations, so the GPU-off case cannot run on this host. Agreed order: composite strikes → a jtreg regression sweep under the raster flag → D10 (the INT_ARGB mirror) → the JNI slot census of the other backends → the Windows hand-over. **Composite strikes:** every logical font on Windows and Linux, every Swing UI font (`FontUtilities.getCompositeFontUIResource`) and every glyph vector of a system font on macOS carries a `CompositeStrike`; neither pipe's Skia route drew them. Red tests: `SkiaRasterTextTest` now requires every case to count its glyphs (the glyph-vector case counted 0), `SkiaTextPathTest` draws each font as a glyph vector too (the GPU pipe: 0). **The design:** `SKGlyphContexts.of(GlyphList)` → the scaler contexts by slot (each slot's strike must be on the Skia scaler, `drawsBySkia`, else the list stays with the glyph images) and the codes re-encoded `slot << 24 | glyph` (JBR's composite encoding keeps the slot in the *low* bits with a per-font shift, so the native cannot decode it; `CompositeStrike` gained `numSlots / strikeForSlot / slotOf / glyphOf`); the shared native loop selects the context by the slot, skips the invisible glyph (0xffff) keeping its advance, and flushes runs on typeface changes as before; the GPU opcode carries the slot table ahead of the images (`ensureCapacityAndAlignment(total, 24)`), the direct and raster entries take a `long[]` (13 slots). **Two more findings on the way:** (1) `GlyphList.setFromGlyphVector` never filled the glyph codes (only images and positions) — a pipe drawing from codes saw zeros; it copies them now (`getGlyphCodes(0, len, glyphData)`); (2) the raster text pipe decides on the *built* list for strings and chars too (a composite strike's slots are only known then), handing the loops the whole call otherwise. **Result:** raster: glyph vectors 40 of 40 glyphs on Skia, ink 0.787 (cg route 1.000), position identical; GPU: both fonts as glyph vectors at 0.79 / 1.13 (smoothing) — the string cases' numbers. **Bench** (400 strings, 13 pt, 1200 × 900 INT_ARGB_PRE): raster Skia text 2.9 ms against loops + Skia scaler 5.8 (2.0×; was 1.7× before the handler fix), LCD-hinted 2.7 vs 3.1. Still outside Skia's route: per-glyph transforms (one strike per transform), rotated glyph transforms, strikes off the Skia scaler.
- **2026-10-29 (Mac, small hours) — fixed cost round 2 (`2db2e5a7babd`; user: "go ahead with fixed cost round 2"): parity on the loops' cheapest call, and a finding that applies to every JNI entry in the project.** Red test first: `SkiaRasterFixedCostTest` gained a non-AA integer `fillRect` round (limits 1.25 on INT_ARGB_PRE, 1.5 on INT_ARGB): ratio 3.33 before. **Three causes.** (1) *A colour-only rectangle entry* (`fillRectColor`): the box is the rectangle's pixels inside a rectangular clip, drawn without save / clipIRect / restore (`Operation::kBox`), and for a non-AA opaque SrcOver or any Src fill a plain store into Java's own pixel layout (`kDirect`: no canvas, no mirror — the loops' `FillRect` is exactly that): 1.47 → 0.85 ms per 2,000 rects, AA rects on INT_ARGB_PRE 2.10 → 1.55 (now 0.7× the loops). (2) *The converter*: `PixelToParallelogramConverter.fillRect` transforms through doubles and normalizes before the pipe sees the rectangle, where the loops go `LoopPipe.fillRect → FillRect` with integers; `SKRasterFillPipe` takes an integer `fillRect` under an integer translation straight to the entry (the converters became static while at it): a stage probe at the JNI entry (`J2D_SKIA_RASTER_PROBE=0`) then read 0.23 µs — the loops' *entire* operation — against lock 0.055 and store 0.065 on top, so the remaining 0.15 µs sat between Java and the native, invisible to the probe. (3) **The sampler found it: `InterpreterRuntime::slow_signature_handler` on every call.** HotSpot generates a fast JNI signature handler only for natives with at most **14 parameter slots** (`Fingerprinter::fp_max_size_of_parameters` = (64 − 5) / 4; a `long` or `double` counts two; the receiver or class does not); above that every invocation walks the signature in the interpreter's slow handler, ≈ 0.1–0.15 µs. The raster natives had 15 (`fillRectColor`) to 24 (`strokePath`, `drawImage`) slots — **every raster operation of slices 1–7 paid it**, and the loops' six-argument `FillRect` never did. Now every entry is ≤ 14: the composite rule, antialiasing, winding rule, caps and joins packed in one int, device points as two 16-bit halves of an int (surfaces over 65535 px a side stay with the loops), the pen and the extra alpha appended to the transform arrays, the glyph count with its flags, and the image's size read natively from `BufImgSDOps.rasbounds` (`libawt/awt/image`, `cvutils` and `common/awt` added to the Skia library's header dirs) instead of travelling. **Result:** non-AA integer rects **0.22 µs against the loops' 0.25** (ratios 0.92–1.07 on both formats over three runs), AA rects 0.63–0.72× on INT_ARGB_PRE and 1.8–1.9× on INT_ARGB (the mirror), and the whole bench moved (loops → raster, INT_ARGB_PRE, ms): round-rects 9.7 → 5.0, ovals 20.0 → 8.3, blobs 13.0 → 5.2, stroked round-rects 11.1 → 7.3, gradient round-rects 18.1 → 11.0, radial ovals 15.8 → 14.1, icons 8.9 → 7.6, rotated images 13.5 → 8.5, bicubic 43 → 56, non-AA ovals 3.6 → 3.7, non-AA round-rects 2.7 → 3.5, non-AA stroked round-rects 4.4 → 6.7. One run's INT_ARGB AA round at 2.9 was noise (1.79 and 1.92 on reruns). Left in the non-AA rows: Marlin's outline plus flattening plus Skia for non-AA strokes (0.66×), and the span-rule round-rects (0.77×) — Skia's non-AA scan converter is not faster than ProcessPath; those rows are now fixed-cost-free and show the rasterizers alone. **Rule recorded (§13.8, memory):** a JNI native in this project takes at most 14 parameter slots; the GPU pipe's own direct entries (`drawGlyphsBySkia` 11, `drawShapedBySkia`, `drawParagraphBySkia`) are under it, the queue opcodes are not JNI calls — but the Metal and Vulkan backends' natives deserve the same census (§14).

(2) *ProcessPath* (an opaque colour, rectangular clip, thin stroke state): `+0.5` on both axes (`MDP_HALF_MULT`): the pixel's top-left corner is the sample point, i.e. the shape shifted half a pixel down and right under centre sampling; a polygon's top sliver that Skia fills on row 10 (7 pixels) the loops do not. Unshifted under STROKE_PURE. (3) *The span iterator* (translucent, paints, shape clips, any fill under a wide stroke state, every stroke outline): end points snapped to the nearest quarter (`floor(v + 0.25) + 0.25`, not `floor(v) + 0.25` — the shared `NormalizingIterator` now takes the position and applies Marlin's two formulas), **and curves flattened at a one-pixel chord tolerance (`MAX_FLAT_SQ 1.0`)**: the loops' non-AA ovals are inscribed polygons visibly inside the true curve (53 Skia-only pixels around a 70 × 50 oval, 16 % of its edge). The pipe normalizes and flattens the same way (`FlatteningPathIterator`, flatness 1, limit 10) before Skia. (4) *Ties*: a pixel centre exactly on an edge is the loops' on the left/top side (`ceil(x − 0.5)`), Skia's on the right/bottom (`floor(x + 0.5)`); a nudge of 1/64 px (Skia's edge precision) up and left resolves them the loops' way, except on edges shallower than 1:64 whose crossings the nudge moves (the concave polygon's 1:14 top edge: 11 of 914 edge pixels). (5) *Strokes*: Skia's stroker non-AA differs from Marlin's by whole pixels (142 of 856 edge pixels on the oval stroke); non-AA strokes now take Marlin's outline through the span rule: 7. **Result:** rectangles, rotated rectangles, mitered strokes, thin lines exact; polygon 11 ties, ovals 4, round rectangle 1, cubic blob 15, oval stroke 7, translucent oval 4 (limits: 2 % of edge pixels for straight shapes, 6 % for curves). **Speed (`RasterShapesBench`, four non-AA rows, 1200 × 900 INT_ARGB_PRE, loops → raster): ovals 4.4 → 4.3 ms, round-rects 3.3 → 3.9, integer rect fills 0.43 → 1.50 (0.29×), stroked round-rects 3.3 → 6.9 (0.47×).** Non-AA is where the loops are cheapest (a `fillRect` is 0.2 µs in the loops, our operation ≈ 0.75 µs of lock, clip set-up and JNI) and Skia's non-AA scan converter is no faster than ProcessPath; the stroke row pays Marlin's outline plus the flattening plus Skia. This slice is complete in coverage and a loss in speed for small shapes: **fixed cost round 2** is the next item (skip the save/clip/restore when the clip is the surface, a rectangle-only entry without the descriptor arrays, the lock amortized across a Graphics' operations), with the loops' 0.2 µs as the target; until then the loops remain the better software pipeline for non-AA rectangles. Quirks recorded: Q24 (ProcessPath's half pixel), Q25 (the span iterator's one-pixel flattening) in §18.

The VM also reported that our shared tip had never reached the fork it pulls from.
- **2026-10-26 (Mac, later) — glyph caches: the 32-pixel cell is the cliff on 2× displays (user: "shouldn't we do something about those sizes right now?").** Instrument first: `SKEnvironment.glyphCacheStats()` (cached / staged / evicted / atlas uploads / uncached, `d48353981e6d`, `SkiaGlyphCacheStatsTest`), printed per frame by `TextCallBench`. **Finding:** both pipes' glyph caches have 32 × 32 cells (`MTLTR_CACHE_CELL_*`, `SKGlyphCache` `CELL_SIZE`, the shared `AccelGlyphCache_AddGlyph` rejects anything larger); a glyph image taller or wider goes through the no-cache path *every frame* — Metal: a texture `replaceRegion` per 32-px tile and a draw per glyph (≈ 10 µs per glyph, 1,400 ms for a frame of 129k glyphs); the Skia pipe: `uploadPixmap` + `drawImage` per glyph with the batch flushed before and after (≈ 2.9 µs). Census (`GlyphSizeCensus`, pixel bounds): at 1× no IDE font/size up to 24 pt exceeds 32 px; **at 2× the cliff is 16 pt: 18 pt 15 %, 20 pt 20 %, 24 pt 75 % of glyphs exceed** (Inter, JetBrains Mono, Helvetica Neue, the system font alike); typical 13 pt glyphs at 2× are 19–21 px tall in a 32-px cell (≈ 25 % of the cell used). On this display (device scale 2): 400 × 12 strings at 13 pt M0 1.0 / T0 1.2 ms, **at 20 pt 7.3 / 13.9 ms** (RenderPerfTest's `LargeTextGray` at 32 pt is this path on both pipes; the editor zoomed past 16 pt on a 2× display is the IDE case). **Bench correction:** a compatible VolatileImage's graphics carries the display's default transform, so `TextCallBench`'s earlier "1×" rows were 2× and "scale=2" was 4×; the tool now prints the device scale and the glyph pixel size (the conclusions of §19b.1 stand: all arms were measured at the same scale). **Low-hanging fruit in `SKGlyphCache` itself (dense text, 26-px glyphs, all cached): none that matters** — the flusher spends ≈ 0.8 ms of a 12 ms frame in the glyph loop (`SKQuadBatch::push` vector inserts, `SkVertices::MakeCopy` bounds, `cellsPerRow()` calls), the rest of T0's +31 % over M0 is behind the GPU wait and needs a GPU timing instrument (`MTLCommandBuffer` GPU start/end through the platform hook) before anything is changed. **Options for the cell:** (a) cell = 32 × device scale (64 at 2×): simple, holds glyphs up to ≈ 32 pt at 2×, but a 2048² texture then holds 1,024 cells and a 26-px glyph uses 16 % of its cell; (b) **size buckets** — caches of 32 / 64 / 128-px cells chosen by max(width, height) (and 16 px for 1× UI text), each its own texture sized for its share; rare batch breaks since a run's glyphs share a bucket; glyphs above the largest bucket keep the no-cache path; (c) variable-size shelf/skyline packing with per-shelf heights (Skia's `RectanizerSkyline`): best utilisation and no classes, but a new eviction policy and the largest change, and `AccelGlyphCache.c` is shared with OpenGL/D3D. Recommendation: (b) for both `SKGlyphCache` (own code) and `MTLTextRenderer` (two more `MTLGlyphCache` instances), test-first with the stats; (c) later if the IDE measurements show utilisation matters. **Done the same day (user: "Let's do buckets now!"):** both pipes have buckets with cells of 16 / 32 / 64 / 128 px, one cache per bucket and kind, created on first use; the configured side is the 32-px bucket's, the 16-px bucket half of it, the larger buckets at least 256 cells (Metal's full-cache policy recreates the texture, and 64 cells thrashed at 20 pt). **Skia pipe** (`SKGlyphCache` commit "glyph caches in size buckets"): per-glyph bucket, the buckets' batches open side by side through a list (glyphs of one paint commute under the blends used). **Metal** (commit "Metal: glyph caches in size buckets"): one bucket per glyph list, its largest glyph's — a bucket switch rebinds the vertex cache's texture and costs a draw, and per-glyph buckets split 24-px words between the 16- and 32-px cells (TextGray 4.2 → 8.5 ms) before the per-list rule (4.6). **Results, device scale 2, 400 × 12 strings:** 13 / 20 / 32 pt — Metal 1.05 / 1.15 / 1.17 ms (was 1.0 / 7.3 / 25), Skia pipe 1.08 / 1.11 / 1.08 (was 1.2 / 14 / 24); RenderPerfTest `LargeTextGray` Metal 1.78 → 0.99, Skia 1.0 → 0.85; 60 pt+ at 2× (120-px glyphs) is `OutlineTextRenderer`'s path on both pipes, untouched. Tests: `SkiaGlyphCacheBucketsTest`, `MetalGlyphCacheBucketsTest` (+ `MTLGraphicsConfig.glyphsDrawnUncached()`), TextRender directory green on both. **Open:** utilisation of the 32-px bucket for 1× UI text is unchanged on Metal (per-list rule puts a line's small glyphs in the line's bucket); shelf packing (option c) stays the later step if the IDE cache-efficiency measurement asks for it.

### 13.8 Skia raster surface (chunk 1 of the long tail; opened 2026-10-28)
- **rule (amended 2026-10-29)** Everything entering `SKRaster.cpp` is declared `kN32` over Java's BGRA ints to reach Skia's hand-written blitters (any other declared order silently takes the generic raster-pipeline blitter, 9× slower per small draw). N32 is RGBA on the macOS and Linux Skia builds and **BGRA on the Windows build** (`SK_R32_SHIFT 16`): `kSwizzle` decides — on RGBA builds colours, gradient stops, image pixels and the LCD geometry are swizzled (R ↔ B, `kBGR_H`); on BGRA builds Java's ints are Skia's pixels and the geometry is `kRGB_H`. Verified both ways by the raster tests (the VM's red step exchanged red and blue everywhere).
- **resolved 2026-10-29** INT_ARGB's mirror is kept between operations (write-lock serial + the state tracker read in `markDirty`; D10): small AA fills at 1.1× the loops. Left: the out-conversion of the touched box per operation (≈ 0.65 ns/px; NEON would halve it), which keeps INT_ARGB non-AA curves and strokes behind the loops.
- **later** The per-thread canvas cache holds up to four targets per thread, and an INT_ARGB target owns a whole-image mirror (4.3 MB at 1200 × 900; 32 MB at 4K): many threads × large images is unbounded. Cap the mirror memory (release the mirror of a target not used for N operations / bytes), or hang the mirror on the surface's ops instead of the thread.
- **finding 2026-10-28 (user: "is the half pixel structural?")** It is, and it is Skia's curve stroking, not normalization: the shared `NormalizingIterator` reproduces Marlin's rules exactly (axis-aligned thin and 3-px lines match the loops within 1 level; the control-point adjustments compared line by line), straight diagonals agree within 13 levels, but on a cubic Skia's offset curve runs ≈ 0.3 px outside the exact offset at the tightest bend (worst pixel 1.76 px from the curve: exact coverage ≈ 0.24, Marlin 0.35, Skia 0.56). `SkPathStroker` approximates cubic offsets with quads under a speed-tuned tolerance. **Splitting each cubic into 8 pieces before stroking (`sun.java2d.skia.strokeSubdivide=3`) brings it into the coverage-model range: cubic 93 → 48 levels, >32-level pixels 81 → 23; circle 75 → 43** (2 and 4 pieces barely help). Applies to the GPU pipe's strokes too (same stroker), where the recorded ≤ 50 levels at 2× has the same origin.
- **open** Stroke accuracy reference: compute exact offset-curve coverage for the test shapes (dense sampling → high-resolution fill) to attribute the remaining 45–75 levels between Skia's and Marlin's strokers (Skia converges to Marlin on the cubic when split, Marlin is the one off on the ellipse); then the default `strokeBend` and the triage class.
- **open** Thin (1-px) strokes of curves: Marlin's carries ≈ 1.3 px of ink per column at the top of a circle (46 / 235 / 49), Skia's 1.0 (1 / 254 / 0) — 14 % more ink on the loops' side, unchanged by subdivision; straight thin lines agree. Which stroker is right needs an exact-area check of Marlin's thin-stroke widening; until then the stroke test's Skia-mode limit stays at 112.
- **knob** `J2D_SKIA_RASTER_PROBE=1|2|3` (lock only / + clip / everything) stays as a measurement knob, like `J2D_SKIA_STATS`.
- **resolved 2026-10-28** Dirty tracking: `SunGraphics2D` calls `surfaceData.markDirty()` in every draw/fill before the pipe runs, so writes through the raster pipe are seen by the GPU pipes' texture caches exactly like the loops' writes.
- **scope** The raster pipes take AA and non-AA shapes with a solid colour or a gradient paint and a Porter-Duff composite (Skia's stroker for undashed AA strokes, Marlin's outline for dashed and non-AA ones; thin non-AA strokes stay Bresenham), transformed draws of INT_ARGB / INT_ARGB_PRE / INT_RGB BufferedImages (`SKRasterDrawImage`; copies stay the loops' blits), and with `sun.java2d.skia.text=skia` text whose strike is on the Skia scaler (`SKRasterTextPipe`); XOR, custom `Composite`, custom `Paint`, texture paints, thin non-AA lines, other image formats, background-colour image draws, composite strikes and rotated text are the loops' (slices to come: fixed cost round 2 → the Windows window surface with the GPU off; texture paints; composite strikes).
- **resolved 2026-10-29 (fixed cost round 2)** Non-AA integer `fillRect` at parity with the loops (0.22 vs 0.25 µs): the colour-only rectangle entry without clip state and with a direct store, `SKRasterFillPipe` past the converter, and every native under HotSpot's 14-slot fast-signature-handler limit (the hidden 0.1–0.15 µs per call on *all* raster operations).
- **rule (JNI)** A native method takes at most 14 parameter slots (a `long`/`double` is two; receiver or class not counted), or HotSpot runs the interpreter's slow signature handler on every call (`Fingerprinter::fp_max_size_of_parameters`). Pack small values; read what the native can read itself (`BufImgSDOps.rasbounds` for the image's size). Device points are packed as 16-bit halves: surfaces over 65535 px a side go to the loops (`SKRasterSurfaceData.createData`).
- **resolved 2026-10-29 (text)** Composite strikes: `SKGlyphContexts` resolves a composite glyph list slot by slot for both pipes (contexts by slot, codes re-encoded with the slot in bits 24–31); glyph vectors of system fonts, logical fonts and Swing UI fonts take Skia's route. Still the glyph images': per-glyph transforms, rotated glyph transforms, slots off the Skia scaler.
- **fact (text, confirmed on Windows 2026-10-29)** The raster canvas's LCD geometry yields the loops' sub-pixel order on Windows; LCD text exists on *opaque* images only (`SurfaceData.canRenderLCDText`), every pipe draws an LCD hint on INT_ARGB(_PRE) as grayscale — `SkiaRasterTextTest`'s LCD row draws on INT_RGB. On macOS Skia's CoreText host draws LCD-hinted text as grayscale regardless.
- **open (test limit)** `SkiaRasterFixedCostTest`'s INT_ARGB round at limit 1.5 is at the median of the VM (1.54, 1.545) and the x64 machine (1.25–1.71, median ≈ 1.48): raise the limit to 2.0 on INT_ARGB or make the test's verdict the median of three, and note that INT_ARGB's remaining cost is the mirror's write-back per operation. `SkiaRasterImageTest` is one level off on one pixel on x86 (nearest, shear, translucent, INT_ARGB source): allow 1 level for nearest on an unpremultiplied source, or find the rounding.
- **resolved 2026-10-29 (sweep, Windows)** `ClipShapeTest#CubicDoDash` / `#PolyDoDash`: Marlin's clipped dasher ends dashes an antialiased pixel short of its unclipped one; the raster pipe clips while stroking only for shapes reaching > 16,384 px past the clip (`VAST_REACH`). **Open question** for Marlin: which dasher is right (a candidate for §15 if the clipped one is wrong — it is the one the loops use by default).
- **later** Bicubic image scaling is slower on Skia (0.75× the loops: `SkCubicResampler` ≈ 17 ns per output pixel against the loops' bicubic ≈ 13); bilinear and nearest are 1.1–1.4×. Measure what the IDE actually uses (icons are bilinear; `ImageUtil`'s own scaling is Java code) before routing bicubic back to the loops; a small-icon draw costs 4.4 µs on either side, per-call overhead to profile (source surface lookup, lock, canvas set-up).
- **rule (images)** Sources enter Skia premultiplied and clamped to the source rectangle, drawn as a non-antialiased rectangle (the centre rule): Skia's unpremultiplied image filtering and decal tiling both differ from the loops' transform helper (28 and 56 levels).
- **tools** `build/skia-smoke/RasterShapesBench.java` (twelve workloads: shapes, strokes, gradients, images, text; loops vs raster; compile and run with `--add-exports java.desktop/sun.java2d=ALL-UNNAMED`), `RectLoop.java` (the fixed-cost loop for the probe and `sample`), `ImageDbg.java` (pixel dumps of one image draw, both routes).

- **resolved 2026-11-02 (`2958023cd952`)** `AppleFontSmoothing` honoured by every route (the JDK's canvas, the cg route, Skia's text path's smoothing request), one luminance class when off; the preference is read once per process. Was: `AppleFontSmoothing` is not honoured by any Java text route on macOS (the JDK's `CGGlyphImages`, our cg / platform route, Skia's CoreText host): CoreGraphics bitmap contexts smooth by default, AppKit switches smoothing off per draw when the preference is 0 (1–3 render like unset on current macOS). Fix when taken up: read the preference as AppKit does (`NSUserDefaults`, so a jtreg test can pass `-AppleFontSmoothing 0` per process), `CGContextSetShouldSmoothFonts(cg, enabled)` in `SKPlatform_SmoothedGlyphMask` and in `CGGlyphImages.m`, Skia's text path requesting `kNormal` hinting only when enabled, and one luminance class while smoothing is off (user's choice). Measurements and artifacts: §12 2026-11-02 afternoon, `build/notes/smoothing-2026-11-02/`.

## 14. Design decisions to re-evaluate (cross-backend, potentially costly)
- **done 2026-10-29: the JNI signature census** — results and PR candidates in §16 (`MaskFill` AA parallelograms, `TransformHelper`, `ScaledBlit`, `BufferedMaskBlit.enqueueTile`, `SunLayoutEngine.shape` over the 14-slot limit; the Metal / Vulkan queues and the Skia GPU entries are not).
- **review round (user, 2026-10-28): which of the raster surface's fixed-cost lessons apply to the GPU pipe.** Not the blitter swizzle itself (Graphite generates a pipeline per paint and format; BGRA vs RGBA costs nothing on the GPU). To check: **(1) pixel conversion at upload** — `uploadPixmap` converts on the CPU whenever a software source's colour or alpha type differs from the texture's, through Skia's generic path (≈ 10 ns/px against 0.65 for a plain loop): INT_ARGB (unpremultiplied) sources into premultiplied textures on every platform, and on Dawn a BGRA-ints → RGBA8-texture swizzle if that is our texture format (x64 IDE profile: `uploadPixmap` 197 of 5,489 flusher samples, `SKBackend_Blit` 550); a BGRA8 texture format on Dawn and a plain premultiply loop (or premultiplied uploads cached per image) are the candidates. **(2) allocations on the flusher** — heap 13 % of the flusher's exclusive time, `new()` 593 ms inclusive, `Mesh::clear` 143 ms (x64 profiles): paints and paths built per operation, the dispatch's vectors, shaders rebuilt per gradient paint, `makeWithLocalMatrix` per transformed draw. Already shared: the stroke normalization rule; region clips are device-space (the GPU pipe clips with paths, so no bug there).

Decisions that were right for the prototype but trade something away; each should be re-measured when the measurement in the last column is available, and compared with how Metal/Vulkan/software do it.

| # | Decision (date, §) | What it costs or risks | Alternative(s) | Re-evaluate when / how |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | **All non-AA geometry is meshes**, never `drawRect`/`drawPath`/`drawImageRect` (2026-10-14, §10, §11.13) | Bypasses Graphite's analytic renderers and any batching Graphite does for rects/images; one `drawVertices` per blit or parallelogram (~2 µs CPU each, same as any Graphite draw). Fractional UI scales (125/150 % on Windows) do not change the cost — the rule applies at 100 % too; they change what would happen *without* it: at fractional scales nothing is pixel-aligned in device space, so Graphite's own primitives would anti-alias every non-AA rect, line and image (blur, not a slowdown). | (a) Graphite `drawEdgeAAQuad`/`drawEdgeAAImageSet` with `aaFlags = kNone` (per-edge AA control, experimental API); (b) accept Graphite's AA on non-AA primitives (visual change product-wide, what Skiko-based UIs live with); (c) upstream feature request for honouring non-AA on paths/images. | RenderPerfTest `Image*`/`FlatBox`/`ClipRotated*` at 100 % vs 150 % `uiScale` on Skia vs Metal; IDE scenes at 125/150 % on Windows (WP 2.2) — blits and rect-heavy scenes are where the mesh path dominates. **Measured 2026-10-14 (§11.7b): no scale-dependent cost, 0.95× at 1.0 vs 0.94× at 1.5.** Alternative (a) measured (§11.14): same speed, pixels not identical, experimental API — knob `sun.java2d.skia.blitQuads=edgeAA` kept for Windows/Dawn. |
| D2 | **Graphite internal MSAA off** (`fInternalMultisampleCount = k1`) (2026-10-14, §10) | AA paths go through Graphite's path atlas (CPU raster atlas or compute atlas) instead of the MSAA stencil/cover renderers: more CPU per AA path, atlas texture memory and uploads. Only AA parallelograms use it in variant 1; **variant 2 (Skia rasterizing rrects/paths) and SVG icons would be fully exposed.** Chrome runs Graphite with MSAA on; we deviate from the configuration Skia tests most. Upside: no multisampled render targets (bandwidth/memory, relevant on iGPUs and tiles), and 8-bit coverage instead of 4 levels. | (a) MSAA on + render non-AA geometry in separate passes (flush around it — kills batching); (b) MSAA on + accept 4-level edges on non-AA geometry drawn in the same pass as a path (what the matrix caught as 10 suspects); (c) `fInternalMSAATileSize`/per-draw thresholds once Skia exposes them. | RenderPerfTest AA tests and `test/jdk/performance` path-heavy scenes with `k1` vs `k4`; variant 2 measurements must be done both ways; power on a laptop. Note that rrects/ovals/rects use the analytic renderer either way — only general paths differ. **Measured 2026-10-14 (§11.7b, `sun.java2d.skia.msaa=4` knob, `91b543896954`): AA parallelograms 0.75–0.85×, everything else ≥ 1.0×; geomean 0.97× over 8 AA tests.** Variant 2 and SVG icons still open. |
| D3 | **Marlin tiles via a 2048² A8 atlas + per-row staging** (§11.11) and **shader-painted tiles drawn one by one** (no `sk_FragCoord` in public ES2 SkSL) | Gradient/texture-painted AA shapes cost one draw per 32×32 tile (an islands header at 1000×40 ≈ 64 draws per repaint). | Private `SkRuntimeEffectPriv` access for `sk_FragCoord`; a mesh with per-vertex canvas coordinates as a second attribute (not expressible in `SkVertices` without custom attributes → `SkMesh`, which Graphite m156 does not support); variant 2 (Skia rasterizes the shape, no tiles). | IDE scene measurements (gradient headers, islands) — count tile draws per frame with `J2D_TRACE_LEVEL`. |
| D4 | **XOR as a runtime blender with bit-by-bit arithmetic** (§11.12) | A ~30-iteration loop per pixel in the fragment shader; `RenderPerfTest *_XOR` tests are excluded from the geomean so the cost is unmeasured. Metal's XOR differs from software anyway, so "match software exactly" was a choice. | Integer SkSL (needs non-ES2 runtime effects, not available for blenders in m156); match Metal's cheaper semantics instead of software's. | Run the XOR benchmark subset; decide which backend's XOR is the reference before investing. |
| D5 | **Global native render state reconciled by Java-side invalidation** (§13) | Correct as long as every context switch invalidates; multi-display and multi-context scenes are untested. | Per-`SKConfigContext` state as Metal does. | Before multi-display measurements. |
| D6 | **Glyph atlas from `AccelGlyphCache` + our own mesh batching** instead of Skia's text stack (§11.9) | Reimplements what Skia's glyph atlas does, but keeps Java's rasterizer, metrics and LCD semantics (the whole point of variant 1 / path b). | Skia text (path a, WP 3.2). | WP 3.2. |
| D7 | **Shape-mode and MSAA defaults are the same on every GPU API** (`analytic`, `msaa=1`; 2026-10-16, §10) | On Dawn/D3D11 `msaa=4` would make general paths and AA parallelograms display-rate (`FlatQuadAA` 13 → 120, `RotatedBoxAA` 40 → 120) where Metal gains little; the uniform default leaves that on the table. | Per-API defaults table (`SKEnvironment` already has the architecture-dependent configuration hook). | When Windows runs on real hardware: measure `msaa=4` coverage quantisation (§11.7d) against the general-path share of IDE scenes. |
| D8 | **LCD glyph batches are per colour** (the LCD blender takes the text colour as a uniform; per-channel coverage in `src.rgb`) (2026-10-16, §11.19) | `TextLCD` 0.1–0.2× GDI on Dawn, ~6 ms CPU per frame for 63k glyphs; the Metal pipeline draws LCD glyphs one by one (~155 ms for 1,500 distinct glyphs) | Colour in a second vertex attribute consumed by the blender, or dropping the LCD mode (Laurent's JBR-7462 TODO; macOS has had no subpixel text since 10.14) | When IDE LCD usage is measured; LWCToolkit still publishes LCD_HRGB as the desktop hint. |
| D9 | **LCD text without a destination read is two fixed-function passes with the gamma folded into the coverage** (`sun.java2d.skia.lcd=twopass`, Windows default; 2026-10-19, §10, §11.20) | ≤ 15 levels off at glyph edges for light text on mid-tones (exact on white and black); two draws per batch; with the overlap split, one draw pair per overlapping group (≈ per glyph on Dawn, unmeasured); two `pow` per channel per pass. | `blend` (exact, needs framebuffer fetch to be cheap); grayscale AA for LCD requests; Skia's own LCD text with dual-source blending (bypasses Java's glyph images); a Skia change exposing dual-source output to runtime effects. | `LcdTextBench` on Dawn with the split; IDE dark-theme scenes on Windows; a Vulkan measurement before `twopass` stays its default. |

## 15. Candidates for upstream reports or questions (Skia)

Checked against Skia's own documentation before listing; "report?" is my current judgement.

| # | Observation | Where seen | Report? |
|---|---|---|---|
| U1 | **`SkSurfaces::AsImage` ordering is the client's job** — a `writePixels` recorded after a draw that samples the atlas may or may not be applied before that draw, depending on device creation order (SrcMaskOps). Surface.h says so explicitly: *"care must be taken by the client to ensure that the contents of the image reflect the desired contents when it is consumed by the gpu."* | §11.13 rule 3 | **No bug.** At most a doc request: spell out that *record order* is not the guarantee and that a sampling draw (or a snap) is what pins an upload. Our fix (upload before every mesh draw) is the intended usage. |
| U2 | **Non-AA is honoured only for axis-aligned rects**; rotated/sheared rects, paths and image rects are always anti-aliased (`Device::drawRect` snaps, nothing else does). Documented only in a source comment ("Graphite assumes everything is anti-aliased"). | §11.13 rule 1 | **Feature request / question** worth asking: is per-edge AA via `drawEdgeAAQuad`/`drawEdgeAAImageSet` the intended way to get non-AA geometry in Graphite, and will it stay public? A pixel-exact 2D API on top of Graphite (ours, Flutter's non-AA modes) needs an answer. |
| U3 | **Whole-pass MSAA**: one MSAA draw multisamples every draw in the pass, so non-AA edges recorded in the same frame get 4-level coverage. | §11.13 rule 2 | Design, not a bug; could be raised together with U2 (the two interact: without U2 nothing would need U3). |
| U4 | **`drawAtlas` becomes one draw + one paint key per sprite** in Graphite (profiled; ~2 µs each), unlike Ganesh. | §11.9 | **Worth a performance report** with the sample profile; the API doc still describes it as a batching primitive. |
| U5 | **`sk_FragCoord` unavailable to public runtime effects in ES2 mode**; needs `SkRuntimeEffectPriv`. | §11.11, D3 | Known limitation; a question on the mailing list about plans, not a bug. |
| U6 | **`SkContexts::MakeGraphite` / `SkContext` non-functional in m156** (only `priv()` public). | §13 | Re-check on the next milestone before reporting; probably in-progress API. |
| U7 | Runtime **blenders are ES2-only SkSL** (no integer ops) even on Metal. | §11.12 | Known; same as U5. |

## 16. Suggestions for other backends (candidates for dedicated PRs)
- **JNI signature census (2026-10-29, host item 4 of 5): natives over HotSpot's 14-slot fast-handler limit in the shipping pipelines.** Every call to these pays the interpreter's slow signature handler (≈ 0.1–0.15 µs, §12 fixed cost round 2). In the software and GPU pipes that run today: `MaskFill.DrawAAPgram` (19 slots) and `FillAAPgram` (15) — the loops' antialiased parallelograms, every AA rectangle through the converter on a software surface (Windows); `DrawParallelogram.DrawParallelogram` (18) — non-AA parallelograms; `TransformHelper.Transform` (18) — every transformed image draw, scaled icons at fractional HiDPI included; `ScaledBlit.Scale` (16) — scaled blits; `BufferedMaskBlit.enqueueTile` (17) — the Metal / OpenGL pipes' Marlin tile upload, once per 32 × 32 tile of every AA shape; `SunLayoutEngine.shape` (16) — every shaped text run; macOS Aqua `JRSUIControl.paint*` (16–19); XRender `padBlitNative` (20), `putMaskNative` (15); ImageIO JPEG `readImage` (19) / `writeImage` (27, once per image). Not affected: the Metal and Vulkan render queues (opcodes in a buffer, not JNI calls) and the Skia GPU pipe's direct entries. Candidates for small dedicated PRs: pack `MaskFill`'s and `DrawParallelogram`'s coordinates, `TransformHelper`'s and `ScaledBlit`'s rectangles, `enqueueTile`'s tile description, `shape`'s flags — each a pure signature change with a measurable per-call gain on the IDE's hottest software paths. Script: `python3 -` over `src/java.desktop/**/*.java` counting a `native` declaration's slots (long/double = 2); 24 natives over the limit in java.desktop.

- **Metal LCD text, overlapping glyphs in one batch (2026-10-21):** `MTLTextRenderer`'s LCD path reads the destination from a copy taken before the batch, so a glyph drawn over a pending glyph of the same batch blends against the old background: `LcdTextLayersTest` (40,000 glyphs, half overlapping) measured 700 px up to 135 levels off the software loops on Metal (Skia: 0 px beyond 32 in every LCD mode). Fix candidates: flush the batch when a glyph overlaps a pending one (the Skia layer idea), or re-copy the destination per overlapping run.

Things the Skia work found or built that would improve the shipped pipelines independently of any Skia decision. Each should become its own ticket/PR when picked up; record the PR here.

| # | Backend | Suggestion | Origin | Effort | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | Metal | **Clip software-source blits to the visible destination and chunk them by the maximum texture size** (`MTLBlitLoops_Blit` uploads the whole source; a legal 20k-px-wide BufferedImage exceeds the texture limit and draws nothing). The Skia implementation is `blitSoftwareSource` in `SKBackend.mm`. | §11.13 (DrawHugeImageTest), review §11.14 | S | open |
| B2 | Metal | **Register `SwToSurfaceTransform` loops for all int source types** (`MTLBlitLoops.java` has them only for IntArgbPre; `IntRgb`/`IntArgb`/`IntBgr(x)` go through `TransformHelper` in software + a plain blit). Pixels identical, less CPU. | §11.13 side finding | S | open |
| B3 | Metal, Vulkan, Skia | **Share one `QueueFlusher`** in `sun.java2d.pipe` instead of three copies (`MTLRenderQueue`, `VKRenderQueue`, `SKRenderQueue`). | review §11.14 | S | open |
| B4 | Metal | **Present window surfaces by submitting on the blit queue instead of waiting for the command buffer** — the Skia backend orders presentation this way without a CPU wait (`SKBackend_Submit` on `blitCommandQueue`); check whether `MTLRenderQueue`'s wait for window destinations is still needed with display sync on. | review §11.14 | M | open, needs measurement |
| B5 | Metal | **XOR without a command-buffer commit per draw** (`MTLRenderQueue.m:209-213` commits before every XOR primitive; Metal XOR runs at 1–3.5 FPS in RenderPerfTest vs 26–51 FPS for the Skia blender). A fragment-shader XOR with framebuffer fetch on Apple GPUs would remove the commit. | §11.7b | M | open |
| B6 | Vulkan | `FILL_AAPARALLELOGRAM` renders without AA (`// TODO this is not AA!`); Metal has an analytic AA-parallelogram shader. | §12 2026-10-13 | M | open, mention to the Vulkan owner |
| B7 | Software loops / jtreg | Six java2d tests fail on Metal on this machine (`CopyAreaOOB`, `GposTest`, `MenuMultiresolutionIconTest`, `GdiBlitOffscreenTest` flaky, `PolyVertTest#opengl`, `SourceClippingBlitTest` flaky); only `PolyVertTest#default` is in `ProblemList.txt`. Also: 16 tests pass `-Dsun.java2d.opengl=True`, which on macOS JBR selects the retired OpenGL pipeline — the `#opengl`/`#id1` variants test nothing the shipped product uses. Triage the `.jtr` files before mentioning them. | §11.13 | S | open |
- **Metal: glyph cache eviction stalls — PR branch `manuel.unterhofer/JBR-10654-metal-glyph-cache` on the fork (`350f161580c2`, pixel-identical to `main` Metal, text in `build/notes/metal-pr/`), → ticket JBR-10667, PR #645 against `main`: JBR-7462 part (Laurent's, flush vs free + pooled texture) + per-cell eviction with completion tracking, 1024², plain A8 layout (2026-10-16).** Confirmed and quantified (§11.17: flush −45…−55 %, Swing paint −20…−33 % with the three switches; pixel-identical).** The Metal glyph cache (512×512, 32×32 cells, i.e. 256 glyphs per AA/LCD cache) is freed wholesale when full: pending quads flushed, command buffer committed **and waited for on the flusher thread**, texture released and reallocated, all glyphs re-uploaded. In a typing run this was 47 % of the flusher's active time (§11.16 (5)); each `replaceRegion` upload also pays the driver's CPU texture compression. Three independent fixes, each small: (a) per-cell eviction as `AccelGlyphCache` already implements (the Skia backend uses it: `SKGlyphCache.cpp`), no GPU wait; (b) a 1024×1024 atlas (4× the cells; 1 MB A8 / 4 MB RGBA); (c) `allowGPUOptimizedContents = NO` on the glyph and mask cache texture descriptors so CPU-updated textures skip lossless compression. Together they should remove most of the Metal-vs-Skia flush gap in text scenes. Measure with the same recording and `sample` after the change.
- **Metal: find the remaining flush cost.** After the glyph cache: per-tile mask `replaceRegion` uploads vs. Skia's staged atlas rows, encoder restart per op change; coalescing command buffers is *not* it (measured slower, §11.16 (4)).

- **Metal grayscale text ≈ 12 % lighter than the software loops and Skia with identical glyph images** (2026-10-22, text sheets v6: Inter 13 154,587 vs 174,796 ink; Helvetica 183,205 vs 203,358). Pre-existing; the IDE's text on Metal is lighter than the software reference by that much. Look at `MTLTextRenderer`'s grayscale path (texture format/sampling, blend) before blaming the scaler side in any comparison. Root cause found the same day: JBR-3820's luminance-dependent coverage exponent (1.4 dark … 0.454 light) in `frag_gmc_text` — intentional, so not a bug but a model to compare against the CoreGraphics-per-luminance route.
## 17. Variant 2 and the commitment ladder — design notes (2026-10-14)

Recorded so work can resume here without re-deriving it. Variant 1 is frozen as the comparison baseline (after the §11.14 fixes).

**Where variant 2 differs from variant 1.** Only `fill(Shape)`/`draw(Shape)` with antialiasing on: `SKSurfaceData.validatePipe` installs a new `SKShapePipe` instead of `AAShapePipe` (Marlin → `SKMaskFill`). Everything else — surfaces, queue, contexts, paints, clips, text, images, blits, non-AA shapes (spans/meshes), rect fills under transforms (parallelogram ops) — is shared. Delta: one Java pipe class, a few Skia-private opcodes in `SKRenderQueue`, their native handlers. The mask path stays as the fallback for shapes the pipe declines.

**Shape pipe rules.** Classify before encoding: `Rectangle2D`/`RoundRectangle2D`/`Ellipse2D` → Skia's analytic rect/rrect/oval renderers (no MSAA, no atlas — the expected win: rounded UI, islands, `FlatOvalAA`, `WiredBubblesAA`, gradient ovals, clipped ovals); everything else → a general path opcode → Graphite path atlas (CPU raster + upload on this configuration; roughly a wash against Marlin, measure with `sun.java2d.skia.msaa=4` as well). Feed Skia the same **normalizing path iterator** Marlin gets (`STROKE_NORMALIZE`), so both rasterizers see identical geometry and the remaining differences are coverage only. Stroke in **user space** under the canvas matrix (Skia stroke style + dash path effect); pre-stroke in Java only for custom `Stroke` implementations.

**Switches: one per seam, owned by the layer that owns the behaviour — no single "Skia level" enum.**

| Dimension | Today | Next rung(s) | Scope | Switch |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GPU backend for the opcode stream | Skia, `sun.java2d.skia=true` | — | Skia pipeline | exists |
| AA shape rasterization | Marlin tiles (AA parallelograms are already Skia paths) | analytic shapes, then general paths (variant 2) | Skia pipeline | **`sun.java2d.skia.shapes=marlin|skia`**, read once in `SKEnvironment`, consulted only in `validatePipe`; mode not boolean so finer splits (analytic on, paths off) can follow |
| Software rasterization | Marlin + C loops | Skia raster as a `RenderingEngine` (coverage tiles), later Skia raster surfaces | every pipeline | existing seam **`sun.java2d.renderer=<class>`** (`RenderingEngine.java:120`); a Skia CPU rasterizer is a value, no new property |
| Glyph rasterization | Java scaler (CoreText here); Skia only composites | Skia renders glyph images from SkFont | every pipeline (shared `AccelGlyphCache` path) | value of the text-engine property below; §10 (2026-10-12) argues against shipping glyph-raster-only, keep it as an experiment rung for appearance triage |
| Metrics, shaping | `sun.font` + HarfBuzz | `sun.font` on SkFont/SkShaper | product-wide re-flow (path a) | **`sun.font.engine=platform|skia`** (seam: `FontManagerFactory`, which has no property in this tree yet); one property for scaler+shaper+metrics as a unit |
| Paragraph layout | `TextLayout`/`LineBreakMeasurer` in `sun.font.TextLine` & co. | SkParagraph behind the existing public API | public API behaviour; independent of which pipeline draws | **`sun.font.paragraph=java|skia`** — a dedicated property (correcting my first answer, which treated this as API design only): the public API stays, its implementation is routed, so it needs a switch like the others; validate at startup that `skia` requires `sun.font.engine=skia` (SkParagraph measures with SkFont; mixing engines breaks the consistency-by-construction argument). The new JBR rich-text API on SkParagraph is separate and needs no property. |
| Platform | macOS/Metal | Windows via Graphite + Dawn (WP 2.2) | port, not a mode | — |

Rationale: each rung independently measurable and revertible, combinations expressible (shapes on / text off isolates re-flow), switches sit next to the code they steer, native code never learns about Java-side routing. Text-metric changes are path-a work and need the plan's stabilization-release commitment; shapes and software raster are path b. Native-only options (MSAA) stay environment variables.

**Variant 2 work order.** (1) property + `SKShapePipe` skeleton: fills of rect/rrect/oval/path; (2) strokes and dashes with the normalizing iterator; (3) matrix (AA shape rows classify Marlin-vs-Skia coverage), RenderPerfTest AA tests both MSAA ways, IDE scenes; (4) decide the default.

**Status 2026-10-14 (night):** option (C) measured (§11.7d): exact non-AA under MSAA works, but MSAA's 4-level AA coverage costs fidelity everywhere Skia rasterizes AA → recommendation `analytic` as default candidate with MSAA off; MSAA remains a per-backend knob. After the REPEAT fix the matrix stands at marlin 853 / analytic 814 / skia 794 cells within 32 levels of software (Metal 695). **Per-backend configuration** (user question): the decisions that genuinely depend on the GPU architecture are few — MSAA on/off (tile-based GPUs resolve MSAA cheaply, the path atlas is CPU work), the non-AA strategy (mesh vs span decomposition vs per-edge-AA draws), upload strategy (staging rows vs per-tile writes), and the shapes mode. Each is already a run-time switch (env `sun.java2d.skia.msaa`, `sun.java2d.skia.blitQuads`; property `sun.java2d.skia.shapes`), so re-testing on Vulkan/Dawn is a matter of running the matrix and RenderPerfTest with other values. **Done 2026-10-14:** the native knobs are system properties (`sun.java2d.skia.msaa`, `sun.java2d.skia.blitQuads`) with a per-GPU-API default table in `SKEnvironment.Configuration`, handed to the native backend before the first context exists; the table's measured values live in §14. Making more of the design configurable is unlikely to pay off: the rest (pixel-origin paints, software-exact lines, normalization, LCD gamma) is Java2D semantics, not GPU preference.

**Status 2026-10-14 (evening):** three modes exist and are measured (§11.7c); the default stays `marlin` until D2 is decided — recommendation so far: `analytic` captures the IDE win with no new visual class and no MSAA; **the next step is option (C)** (MSAA on + span-decomposed rotated non-AA parallelograms and blits), which would let the all-Skia mode take general paths too.

**Status 2026-10-14:** (1)–(3) done except IDE scenes (`e45ac589717d`); results §11.7c. **Hybrid (A) implemented** as `sun.java2d.skia.shapes=analytic` (`980b2ce71b13`, jbr25 `2100c8c0de28`): `SKShapePipe.Hybrid` routes analytic shapes to Skia and the rest to the Marlin pipe installed for the state; matrix 798 cells within 32 levels of software (marlin 835, skia 777). Benchmark of the hybrid pending (display busy with jtreg). **jtreg subset with `shapes=skia`** (`build/jtreg-skia-v2.log`): 256 passed / 7 failed — the six known Metal-also/flaky ones plus `SrcMaskOps#id1`, which at the default 2× scale sees `ff2020ff` instead of blue at the corner pixel of a 1-px AA `drawRect` under `Src` (Robot capture downsampled from 2×; off-screen at 1× all three modes give the exact software pixels): a miter-corner coverage difference between Skia's stroker and Marlin's, within the "coverage-level" class but outside that test's tolerance. No new Skia-specific failures otherwise. **IDE on jbr25 with `shapes=analytic`**: IDEA 2026.2.3 starts (JRE 25.0.4+1-b508.27, property confirmed in the JVM options), project view, Markdown preview, terminal, badges and rounded controls render correctly, 0 log errors (`idea/startup-check-analytic.png`). **Hybrid benchmark** (`rpt-hybrid-aa.log`, 17 AA tests): 1.14× vs variant 1 — axis-aligned ovals/bubbles 2.6–2.9×, everything else ≈ 1.0× including rotated ovals, which the first hybrid handed back to Marlin although Skia renders them 2.4× faster as paths. Follow-up `analytic shapes under any transform` (next commit): rotated/sheared rect/rrect/oval go to Skia's analytic renderers in user space under the transform, in both modes; matrix unchanged (analytic 798, skia 779 within 32 levels). Next: the hybrid mode (A) as a third value of the property or as the behaviour of `skia`, option (C) prototype (span decomposition for rotated non-AA parallelograms and blits under MSAA), jtreg subset in `shapes=skia`, IDE run on jbr25 with shapes on Skia, then the default decision.
