## 18. Quirks of the reference pipelines, and what approximating them costs

- **Whole-pixel advances are rounded in two different spaces (2026-10-22).** With fractional metrics off, `CStrike` (CoreText, every system font on macOS) rounds the advance in *user* space (`AWTStrike.m`: the advance is mapped through the font transform without the device transform, then `round()`), while `FileFontStrike` over the FreeType scaler (every `Font.createFont` font on macOS, so the IDE's bundled JetBrains Mono and Inter, and every font on Windows/Linux) rounds in *device* space and maps back through `invertDevTx`. At 1.25×/1.5×/2× the two rules disagree on roughly half the glyphs by one device pixel (Menlo at 14 pt × 1.25: every glyph). Consequence: text metrics of the same string differ between a system font and a bundled font not only by the font but by the rule; and any new scaler has to pick the rule per strike type to be pixel-compatible (SKFontScaler: `createScalerContext(matrix, deviceScale, …)` for the CoreText-style strikes, the plain contract for the FreeType-style ones).
- **Grayscale text on macOS is "smoothed" CoreGraphics output, not coverage (2026-10-22).** `CGGlyphImages.m` renders every glyph into an ARGB (premultiplied-first, sRGB) bitmap with the context's default font smoothing left on and only `shouldAntialias` set from the hint; because the context has an alpha channel, CoreGraphics applies its smoothing (a widening of the stems proportional to the size, ≈ 0.013 em: 0.17 px at 13 px, 0.35 px at 26 px) but no sub-pixel colouring, and the JDK then averages the three channels (`CGGI_ConvertBWPixelToByteGray`: 255 − (R+G+B)/3). So the JDK's grayscale glyphs carry ≈ 15 % more ink than true coverage, and the same font renders heavier on macOS than through FreeType on Windows/Linux (where grayscale is plain coverage, hinted). No public Skia API reproduces "smoothing without sub-pixels": the CoreText port smooths only with sub-pixel edging. Re-evaluate: platform-consistent coverage (lighter than today's macOS text, equal to Windows/Linux) vs an emulation (`sun.java2d.skia.text.gray=darkened`, Skia's path rasterizer with the em-proportional widening).
- **LCD text on macOS is the JDK's own blend over CoreGraphics' sub-pixel masks (2026-10-22).** macOS itself has not drawn sub-pixel text since Mojave, but the JDK still asks CoreGraphics for sub-pixel coverage (the `argb` glyph descriptor, byte order host) and blends it per channel in its own loops/shaders with the `lcdContrast` gamma; `CStrike` requests LCD only for opaque colours on opaque destinations (`canRenderLCDText`). Skia's CoreText port produces the equivalent masks (within 28 levels, ink +2…5 %), so the "LCD" appearance on macOS is a JDK artefact that any engine can keep or drop; IntelliJ's default on macOS is grayscale.
- **Sub-pixel glyph positioning is a JBR extension with its own conventions (2026-10-22).** With `TEXT_ANTIALIAS_ON` + `FRACTIONALMETRICS_ON` the glyph record carries `subpixelResolutionX × subpixelResolutionY` images (`java2d.font.subpixelResolution`, FreeType and CoreText scalers alike), the text pipes pick the image by the position's fraction, and `CStrike.getGlyphImageBounds` snaps positions with `(int)(x * res + 0.5) / res`; LCD glyphs never get sub-pixel variants. An engine that positions glyphs itself (Skia's own text rendering) would have to reproduce this quantization to keep layout identical.
- **Bounds of bitmap glyphs are whole pixels on the Skia host, exact on CoreText (2026-10-22).** `CStrike.getGlyphOutlineBounds` answers with `CTFontGetBoundingRectsForGlyphs`, fractional for every glyph including Apple Color Emoji (sbix bitmaps); Skia's `SkFont::getBounds` for a glyph without an outline returns the glyph image's integer pixel bounds, a pixel wider than the ink (−1, −9, 164.8 × 12 vs 0.46, −7.05, 162.3 × 8.9 for a 9 pt line). `SKFontScaler` therefore takes the outline's tight bounds where there is an outline and the host's only for bitmap glyphs, so `GlyphVector.getVisualBounds` of emoji is up to a pixel larger than today's. Same family as the COLR/CBDT handling in the FreeType scaler (`getGlyphRenderData` bitmaps), to re-evaluate together when colour fonts get a proper design.
- **The two engines on one platform (2026-10-22).** On macOS, system fonts are CoreText (`CFont`/`CStrike`), but every `Font.createFont` font — the IDE's bundled JetBrains Mono and Inter — is a `TrueTypeFont` on the FreeType scaler: unhinted-but-smoothed vs hinted-plain-coverage glyphs, user-space vs device-space advance rounding, different LCD filters, side by side in one window. Moving the bundled fonts to a CoreText host (Skia's) changes their appearance (Inter 13 px aliased visibly heavier, LCD ink +30…60 %) while system fonts stay; moving everything to FreeType (custom Skia build) would do the opposite for system fonts. Platform consistency is the question to settle before T3.

What Java2D's software loops, Marlin and the Metal pipeline do that is not "correct" in any absolute sense, not what comparable systems (Skia/Chrome, Direct2D, Core Graphics) do, or simply an implementation accident — together with how the Skia backend approximates each today and a ballpark of the cost. If path a accepts differences in text flow, text rasterization and AA in general, the rows marked *droppable* can go at the same time; the rows marked *semantics* are Java2D API behaviour and should stay regardless. Costs are estimates, not measurements, unless a section is cited.

| # | Quirk | Nature | Where it shows | How we approximate it | Cost today | If differences are accepted |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | **Paints evaluated at integer pixel coordinates** (paint contexts loop over `x`, not `x + 0.5`; Metal shaders subtract 0.5) | implementation convention; every other system samples at centres | every gradient/texture off by half a pixel vs centre sampling; REPEAT seams | half-pixel local matrix on each paint shader | ~0 | *droppable* — a half-pixel shift nobody would notice outside an A/B |
| Q2 | **REPEAT boundary pixel wraps** because Java's float t lands on the integer | float rounding accident | one pixel per seam | 1e-5 bias in t | 0 | *droppable* (then Skia's own ulp behaviour decides the seam pixel) |
| Q3 | **Linear REPEAT seams are float noise** (software disagrees with itself from seam to seam) | rounding accident | one pixel per seam, both GPU pipelines | not approximated (impossible) | — | accept |
| Q4 | **Gradient colours quantized to a 256-entry LUT with truncation** | implementation shortcut (1997 performance) | ±1 level across every gradient; banding identical to Java's | not approximated; Skia interpolates continuously | — | accept; replicating needs a LUT texture + shader (small GPU cost, moderate code) and would *add* banding |
| Q5 | **STROKE_NORMALIZE**: AA path end points snapped to pixel centres, control points shifted along; non-AA to quarter pixels | Java-specific "crisp lines" convention; Skia/Chrome/CG do not do this | the look of every AA shape: 1-px strokes crisp, shapes shifted ≤ 0.5 px | `NormalizingIterator` in Java (exact) | ~0 CPU (one pass over the path) | *droppable in path a* — but it is the most visible change of all: thin AA strokes become softer; keep for b |
| Q6 | **Thin AA strokes are 1/8 px wide** (`MinPenSizeAA`) | Marlin accident (sub-pixel count) | hairline AA strokes nearly invisible in Java | width rule in `SKShapePipe` | 0 | *droppable*: a 1-px minimum would look better and match other systems |
| Q7 | **Skia's hairline rule** (AA strokes ≤ 1 device px drawn as hairlines with a different coverage model) | Skia's quirk, not Java's | 1-px AA strokes | stroke outline filled (`FillPathWithPaint`) | stroker CPU per stroke, same order as Skia's internal stroking | if Skia's look is accepted, use Skia's stroke (saves a path copy) |
| Q8 | **Dash measurement**: Marlin's dasher vs Skia's `SkPathMeasure`/conic ovals | both are approximations; neither is "right" | dash ends drift ≤ 1 px along curves | Java's `createStrokedShape` for dashed strokes | tens of µs per dashed draw (rare) | *droppable*: Skia's dash effect is cheaper and fine |
| Q9 | **Bresenham pixel selection** (`DrawLine.c` error terms, polyline joints drawn once) | Java's software algorithm; Metal already differs | 1-px non-AA lines | exact replica emitting runs | 0 (same loop) | keep: cheap and non-AA lines are "semantics" to users |
| Q10 | **Non-AA pixel-centre rule for parallelograms/blits** (half-open, ties to the right/bottom) | Java semantics (software loops define it) | rotated non-AA fills, scaled images | meshes (no MSAA) or span decomposition (MSAA) | 2 µs per draw / CPU per row | *semantics* — keep |
| Q11 | **Marlin coverage**: 8×8 sub-pixel sampling → 64-level coverage with its own rounding (`SUBPIXEL_LG_POSITIONS`) | implementation choice | every AA edge, ≤ 32 levels vs analytic | not approximated; Skia analytic/atlas gives 256 levels | — | accept (Skia is at least as good); matching would require Marlin itself |
| Q12 | **Metal's analytic AA parallelogram shader** vs Marlin for the same rect: Metal differs from software too | Metal quirk | AA rect fills/strokes | we draw paths; candidate analytic `drawRect` | 0 / small gain | accept |
| Q13 | **LCD text**: gamma-blend with `contrast/100` exponent, text colour pre-adjusted; on modern macOS the "LCD" glyph images are neutral (no subpixel) | Java/Metal convention; macOS removed subpixel AA in 10.14 | all LCD text | runtime blender replicating `lcd_color` | one `pow` per channel per pixel (GPU, negligible) | *droppable in path a* (Skia's own LCD/gamma tables); on macOS "LCD" is just gamma-corrected gray anyway |
| Q14 | **Glyph sub-pixel positioning** via N pre-rendered sub-images per glyph | Java convention (Skia does the same with 4 positions, differently keyed) | text placement | atlas sub-images | atlas memory ×N | *droppable in path a* with Skia text |
| Q15 | **XOR**: software is exact bitwise on the pixel; Metal's shader differs (and commits a command buffer per draw) | semantics (software) vs Metal bug | XOR fills/text | bit-by-bit blender matching software | ~30 shader iterations per XOR pixel (XOR is rare) | *semantics* — keep; only the Metal/software disagreement is a Metal issue (§16 B5) |
| Q16 | **`Src`/`SrcIn` with coverage = lerp** (MaskFill semantics); Metal writes premultiplied values instead | software semantics; Metal bug | translucent `Src` shapes | Skia's coverage handling already lerps | 0 | *semantics* — keep (Metal should be fixed, §16) |
| Q17 | **`div8table` unpremultiply rounding** | implementation detail | readback, colours | exact replica | 0 | *droppable* (±1 level) |
| Q18 | **Software blits never sample outside the source rect** (edge extension), bilinear with pixel-centre inclusion | semantics (image edges must not bleed) | scaled/rotated images | half-texel coordinate clamp + mesh/spans | 0 | *semantics* — keep |
| Q19 | **Shape clips are hard-edged span regions** (no AA clip in Java2D) | API semantics | clipped AA shapes have hard clip edges | spans → clip path | path clip cost per shape | *semantics*; a soft clip would be a new feature, not a fix |
| Q20 | **BufferedImageOps / `getRaster` on accelerated surfaces unsupported** | parity with Metal | rare | not supported | — | keep |
| Q21 | **Marlin tile protocol** (32×32 coverage tiles, per-tile upload) | implementation (the whole reason for variant 2) | every AA shape | atlas + row staging / variant 2 | measured §11.7c | *droppable* with variant 2 |
| Q22 | **Text metrics/advances from Java's scaler** (CoreText here), rounding rules | semantics for layout; path a replaces them | all text | untouched (Skia composites Java's glyphs) | — | path a: the one-time re-flow (§5) |
| Q23 | **Image edges are hard** (a destination pixel is drawn when its centre maps inside the source; no coverage at the image's border, however it is rotated or placed) (2026-10-28, raster slice 5) | Java semantics (the transform helper); Skia antialiases an image's rectangle on request and Chrome/CG do | rotated images, images at fractional device positions (not integer scale steps such as 16 → 20 px at 125 %, whose edges land on pixel boundaries) | non-antialiased rectangle over a clamped image shader (`SKRasterDrawImage`; the GPU pipe's meshes, Q10) | 0 | *triage (design)*: antialiased image edges are the one place the Skia route could look better rather than equal; same class as the AA-shape coverage question |
| Q24 | **ProcessPath samples the pixel's top-left corner** — an opaque non-AA fill under a thin stroke state and a rectangular clip is scan-converted with a half-pixel offset on both axes (`MDP_HALF_MULT`), unshifted under STROKE_PURE; the same shape under a translucent composite, a paint, a shape clip or a wide stroke state goes through the span iterator with centre sampling and quarter-pixel normalization instead (2026-10-28, raster slice 7) | Java's 1.1 pen model ("hangs down and to the right") kept alive in one renderer; the fill of a shape depends on the current stroke and composite | diagonal and curved non-AA fills shift by half a pixel between the two routes (a polygon's top sliver, the rows of an oval) | the raster pipe shifts the path by (0.5, 0.5) on the ProcessPath route and normalizes on the span route; ties nudged by 1/64 px | 0 (Java-side transform) | *droppable*: one rule (centre sampling, the span iterator's) for every non-AA fill would move ProcessPath's shapes by half a pixel once, for the better |
| Q25 | **The span iterator flattens curves at a one-pixel chord tolerance** (`MAX_FLAT_SQ 1.0`, `SUBDIVIDE_MAX` levels): non-AA ovals, round rectangles and stroke outlines are inscribed polygons up to a pixel inside the curve (2026-10-28) | implementation shortcut; ProcessPath and Skia follow the curve closely | 16 % of an oval's edge pixels differ from the true curve; the loops' non-AA ovals look slightly small and polygonal | `FlatteningPathIterator` at flatness 1 before Skia on the span route | the flattening per shape | *droppable*: Skia's curve is the accurate one; dropping the emulation changes non-AA curved fills by one pixel along the edge |
| Q26 | **Marlin's stroker ignores redundant segments** — a zero-length line, a line back along the previous one, a lineTo after a close — and strokes the clean path; Skia's stroker lets them shape the join (2026-10-29, `JoinMiterRedundantLineSegmentsTest`) | stroker implementation detail; neither is wrong | miter joins of paths built with redundant points: 3–4 levels on a few pixels | zero-length segments dropped before Skia's stroker; collinear back-tracks not | 0 | *accept*: the loops' outline is the baseline by convention; a path cleaner before Skia's stroker would close it |

Reading: rows Q1, Q2, Q6, Q8, Q17 cost nothing to keep and buy exact A/B comparability; they are the first to drop if path a is chosen, because they only exist to match an accident. Q5 (normalization) is the decision that changes how the product looks; it is also the one Skia-based UIs have lived without. Q11 and Q13 are where "accepting differences in AA and text" turns into simplification: no Marlin tiles, no custom LCD blender. Everything marked *semantics* stays in every path.

## 19. IDE integration performance test plan — Metal vs. Skia (2026-10-14)

### 19.1 What exists (research, `../ultimate` master)

- **The IJ-Perf "UI" suite** is `tests/intelliJPerformanceTests/testSrc/com/intellij/performance/ui/` (`@Subsystems.UI`): `IntelliJSourcesUIFunctionalityTest` (Search Everywhere ×6 kinds × cold/warm, Find in Files, expand project view / main / project / editor menus, file-structure popup Java/Kotlin, file history, `scrollEditor` on `ContentManagerImpl.java`), `PopupsPerformanceTest` (9 popups via Driver + Robot), `SettingsDialogPerformanceTest` (open settings early/after smart mode, open editor tabs), `BlockTerminalCompletionTest`. The dashboard `ij-perf.labs.jb.gg/intellij/uiDashboard` (49 charts, source `UIDashboard.vue`) adds the typing tests `typingInJavaFile_{4,16}Threads`, `typingInKotlinFile_{4,16}Threads` (`ResponsivenessTyping{Java,Kotlin}Test`, project `MavenJavaDesignPatterns`) for the `ui.lagging#max_value`, `ui.latency#max_value`, `popup.latency#max_value` charts. TC: "Integration Performance Tests for UI IntelliJ Project on DevServer" (monolith / split / AIA) — **Linux EC2 c6id.8xlarge, no GPU**, so nothing there measures Metal or Skia; our comparison has to run on macOS hardware.
- **Rendering-sensitive metrics already produced:** `typing` span attributes `latency#max / #p90 / #mean_value` (`DelayTypeCommand` ← `LatencyListener` ← `EditorImpl.measureTypingLatency()` called from `EditorComponentImpl.paintComponent`, i.e. **key press → editor painted**); FUS `ui.latency`, `ui.lagging`, `popup.latency` (`IdeHeartbeatEventReporter`, EDT heartbeat); `AWTEventQueue.dispatchTimeTotal` (every publish) and `dispatchTime{Avg,90P,Max}Ns` (`OtelReportingEventWatcher`); `scrollEditor#{max,average}_awt_delay`, `#{max,average}_cpu_load`; `popupShown#<popup>` / `afterShow#…`; Settings-dialog spans. Everything else on the dashboard (Search Everywhere, Find in Files, find usages) is indexing/PSI-bound — useful only as a sanity check that Skia does not break them.
- **Running on our JBR:** `-Dintellij.test.jbr.path=<Contents/Home>` (`StarterConfigurationStorage.JBR_PATH_SYSTEM_PROPERTY`, AT-4050) — in installer mode `JBRResolver.applyInstallerJbrOverrideIfLocalJbrPathNotEmpty` symlinks the app's `jbr` to it (macOS: `MacOsIdeDistribution` line 55; original kept as `.bundled`). VM options per context: `context.applyVMOptionsPatch { addSystemProperty("sun.java2d.skia", true); addSystemProperty("sun.java2d.skia.shapes", "analytic") }` or per run via `runIdeWithDriver { addVMOptionsPatch { … } }`. Installer mode: `@ExtendWith(UseInstaller::class)` or `JUNIT_RUNNER_USE_INSTALLER=true`; `ExtendedIdeDownloader` takes the last successful master installer (needs TC access), `USE_LATEST_DOWNLOADED_IDE_BUILD` reuses it. Results: `IJPerfMetricsPublisher` writes `metrics.performance.json` (TC artifact; printed to stdout locally); `mode` = `IJPERF_TEST_MODE` env → use `metal` / `skia-marlin` / `skia-analytic` so the same dashboards can split the arms if we ever publish from a Mac agent.
- **JBR team's own tool:** `plugins/runtimePerformanceTesting` (README there; plugin id `com.jetbrains.plugins.runtimePerformanceTesting`, not bundled — build and drop into `idea.plugins.path`): `-Djb.runtimePerformanceTesting.test=rendering` (editor `paintImmediately(visibleRect)` loop, 10 × 5 s, min/max/avg FPS, waits for CPU idle between iterations) and `=typing` (`.ijperf` script, EDT response time µs). Prints `##teamcity[buildStatisticValue …]`, optional JSON (`…statistics.report.jsonPath`), async/YourKit profiler hooks. The JBR 2026 plan (Alexey Ushakov's doc) lists "more scenarios for this plugin + RenderPerfTest + mapbench-style record/replay of IJ UI" as open work — our scenes can feed that.
- **Gaps for a renderer comparison** (none of the above paints the things Skia changes): no islands hover/animation, no smooth (pixel-step) scrolling, no tool-window show/hide/resize, no tab switching, no theme switch, no HiDPI/scale matrix, no Terminal output, no JCEF, no power/GPU sampling. Typing is the only existing metric that includes paint time end to end.

### 19.2 Method (same for every scene)

- **Arms:** `metal` (`-Dsun.java2d.metal=true`), `skia-marlin` (`-Dsun.java2d.skia=true -Dsun.java2d.skia.shapes=marlin`), `skia-analytic` (`…shapes=analytic`); optionally `skia-skia`. **Same JBR image for all arms** (jbr25 Skia image, `build/wt-jbr25/build/macosx-aarch64-skia/images/jdk-bundle/jdk-25.0.4.jdk/Contents/Home`) so only the pipeline property differs; verify the arm from `idea.log` (`Skia rendering enabled: YES (…, shapes: …)` with `-Dsun.java2d.skia.verbose=true`; Metal arm has no such line and `sun.java2d.metal=true`).
- **IDE:** one installer build for all arms (the master nightly Starter picks, or IDEA 2026.2.3 whose runtime our image was branched from — the test sources must match the installer's build line, so take the nightly and the matching ultimate master worktree). Same config/system dirs wiped per run (Starter does this), same project cache state (warm-up run per arm, discarded).
- **Hardware/environment:** M1 Max, built-in display at 2× (1728×1117 pt), AC power, nothing else on the GPU (no Meet, no browser video — §11.7 lesson), IDE window maximized by the test. Each scene × arm **≥ 5 repetitions, interleaved** (metal, skia-marlin, skia-analytic, metal, …) to spread thermal/background drift; report median and IQR; flag deltas > 5 % (latency) / > 10 % (FPS, CPU) only if the IQRs do not overlap.
- **Per-run side channels** (collected by a wrapper around the Starter run, no IDE change): `powermetrics --samplers gpu_power,cpu_power -i 1000` (needs sudo → run the sampler once per session with a cached credential or skip), GPU utilization (`ioreg`/`powermetrics gpu_power`), RSS and GPU memory of the IDE process (`footprint`/`vmmap --summary`), `AWTEventQueue.dispatchTime90P/Max` and the FUS `ui.latency/ui.lagging` from the IDE's own telemetry (already in the publish). Skipped frames: `MTLLayer`/Skia present counters from `-Dsun.java2d.trace=log` are too noisy; use the FPS plugin instead.

### 19.3 Phases

**Phase 0 — infrastructure (½ day).** `git worktree add ../ultimate-perf origin/master` (the `../ultimate` working copy on `fleet/compose` with 40 modified Fleet files stays untouched — nothing to back up because nothing there is edited). Open the worktree, Build Project (JPS, one-time ~30–60 min; the IDE under test comes from the installer, so no dev-server build). Run `PopupsPerformanceTest` once with `@ExtendWith(UseInstaller::class)`, `-Dintellij.test.jbr.path=…`, `IJPERF_TEST_MODE=metal`; confirm from `idea.log` that the IDE runs on our JBR (`JRE: 25.0.4+1-b508.27`) and that `metrics.performance.json` appears. Repeat with the Skia properties; confirm the verbose line. Write the three-arm runner script (`build/skia-bench/ijperf/run-arms.sh`) that loops arms × repetitions, sets env/`-D`, and copies each `metrics.performance.json` + `idea.log` into `build/skia-bench/ijperf/<scene>/<arm>/<n>/`, plus `compare.py` (median/IQR table per metric, like `build/skia-bench/compare.py`).

**Phase 1 — existing suite, rendering-relevant subset (1 day of machine time).** Scenes and the metrics that matter:
| Scene (existing test) | Project | Metrics to compare | Why it is rendering-sensitive |
|---|---|---|---|
| `typingInJavaFile_{4,16}Threads`, `typingInKotlinFile_{4,16}Threads` | MavenJavaDesignPatterns | `typing#latency#max/p90/mean`, `ui.latency#max`, `ui.lagging#max`, `AWTEventQueue.dispatchTime90P/Max` | key → paint, every keystroke repaints editor + gutter + inlays (text atlas, AA carets, selection rects) |
| `PopupsPerformanceTest.testPopups` (9 popups) | IntelliJ worktree @ pinned commit | `popupShown#*`, `afterShow#*`, `popup.latency#max` | popup = rounded, shadowed, translucent window: AA rrects, images, text — the islands look |
| `SettingsDialogPerformanceTest` (4 cases) | IntelliJ worktree | settings spans, `AWTEventQueue.*` | large dialog first paint: tree, borders, icons |
| `runScrollEditorTest` | IntelliJ worktree (`ContentManagerImpl.java`) | `scrollEditor#max/average_awt_delay`, `#…_cpu_load` | full editor repaint per 5-line step |
| `runExpand{Main,Project,Editor}MenuTest`, `runExpandProjectViewTest`, `runShow{Java,Kotlin}FileStructurePopupTest` | IntelliJ worktree | `%expand*Menu`, `projectViewInit`, `FileStructurePopup` | menus/tree painting with icons and text |
| Search Everywhere, Find in Files, file history | IntelliJ worktree | pass/fail + `searchEverywhere_dialog_shown` only | indexing-bound; sanity that Skia breaks nothing |
The IntelliJ-worktree scenes need the ultimate repo as the test project (Starter creates `../intellij` worktrees at the pinned commit) and a 20–40 min warm-up per arm; the typing scenes are cheap (small Maven project) — run them first and most often.

**Phase 2 — render-heavy scenes the suite lacks (1–2 days, new test class in the worktree, e.g. `tests/intelliJPerformanceTests/testSrc/com/intellij/performance/ui/RenderingPerformanceTest.kt`, Driver + `performanceTesting` commands; keep it on a branch, upstreaming is optional).** Each scene wraps its loop in `startProfile/stopProfile` so the span and the AWT/FUS metrics are published like the others:
1. *Islands hover/animation* — maximize, toggle `ToggleCompactMode`, move the Robot pointer across tool-window stripe buttons and editor tabs for 20 s (hover highlights = AA rrect fills + fades), open/close the project widget popup 20×. Metrics: `AWTEventQueue.dispatchTime90P/Max`, `ui.latency`, FPS via the runtime plugin's paint counter (below), GPU power.
2. *Smooth scrolling* — `scrollEditor` with 1-line steps and 16 ms delay through a 3000-line file with code vision and inlays enabled; also `Project View` and `Structure` tree scrolling via Robot mouse wheel. Metrics: awt delay, dropped-frame proxy (events dispatched per second vs. 60).
3. *Tool-window churn* — show/hide Project, Terminal, Problems, Git tool windows 20× each; drag the splitter 10× (full-frame relayout + repaint).
4. *Editor tab switching* — 10 open files, cycle 50× (`NextTab`), with and without soft wraps.
5. *Theme switch* — Light ↔ Dark 10× (`QuickChangeScheme`/`ChangeLaf`): everything repaints, icon cache invalidation (image uploads).
6. *Terminal output* — `seq 1 200000` in the Block Terminal (text-heavy, LCD/gray text path, scrolling).
7. *Settings dialog loop* — open/close 10× after warm-up (first paint vs. warm paint).
8. *HiDPI variant* — repeat 1, 2, 4 at `-Dsun.java2d.uiScale=1` on an external 1× display if available (the matrix showed 1× is where pixel rules bite).
Also run the **runtimePerformanceTesting plugin** on the same files for both of its scenarios: `rendering` (paint throughput of the editor view, the purest renderer number we can get inside the IDE) and `typing` (EDT response µs). Launch it with the same three arms through our existing `idea-*.vmoptions` + `idea.plugins.path`; it exits by itself and prints the statistics.

**Phase 3 — footprint and power (½ day).** For the typing and islands scenes: RSS and GPU memory per arm (`footprint -p`, `vmmap --summary`; expect the +280 MB Graphite budget item, §13), package/GPU power over the scene (`powermetrics`), and startup time to first frame (Starter `startup` metrics on IDEA 2026.2.3: `appInit`, `firstFrame` — Metal context vs. Graphite context creation).

**Phase 4 — optional CI.** If a macOS agent with a GPU is available to the JBR team (the `ij-runtime` channel owns the JBR promotion configs), run the Phase 1/2 classes there with `IJPERF_TEST_MODE=metal|skia-analytic` so IJ-Perf shows the arms side by side on `uiDashboard`-style charts; otherwise the local JSON + `compare.py` is the deliverable, and the Phase 2 scenes become the "more scenarios for the performance plugin" item of the JBR 2026 plan.

### 19.4 Expected sensitivity and decision use

- Scenes dominated by Java2D painting of islands geometry (1, 3, 7, popups) should show `skia-analytic` ahead of Metal (§11.7c: 2.2–3.5× on AA rrects/ovals in isolation) while `skia-marlin` ≈ Metal; if the IDE-level numbers do not move, the paint share of frame time is small and the rrect fast path (path c, WP6) is the cheaper way to get the same.
- Text-heavy scenes (typing, terminal, scrolling) test the glyph atlas and LCD blender — parity is the target, not a win.
- Theme switch and tab switching test image uploads (icons) — Graphite's record-time copy (§11.13 rule 4) is the risk.
- Footprint and power are the likely regressions; they belong in the recommendation memo next to the FPS numbers.

## 19b. Text-stack performance plan — today's Metal vs. the fidelity-equal hybrid vs. the full Skia route (2026-10-23, agreed as the step after the cleanup)

**Why now:** stage 4 made three stacks byte-identical in output, so a performance difference measures machinery, not pixel work.

**Arms (one image, JVM flags; the scene runner's `EXTRA_PROPS`):**
| arm | pipe | layout | glyph source | blend | pixels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M0 | Metal | Java | platform scaler images | JBR-3820 gamma shader | today's look |
| M5 | Metal | Java | Skia scaler, `gray=cg` | plain (`textGammaCorrection=false`) | native within 0.3 % |
| T5 | Skia pipe | Java | Skia scaler, `gray=cg` | plain | = M5 |
| F | Skia pipe | SkParagraph + SkShaper (`text=paragraph`) | cg masks in Skia's strike (`gray=cg`) | Skia text path | = M5, = T5 |
A 2×2 of renderer × text route at constant fidelity, plus M0 as today's baseline with its own look. Optional: T1 (Skia text path from Java's glyph codes, `text=skia gray=cg`) to split "Skia's atlas" from "Skia's shaping".

**Measurements:**
1. Steady state, warm caches: editor scroll and settings scenes, method timing (§11.16 instrument), n ≥ 5, distributions. Expect GPU-bound parts similar (both batch quads from an atlas).
2. Glyph churn: `GlyphChurnBench` (`build/skia-smoke`) extended to draw through `drawString`, so the shaper/paragraph routes are exercised; one-pixel readback as the sync. Cache-miss cost should be equal across M5/T5/F (CoreGraphics rasterization dominates: ≈ 4 ms per new luminance class and 84 glyphs).
3. Per-call CPU (the real question for F): many distinct short strings per frame; Java's plain path = character map + advances; F = HarfBuzz + ICU bidi/script runs + a text blob or paragraph per call (SkParagraph's `ParagraphCache` helps repeated strings). Report µs per string and per glyph.
4. Memory and startup: atlas and strike footprint (`cg`: one strike per luminance class on both sides), ICU/shaper/font-collection initialisation on first text, dylib growth from skshaper/skparagraph/skunicode/icu (unmeasured, §13.3).
5. Power: scroll and idle caret on the laptop (plan §4 WP1/WP3).

**Prerequisites (must land first):**
- **Queued opcode for the Skia text path — done 2026-10-26 (§12).** All three stage entries (`drawGlyphsBySkia`, `drawShapedBySkia`, `drawParagraphBySkia`) used `rq.flushAndInvokeNow`, a synchronous queue flush per text call, while Metal's text is batched in the queue buffer; they are opcodes 210–212 now, one flush per frame like Metal (`SkiaTextPathQueuedTest`, `FlushRate`).
- Thread attribution: Java rasterizes glyph images on the calling thread, Skia inside its strike at draw recording; on this backend both run on the flushing thread — the instrument must attribute by phase (layout / rasterize / upload / draw).
- Non-Robot RenderPerfTest mode (the Robot mode hangs for hours on the main-line runs, §13.6); CI perf still blocked (munit-924) → local runs.

**Effort:** ≈ 1 day prerequisite + 2–3 days for the table + 1 day power. **Expected reading:** M5 vs M0 = luminance classes + plain blend (small); T5 vs M5 = the renderer; F vs T5 = shaping/paragraph per call, where a real difference may appear.

### 19b.1 Results, 2026-10-26 (measurements 2–4 on the Mac; 1 and 5 pending)

Image `20d11b82d09d`+`109b5f90ea72` (queued text opcodes, strike-cache property). Tools: `build/skia-smoke/TextCallBench.java` (per-call: strings per frame into a VolatileImage, one-pixel readback as the frame's end; reports the caller thread's paint time and the total, and the Skia counters that moved), `GlyphChurnBench.java` (+`aci`), `RenderPerfTest -e=volatile -t` (caller-thread paint time only; the caller is charged for the flusher once the 6.4 MB buffer fills — 3000 strings × 43 glyphs × 16 B ≈ 2 MB per frame does that every third frame). Arms as the table above plus **T1** (`text=skia gray=cg`: Skia's text path from Java's glyph codes), **T1n** (`text=skia`, Skia's coverage masks), **FS** (`text=shaper gray=cg`), **FP** (`text=paragraph gray=cg`, attributed strings); `+sc64` = `-Dsun.java2d.skia.strikeCache=64`.

**A. Per call, no colour changes (medians, ms per frame, total incl. the sync):**
| scene | M0 | M5 | T5 | T1 | FS | FP |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 400 × 12-char strings, same set | 0.94 | 0.92 | 0.96 | 0.89 | 0.97 | 0.95 |
| 400 × 12, new strings every frame | 1.04 | 1.07 | 1.08 | 1.04 | 1.05 | 1.05 |
| 400 attributed strings (TextLayout / SkParagraph) | 3.10 | 3.16 | 3.20 | 3.18 (aci on T1 = TextLayout) | — | 3.12 |
| 3000 × 43-char strings (RenderPerfTest's shape) | 8.8 | — | 11.6 | 15.3 | 37.1 | — |
Caller-thread time of 400 plain strings is 0.07–0.08 ms on every arm (≈ 17 ns per glyph: Java's layout and queue encode are cheap; `TextLayout` costs ≈ 4.5 µs per attributed string on the caller, on every arm since the paragraph route's Java side still resolves fonts and strikes per run). The Skia text path's glyph draw costs ≈ 0.04 µs per glyph over the glyph-image pipes (T1 15.3 vs T5 11.6); **shaping costs ≈ 7 µs per 43-char string ≈ 0.17 µs per character** (FS − T1 = 22 ms per 3000 strings; profile: HarfBuzz's AAT ligature state machine for Helvetica Neue, CoreText cmap lookups through `SkTypeface_Mac`, UTF-16→UTF-8). 12-char strings showed no measurable shaping cost at 400 per frame. SkParagraph at 2820 distinct glyphs in 60 lines (churn bench, `aci`): **3.3 ms vs TextLayout's 7.7 ms on Metal** — once the strike cache is large enough (33 ms before, see C).

**B. Glyph churn (`GlyphChurnBench`, distinct glyph images per frame, ms per frame):** 94: 0.52–0.56 on all glyph-image arms and FS; 940: 2.33–2.39; 2820: 6.3 — M0 = M5 = T5 = T1 = FS within noise, i.e. cache-miss cost is CoreGraphics' rasterization on every arm, as expected. FP `aci`: 0.89 / 1.29 / 32.8 → 3.26 with `+sc64`.

**C. Colours — the finding of the day.** A random colour per string (RenderPerfTest's particles do this):
| 3000 × 43, distinct colours per frame | M0 | M5 | T5 | T1n | T1 | FS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 8.8 | — | 11.6 | — | 15.3 | 37.1 |
| 16 | 9.8 | **176** | 21.3 | 15.1 | 19.0 | 39.4 |
| 64 | 9.7 | 182 | 51.7 | 25.3 (+sc64 18.7) | 77.6 (**+sc64 22.5**) | 102 (**+sc64 44**) |
| 3000 | 9.7 | 185 | 53–58 | 200 (+sc64 103) | 829 (+sc64 126) | 848 |
400 × 12 with palettes of 1 / 2 / 16 / 400: M0 1.1 / 1.3 / 1.2 / 1.3; M5 1.1 / 1.3 / 9.5 / 10.0; T5 1.2 / 1.2 / 2.5 / 3.8; T1n 1.5 / 1.6 / 2.5 / 4.1; T1 1.7 / 1.7 / 2.8 / 4.3.
Mechanisms (profiles with `sample`, `build/skia-perf/text-2026-10-26/prof-*.txt`):
1. **Skia's strike cache (`SkStrikeCache`, default budget 2 MB, `SK_DEFAULT_FONT_CACHE_LIMIT`).** Skia keys an A8 strike by the paint colour quantized to a few bits per channel (`SkMaskGamma::CanonicalColor`; the width is set in `SkScalerContext.cpp`, not in the prebuilt), so dozens of colours = dozens of strikes; the cg route adds a proxy typeface per luminance class (16) on top. Past 2 MB every frame rasterizes its glyphs again (`SkScalerContext_Mac::generateImage`, on cg `SKPlatform_SmoothedGlyphMask` ≈ 6 µs per glyph). **Fix available:** `-Dsun.java2d.skia.strikeCache=<MB>` (`109b5f90ea72`, `SkiaStrikeCacheBudgetTest`); the default the backend should ship with is a design item (Chrome/Skiko run Skia's default; an IDE has more text colours and sizes on screen than a web page). Also behind RenderPerfTest's T1 `TextGray` 73.6 → 16.2 ms and FS 89.6 → 36.2 ms with `+sc64`.
2. **Graphite's glyph atlas** (`DrawAtlas`, `fGlyphCacheTextureMaximumBytes` default 8 MB) re-packs every frame at 3000 colours even with `+sc64` (`RectanizerSkyline::addRect` = 64 % of the flusher's samples): the residual 103 ms on T1n. Not tuned (the option exists; untested) — 3000 distinct colours per frame is not an IDE regime.
3. **Metal's glyph cache** (`MTLTextRenderer.m`: one 512 × 512 texture of 32 × 32 cells = **256 cells**) thrashes as soon as the cg route's 16 luminance strikes × sub-pixel variants exceed it: M5 = 176 ms at 16 colours, profile = `MTLTR_DrawGlyphList` + AGX texture-upload compression. M0 is immune only because one strike's glyphs fit. **M5 is not a viable hybrid without a larger Metal glyph cache** (the same 256-cell cache is why §11.9 built `SKGlyphCache` for the Skia pipe).
4. **`SKGlyphCache` under per-luminance strikes** (T5: 11.6 → 21 → 52 ms at 1 / 16 / 64 colours): cells multiply by class × sub-pixel variant and the atlas evicts; same nature as 3, better capacity. Sizing per class is a follow-up.
5. `aci` on the Skia-scaler arms (T5 12.5 ms vs M0 7.7 at 2820 glyphs) is the known `StandardGlyphVector.getDefaultStrike(luminance)` thrash (follow-up list, 2026-10-23).

**D. RenderPerfTest text tests (`-e=volatile -t`, caller ms per frame, 3000 strings of 43 chars with random colours):**
| test | M0 | M5 | T5 | T1 | T1+sc64 | FS | FS+sc64 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TextGray | 4.2 | 4.2 | 5.9 | 73.6 | 16.2 | 89.6 | 36.2 |
| LargeTextGray | 1.8 | 2.0 | 3.1 | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.3 | 1.3 |
| WhiteTextGray | 3.9 | 3.8 | 5.0 | 9.8 | 10.0 | 29.9 | 30.1 |
| TextNoAA | 4.2 | 4.0 | 5.6 | 12.9 | 13.4 | 33.4 | 34.3 |
| TextLCD | 4.2 | 4.3 | 5.9 | 73.1 | 18.5 | 86.0 | 36.1 |
M5's caller time hides its flusher thrash (the buffer does not fill at Metal's encode size); T5 is +40 % on the caller (the flusher's slower glyph upload surfaces through the full buffer); T1 is 2.5–3× M0 in this regime; large text favours Skia's text path (1.0 vs 1.8: fewer, bigger glyphs; Skia's path has no per-glyph tile work).

**E. Memory and startup (TextCallBench):** RSS after 250 frames 348–355 MB on every arm for plain strings (≈ 500 MB with attributed strings, all arms); the first frame 940–1000 ms on every arm (font-manager and pipeline initialisation dominate; ICU/shaper/paragraph initialisation is not visible at this granularity). dylib growth from skshaper/skparagraph/skunicode/icu still unmeasured (§13.3).

**F. Defaults and knobs applied the same afternoon (user's decisions; commits `dfca3912ca2a` Skia budgets, `e90c09f64c16` Metal glyph cache, `eda9990f9024` luminance classes).** Skia's strike cache 64 MB by default (`sun.java2d.skia.strikeCache`, 0 = Skia's 2 MB); `SKGlyphCache` 2048 texels a side under per-luminance strikes, 1024 otherwise (`sun.java2d.skia.glyphCache=<texels>`); Graphite's glyph atlas at Skia's 8 MB unless `sun.java2d.skia.glyphAtlas=<MB>`; Metal's glyph caches 2048 texels under per-luminance strikes (`FontUtilities.textLuminanceDependent`, read through JNI in `MTLOptions`), 512 otherwise (`sun.java2d.metal.glyphCacheSize`, which existed: 1024 already cuts M5 from 168 to 31 ms, 2048 reaches M0's 10 ms, 4096 adds nothing). `sun.java2d.skia.text.luminanceClasses` = 1, 2, 4, … 256 (default 16; 256 exact; **1 = the cg route as before the classes existed**: dependence off, every mask at `cgLuminance`, on both sides) — it does not touch Skia's own text path's colour keying. Tests: `SkiaStrikeCacheBudgetTest` (three budgets × default / cg / explicit), `MetalGlyphCacheSizeTest`, `LuminanceClassCountTest` (mapping and count on both sides, 1 / 4 / 16 / 256 / invalid). **Verified at 3000 × 43 with the defaults only:** 16 colours M0 9.8 / M5 10.2 / T5 14.0 / T1 18.4 / FS 40.1; 64 colours M5 10.3 / T5 14.0 / T1 22.5 / FS 43.8; FP churn frame 3.5 ms. Classes 1 / 4 / 16 equal at 64 colours (T5 13.7, T1 22); **256 classes overflow the 2048-texel `SKGlyphCache` again (T5 57.7)** — the cache defaults are sized for 16. TextRender directory 24/24. **Rule agreed with the user:** whatever extension of another backend needs larger text caches (the hybrid, luminance-keyed masks) gets them, so every arm is measured at its best. **Follow-up:** measure the caches' efficiency (hit rates, evictions, texture memory) on the real IntelliJ performance tests before fixing the final numbers.

**G. The cache keys, settled (user's question).** Our cg route keys a strike by (font, font matrix, device transform, style, AA, FM, **luminance class**), the class = Rec.601 luminance of the colour quantized to `textLuminanceClasses`; glyph images by (glyph code, sub-pixel variant); the GPU atlas by the glyph image. Hue never enters: red and an equal-luminance gray share one strike. Skia's own text path keys `SkScalerContextRec` by (typeface, size, matrix, hinting/sub-pixel flags, mask format, gamma and contrast constants, **`fLumBits` = `SkMaskGamma::CanonicalColor(paint colour)`**, the RGB triple at 3 bits per channel = 512 values — a *colour*, despite the setter's name `setLuminanceColor`), glyphs by packed id (glyph, sub-pixel quarters), the Graphite atlas by (strike, packed id). Under our cg route inside Skia's strike the proxy typeface *is* the class and `ignorePreBlend` zeroes `fLumBits`. The pre-blend holds one table per channel; an LCD mask uses all three (the 9 bits matter), an A8 mask one of them (green, in Skia's sources as remembered — `SkScalerContext.cpp` is not in the prebuilt), so for grayscale Skia's key distinguishes up to 64× more strikes than there are distinct masks. **Caveat found on the way:** "coverage" on Skia's own text path (T1n) is Skia-pre-blended per colour, not Java's linear coverage; a sheet comparison before anyone treats it as such. **Why key on colour at all:** gamma-correct text needs a correction that is non-linear in coverage and depends on the text colour (and ideally the destination); a fixed-function blend is linear in coverage and per-vertex colour cannot change that, so either the coverage is pre-distorted per colour in the mask (Skia, FreeType's and Apple's CPU heritage: no destination read, portable to any GPU and CPU blitter), or the shader reads the destination (the JDK's LCD shaders copy the destination region per glyph; our Skia backend's LCD blend uses framebuffer fetch on Apple GPUs; JBR's Metal grayscale gamma shader likewise works without a per-colour mask). Keying on colour is the price of the first design; the second pays per pixel instead.

**H. Round b, 2026-10-26 13:00 — the same round on the image with the bucket caches, the 64 MB strike cache and the 2048-texel caches under luminance strikes (`build/skia-perf/text-2026-10-26b/round-b.txt`; device scale 2, so 13 pt = 26-px glyphs).** *Caveat:* it ran during a video call; frames under 2 ms (A1–A3, L) are inflated by wake-up latency — M0 +16 %, the Skia text-path arms 2–3× — while the 10–40 ms frames are within 5 % of round a. The small-frame rows are to be re-run on a quiet machine before they are quoted.
| ms per frame | M0 | M5 | T5 | T1 | FS | FP |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A4 3000 × 43, one colour | 9.6 | 8.9 | 11.9 | 16.2 | 38.8 | — |
| C1 3000 × 43, 16 colours | 9.9 | **10.6** (was 176) | **14.0** (was 21) | 19.1 | 40.9 | — |
| C2 3000 × 43, 64 colours | 10.0 | **10.7** (was 182) | **14.2** (was 52) | **23.0** (was 78) | **46.1** (was 102) | — |
| L 400 × 12 at 20 pt (40 px) | **1.11** (was 7.3) | 1.14 | **1.20** (was 14) | 1.69 | 3.67 | — |
| L 400 × 12 at 32 pt (64 px) | **1.14** (was 25) | 1.19 | **1.26** (was 24) | 1.83 | 3.65 | — |
| B churn 940 glyph images | 2.55 | **0.60** (was 2.39) | **0.71** (was 2.39) | **1.27** (was 2.38) | 1.71 | 1.62 (aci) |
| B churn 2820 glyph images | 9.2 | 8.7 | 7.3 | **3.7** (was 6.3) | 4.1 | **3.6** (aci; was 33) |
| D RenderPerfTest TextGray / LargeTextGray | 4.9 / 1.17 | 5.2 / 1.36 | 6.9 / 1.02 | 21.3 / 1.28 | 43.1 / 1.65 | — |
| D10 (resolved 2026-10-29: the mirror is kept between operations — write locks counted through a wrapped `Lock`, Java-side writes through the state tracker read in `markDirty`; INT_ARGB AA rects 1.9 → 1.1× the loops; the out-conversion per operation remains) | **Unpremultiplied (INT_ARGB) images pay a conversion per operation on the raster surface** — a whole-image premultiplied mirror as the destination (slice 2) and a premultiplied copy of the source rectangle as a filtered image source (slice 5); premultiplying before filtering is the correct rule (the loops do it; Skia decodes into premul for the same reason), *where* the cost lives is the decision (2026-10-28, §13.8) | ≈ 0.65 ns per touched pixel in and out per operation; small fills on INT_ARGB at 2× the loops; INT_ARGB is, as far as known, the IDE's dominant BufferedImage format (`ImageUtil.createImage`, `JBHiDPIScaledImage`) | a premultiplied shadow per image invalidated through the surface's state tracker (converted once per change, not per draw); NEON for the loops; steering the IDE's image factories to INT_ARGB_PRE where it owns the image | after an IDE census of BufferedImage types and draw counts per frame (editor, project view, settings); re-measure with `RasterShapesBench` type 2 against type 3 |
Readings: (1) the cache work holds under the full matrix — M5 is back at M0's cost with colours, T5's colour penalty is gone, large text costs what small text does on every arm; (2) **churn**: the churn bench's sizes 16–18 pt at 2× were over the cell before, so every Skia-scaler arm drops from 2.4 to 0.6–0.7 ms at 940 images; **M0 does not** (2.55) because the Metal pipe without luminance strikes keeps the 512-texel default = 256 cells of 32 px, below a 752-image working set — the one remaining cache-size decision on Metal (raise the plain default to 1024 or 2048 as well); at 2820 images the Skia *text path* arms (T1, FS, FP) are the fastest at 3.6–4.1 ms against 7–9 for the glyph-image arms: Skia's skyline-packed atlas and 64 MB strike cache absorb churn that the cell caches thrash on; (3) A3 FP's caller time fell from 1.83 to 0.32 ms with the total at 6.3 (TextLayout's work is off the caller and SkParagraph's layout is on the flusher) — round a's 1.83 / 3.12 does not match this and is to be re-measured with the rest of the small frames; (4) the big-frame ordering for plain text is unchanged: M0 ≈ M5 < T5 < T1 < FS.

**Reading against the expectations:** M5 vs M0 — not "small": the Metal glyph cache makes the hybrid unusable with luminance strikes (3). T5 vs M5 — the renderer itself is within 1.3× on the caller at RenderPerfTest's shape and equal in the churn bench; the Skia pipe's per-class atlas sizing is the item (4). F vs T5 — shaping is a real, bounded per-call cost (≈ 0.17 µs per character, ≈ 7 µs per 43-char string; nothing measurable for 12-char strings), SkParagraph beats TextLayout by 2× on the flusher once the strike cache is sized, and the strike cache default is the one thing that must change before any F-arm number is representative. **Pending:** measurement 1 (IDE scenes, on screen, needs the user's go-ahead and ≈ 190 s × arms × n ≥ 5) and 5 (power; `powermetrics` needs sudo).

## 20. Windows VM session (2026-10-15/16) — raw record; merged 2026-10-16 into §0, §10, §11.18, §12, §13, §14

- **2026-10-15 (VM, afternoon)** — WP 2.2 step 1, toolchain. Guest: Windows 11 Pro ARM64 (26200) in Parallels; GPU `Parallels Display Adapter (WDDM 2.0)`, `dxdiag`: **feature levels ≤ 11_1, DDI 12, no D3D12** → Dawn on D3D11 only, as expected. Already installed: **Visual Studio Community 2026** 18.8 (toolset 14.51, Hostarm64/arm64, SDK 10.0.26100) — accepted by `toolchain_microsoft.m4` (`VALID_VS_VERSIONS="2022 2019 2026"`), so no VS 2022 Build Tools were needed; Microsoft OpenJDK 25.0.3 arm64 as boot JDK; an unused MSYS2. Installed via winget: Cygwin (`Cygwin.Cygwin`; packages are added with the `cygwinsetup` alias from `Cygwin.CygwinSetup`, there is no `setup-x86_64.exe` in `C:\cygwin64`) + `autoconf make zip unzip`. Clone `D:\Projects\JetBrainsRuntime` (local disk, fork as `origin`), branch jbr25 checked out. `configure --with-conf-name=windows-aarch64 --with-toolchain-version=2026 --with-debug-level=release` → native `Hostarm64\arm64\cl.exe` 19.51; **`make images` succeeded without `--with-skia`**, image at `build/windows-aarch64/images/jdk` runs (`25.0.4-internal`). Only noise in the log: boot-JDK CDS `addexports` mismatch messages. `bin/idea.sh -c windows-aarch64` generated the IDEA project (fixes the missing Git mapping); IDEA 2026.2.3 + MCP connected. - **2026-10-15 (VM) — decision (→ §10): Java host-surface seam.** `SKSurfaceData` becomes abstract in `share/classes` (pipes, `validatePipe`, LCD rule, flush/dispose protocol stay; host-owned bounds/destination/transparency/scales/replacement/native resource/`isOnScreen` go to subclasses); new interface `SKGraphicsConfig` in `share/classes` (precedent: `OGLGraphicsConfig` implemented by `CGL`/`WGL`/`GLX` configs) implemented by `MTLGraphicsConfig` and `Win32SKGraphicsConfig`; the portable Skia classes move to `share/classes/sun/java2d/skia/`. Names, by the §10 rule applied per half: **Java = windowing layer, native = GPU API** — macOS `MTLSKSurfaceData` + `MTLSKPlatform.mm` (both `MTL`), Windows `Win32SKGraphicsConfig`/`Win32SKSurfaceData` (like `Win32VKGraphicsConfig`) + `DawnSKPlatform.cpp`/`DawnSKEnvironment.cpp` (Dawn is the API Skia runs on; D3D11 is Dawn's backend and the Java side never sees it). Reason the split happens before the Windows compile check: the JNI headers of seven Skia classes are generated only where their Java sources are built. Also: Dawn's `webgpu_cpp.h` needs C++20 (`std::span`) → `-std:c++20` on Windows in `lib-skia.m4`.

- **2026-10-15 (VM, night) — the Dawn window surface presents.** Root cause of the `E_ACCESSDENIED`: DXGI allows one flip-model swap chain per HWND; AWT replaces the window `SurfaceData` on every bounds change (5× during pack/show) while the previous `DawnSKSurface` still owns its swap chain; Dawn escalates DXGI's refusal to a **lost device**. Probe (now removed) confirmed: first creation `S_OK` for FLIP_DISCARD/FLIP_SEQUENTIAL/DISCARD, later ones `0x80070005` for both flip models, `S_OK` for legacy DISCARD. Fix: the swap chain belongs to the HWND — a new window surface takes over the `wgpu::Surface` of the surface it replaces (per-HWND registry under a mutex) and reconfigures it; `SurfaceCapabilities` are queried (usages 0x17, 4 formats incl. BGRA8, FIFO, Opaque) and the instance is ticked after `Configure` so Dawn errors are logged. Result: SkiaRender window on screen (`skia-window-skiatop.png`, frame `alwaysOnTop` so the capture is not IDEA), no errors, resize + 40-repaint loop clean. **Open: insets** — the texture spans the frame bounds and Java paints at the inset offset, so the client area shows the content shifted by title bar/border with a black strip bottom/right; D3D9 solves this with `xoff/yoff` on Present — mirror it (client-sized swap chain, copy from the inset offset). Patterns to adopt from the other backends (recorded after reading `D3DScreenUpdateManager`, `Win32VK*`, `MTLLayer.m`): a `Win32SKScreenUpdateManager` owning window surfaces + swap chains (instead of the native registry), D3D9's lost/restore/GDI-fallback protocol for Dawn device-lost and `GetCurrentTexture` `Lost|Outdated`, presentation off the flusher thread (D3D9 updater / Metal display link) before frame-time measurements.

Insets: the Java peer's `getInsets()` is still null when the first window surfaces are created during `pack()`, and `AwtComponent_GetInsets` is not exported from `awt.dll` (D3D9/WGL use it from inside libawt), so `DawnSK_CreateSurface` derives them from the HWND (`ClientToScreen` origin minus `GetWindowRect` origin — what `AwtFrame::GetInsets` computes) → texture spans the window bounds (646×407), swap chain = `GetClientRect` (620×336), present copies from the inset offset (13,58). Status board: **WP 2.2 "VM / no Vulkan" row: initializes ✔, renders off-screen ✔ (973/168/11 vs Metal-Skia), presents ✔; frame time / pipeline-compile startup cost still to measure.**

Metal never had this: `startRedraw` is asynchronous, the display link presents once per frame.** That is the "presentation off the flusher thread" item (D3D9 updater / MTLLayer display link) — now the first-order performance fix on Windows, ahead of any rendering tuning. Next: non-blocking present mode as a quick check, then a presenter that coalesces to one present per vblank.

Cause: both parallelograms of one paint went into one mesh, and a runtime blender reads the destination — Metal through framebuffer fetch (per fragment, in order), Dawn/D3D11 through a **copy of the destination taken before the draw**, so overlapping quads of one draw all see the pre-draw pixels. `SKQuadBatch::begin` now draws the pending quads first whenever the paint has a runtime blender (XOR, LCD): one draw per primitive there. **Matrix on Dawn vs the Mac's analytic image: 973 pass / 179 coverage-level / 0 suspect.** Rule for §15/§14: a dst-reading blender must not share a draw across primitives that may overlap; glyph batches are per string already. The frame sits at ≈ 16.7 ms (1000 per-colour ring draws, 16k quads), so the Robot-timed median flips between 60 and 40 — the earlier 59 was the lucky side. A non-quantised measure (CPU/GPU frame time) is needed before optimising this; candidates are the per-colour draws (merging colours for plain quads was measured in the same bistable zone and is therefore undecided, not "slower").

RenderPerfTest's `TextLCD` (1000 strings, 1000 colours) is the synthetic worst case; IDE frames have a handful of text colours. Settle with the Windows IDE typing scene (§19) before changing anything; options if needed: colour as a second vertex attribute via `SkMesh` (not in Graphite m156), or a per-colour coverage-atlas draw with `SkBlendMode` arithmetic instead of a runtime blender.

    | variant | 100 strings | 500 strings |
    |---|---|---|
    | software loops (reference) | — | 8.2 |
    | **no split** (round-2 state, wrong at overlaps) | 7.8 | 11.1 |
    | per-quad split (round 3) | 1214 | 5836 |

    Reading: every dst-reading draw costs ≈ 80–90 µs on Dawn/D3D11 in this VM regardless of size (Graphite ends the render pass, copies the destination, starts a new pass — `DstReadStrategy::kTextureCopy`), and in LCD text **almost every adjacent glyph pair overlaps by construction**: the LCD glyph images carry the filter's 1-px padding on both sides, so 100 × 84 glyphs ≈ 8500 draws → 0.77 s per frame. Tolerating an overlap of ≤ 2 px (the padding) cut it to 23/89 ms but is a heuristic with residual errors (3 ink pixels missing, one pixel at max 255 on the probe) and still 3–8× the no-split frame — measured, **not committed**. The `lcdBlender` applies gamma (`pow(dst, gamma)`, mix, `pow` back; contrast 140 by default), so an exact dst-free decomposition (kModulate + kPlus passes) is not available; a dst-free LCD would be approximate in the gamma term. **Decision needed (user / §14):** on backends without framebuffer fetch, LCD text is either correct and ≈ 100× slower than GDI (as now), or batched per list and wrong where glyphs overlap (round 2), or rendered through another path (grayscale fallback for LCD requests on Dawn; an approximate two-pass LCD without dst read; Skia's own LCD text with dual-source blending, which would bypass Java's glyph images). The per-list split costs nothing on Metal (framebuffer fetch) and nothing for grayscale text anywhere; XOR is unaffected in practice (few primitives).
  - **R1 done as its own commit — `6046b77c224 JBR-10654 Skia: one Dawn device per adapter, shared by the configurations`** on **`…-jbr25-windows4`** (fork): `DawnSK_CreateConfig` keeps `std::shared_ptr<DawnSKDevice>` per backend under a mutex, reuses it unless `lost`, traces "new device"/"shared device". `build/skia-smoke/DisplayChangeProbe.java` drives `Win32GraphicsDevice.displayChanged()` twice: three configurations, one device, and a blit from an image of configuration 0 into one of configuration 2 draws (red centre); with the environment-level `displayChanged()` the old image is invalidated by the change itself (`IMAGE_INCOMPATIBLE`, contents gone), as intended by the volatile managers. `SKPlatform_SameDevice` is now true across display changes on Windows.
- **2026-10-19 (VM, evening) — LCD text in two passes without a destination read (user decision: try it; `ccdc9cde999` on `…-jbr25-windows4`).** New knob `sun.java2d.skia.lcd=blend|twopass` in `SKEnvironment.Configuration` (default `twopass` unless the GPU API is `metal`; `defaultsFor(gpuApi)` finally uses its parameter). `twopass`: `SKQuadBatch` draws each LCD batch twice (`SKQuadBatchTwoPass`: kModulate with `1 - cov'`, kPlus with `colour * cov'`), the shaders are runtime *shaders* over the atlas image shader (no dst read, no render-pass break, so per-list batching returns; the batch is keyed by the paint's colour), the uncached LCD glyph path draws two rects. Gamma: `cov'` is the linear weight that reproduces the gamma blend (`contrast/100`) on white and on black, mixed by the channel's text value (variant B; variant A, a step at 0.5, was worse on gray-on-dark).
  - **Speed (LcdTextBench, 1×):** 100 / 500 lines: **8.4 / 14.0 ms** two-pass vs 714 / 3983 ms blend (software loops 8.2 at 500; grayscale AA 7.1 / 9.4). The LCD probe is unchanged at 1789 px / max 2 (white background: exact). Matrix 974 / 178 / 0, window 0 differing, both in the new default.
  - **Fidelity (`build/skia-smoke/LcdBackgrounds.java`, 6 backgrounds × 6 text colours, vs the software loops, max / mean channel difference on text pixels):** exact (≤ 2–4, as `blend`) for black text on every background, white on white/black/pale, red on white/black; **white or gray187 text on gray128 / darcula43 / navy: max 13–15, mean 6–10** (text reads slightly heavier or lighter); orange on black max 9. `blend` is ≤ 4 everywhere. The residual is inherent to a dst-free gamma blend (DirectWrite and Skia's own text accept the same class of error); the IDE dark-theme case (187 on 43) is max 10 / mean 6.
  - **Research, Skia API for dual-source blending:** Graphite's `DawnCaps` sets `fDualSourceBlendingSupport` from `wgpu::FeatureName::DualSourceBlending` and `fFBFetchSupport` from `FeatureName::FramebufferFetch` (feature-flag driven, no backend special cases); `Caps::getDstReadStrategy()` is a two-way branch, framebuffer fetch if `fFBFetchSupport` else texture copy, with a TODO (b/238757201, b/383769988) that other strategies are not wired; nothing selects an input-attachment read for Vulkan in main. Dual-source blending is used internally for Skia's own LCD text only; no public API (`SkBlender`, `SkRuntimeEffect`, `drawVertices`/`drawAtlas`) exposes a second colour output, and **no feature request or skia-discuss thread asking for it was found** (searched issues.skia.org, skia-discuss, GitHub). Dawn itself exposes `dual-source-blending` as an adapter feature. Conclusion: a dual-source path would need a Skia change (a `blend_src1`-style output for runtime shaders plus Graphite pipeline support); the two-pass is the available equivalent and does the same arithmetic in two draws.

Marginal per 1000 lines of 84 glyphs: software 11.4 ms, two-pass LCD 13.5 ms (≈ 20 % slower per glyph than the CPU loops on this VM's paravirtualised GPU), grayscale on Skia 6.9 ms (≈ 40 % faster). The LCD shader evaluates two `pow` per channel in each pass: a per-colour LUT or a cheaper fit would move it toward the grayscale cost (open, §13). VM hand-over to the Mac closed with this entry.
- **2026-10-19 (VM, late) — Round 5: the overlap split, measured and replaced by layers.** Clone reset to `55e2af845be` (the Mac's split, eviction fix and contrast key folded in; `…-windows4` deleted). **The per-overlap split on Dawn (two-pass, `LcdTextBench … lcd cheap`): 125 / 409 / 760 ms for 100 / 500 / 1000 lines** (no split 2.7 / 8.9 / 14.2; software 1.4 / 5.8 / 11.7): a draw pair per glyph, 4–5 µs per draw even without a destination copy. Both jtreg tests pass on Dawn as shipped (`LcdTextBackgroundsTest` blend max 7 / twopass max 24, `GlyphAtlasEvictionTest` gray max 0 / LCD max 2, no ink missing).
  - **Fix (fixup to the two-pass commit, on `…-jbr25-windows5`): layers instead of flushes.** `SKQuadBatch` keeps a base mesh and further `Mesh` layers while `splitOnOverlap`: a quad that overlaps a pending quad goes to the first layer where it overlaps nothing (a new one if needed) instead of ending the batch; the layers are drawn in order after the base, each with the one draw or pass pair. Within a layer nothing overlaps, so a pass pair composes correctly; a quad is always drawn after everything it overlaps. Draw count scales with overlap depth, not glyph count. Overlap extents live in a hashed 64-px grid (64 buckets, 16-bit indices) so a test touches the nearby quads only; the base is drawn at 1024 quads. **Overlap extent = the glyph's ink** (pixels above 8/255 in any channel, tight bounds computed once per cell at upload, `SKGlyphCache::ink`) for two passes: a pixel without coverage is multiplied by 1 and gets 0 added. **For a destination-reading blender the whole quad counts**: on the texture-copy backends a zero-coverage fragment writes the stale copy back, so ink-only extents erased neighbouring glyphs (the shipped `LcdTextBackgroundsTest` in blend mode caught it at max 255; fixed before commit).
  - **Numbers (Dawn, 1×, cheap sync), ms per frame for 100 / 500 / 1000 lines:** two-pass **4.2 / 14.3 / 25.8**; blend 5.1 / 26.7 / —; grayscale 1.7 / — / 7.7; software 1.4 / 5.8 / 11.7. 90 non-overlapping lines (a 2×45 grid): software 1.3, two-pass 3.8, blend 4.8, grayscale 1.7. The bench's 1000-line layout stacks lines on each other (depth ≈ 20), which is why 1000 lines cost more than the no-split 14.2; real text has depth 1–2. Blend mode on Dawn gains the same layering (714 → 5.1 ms at 100 lines), so the XOR and Metal-less blender paths profit too. **Note for Metal:** under framebuffer fetch the blender needs no split at all (in-order per fragment); `splitOnOverlap` for the blender could be gated on a platform capability (a draw per overlap group is cheap, but the layering work is wasted there).
  - `LcdTextBackgroundsTest` extended with a third copy (two overlapping lists → three layers): passes, blend max 9, twopass max 25 on Dawn. `DisplayChangeProbe` on the final branch: new device, then shared device twice, cross-configuration blit draws. Matrix 974 / 178 / 0, window 0 differing, LCD probe max 2 unchanged. Open: the two `pow` per channel per pass (LUT), and the Metal gating above.
- **2026-10-20 (VM) — LCD coverage table, accumulate strategy, blender split gated on framebuffer fetch** (topical commit on `…-jbr25-windows5`, after the layered-split fixup). Interleaved bench on Dawn (`LcdTextBench … cheap`, ms per frame; "stacked" = the 1000-line layout ≈ 20 glyph lists deep, "grid" = 90 non-overlapping lines):

    | mode | 1000 stacked | 90 grid |
    |---|---|---|
    | software loops | 11.9 | 1.3 |
    | grayscale on Skia | 8.0 | 1.8 |
    | two-pass, power functions (yesterday) | 25.8 | 4.2 |
    | **two-pass, table** | 25.4 | 3.8 |
    | **accumulate** | 13.7 | 2.6 |
    | blend (dst copy, layered) | 47 | 4.7 |

  - **Table:** `lcdTable(colour, contrast)` builds a 256×1 RGBA image (per-channel corrected coverage) once per colour and contrast, cached per context (64 entries); the pass shader samples it three times instead of six `pow`. Helps at realistic depth (4.2 → 3.8), nothing at depth 20 (draw count dominates there).
  - **Accumulate** (`sun.java2d.skia.lcd=accumulate`): the LCD cache's batch draws into a destination-sized scratch surface (`cc->lcdCoverage`, RGBA opaque) with kScreen (union of coverages = sequential same-colour compositing), `beforeDraw` clears the batch bounds, `afterDraw` composites them in the two table passes; no layers, no split (kScreen is fixed-function and ordered). Two draws + a clear per batch regardless of glyph count or depth. **Fastest LCD variant and the most faithful at overlaps** (`LcdTextBackgroundsTest` max 15 vs two-pass 25; LCD probe max 2; eviction test max 2). Cost: a scratch texture per context (destination size, grows, never shrinks), the composite fill over the batch bounds (two passes), and `mergeColors` off for a batch with an `afterDraw` hook (its paint keys the colour for the composite; a first version merged colours into white and composited white text: the new jtreg run was red, then green). Not the default; the user decides.
  - **Blender gate:** `SKQuadBatch::splitBlenders` (set from `SKBackend_FramebufferFetch()`, which the Java side feeds from `framebufferFetchNative()`): with framebuffer fetch a runtime blender's quads stay one draw; without it (Dawn, Metal on Intel/AMD) they layer as before. Compiled here with the gate off (Dawn); the Metal half is a Java-only change (`configureNative(msaa, edgeAA, lcdMode, framebufferFetch)`).
  - Verified in the default (`twopass`): matrix 974 / 178 / 0, window 0 differing; jtreg LCD test blend max 9 / twopass max 25 / accumulate max 15, eviction gray 0 / lcd 2 / accumulate 2.

- **2026-10-20 (VM, night) — the "accumulate keyword-colour defect" RETRACTED: it was IDE state, not compositing.** Bisecting inside the backend with the IDE scene as oracle: flushing the accumulate batch after every glyph list changed nothing; a focused trace of every glyph list, clear and composite touching the word's pixel box showed the IDE itself requesting `public` in gray `6F737A` (the "redundant/unused" attribute) under accumulate and in keyword orange under two-pass, with an extra teal inline hint only under accumulate, i.e. different highlighting state, and the composites faithfully following the requests. The one per-arm state the runner kept between runs was the IDE's `system` directory (indexes, caches); after wiping both arms' system directories the accumulate run requests orange like the others and the two captures agree pixel for pixel at the word (0 beyond 32). `run-scenes.sh` now wipes `system` per run (≈ 1 min re-indexing, inside the existing smart-mode wait). Consequences: accumulate has **no known rendering defect**; its sheet results (max 15 at overlaps, better than two-pass) and its speed stand; the A3 columns of today's matrix tables are invalid and the matrix is rerunning from identical IDE state for all arms. The probes written for the hunt (`SpanProbe`, `SpanWindowProbe`, `AccumulateProbe`, `EvictProbe`, `RepaintProbe`, `LcdColorRunsTest`) moved to `build/text-quality/probes/`; all were green because there was nothing to find. Lesson for the instrument: every per-arm persistent directory is a hidden variable; the runner must start each arm from byte-identical state, and a rendering difference seen in exactly one arm should first be checked against the IDE's own op stream (the trace did it in one run) before any backend hypothesis.

Against yesterday: two-pass 1000 lines 25.4 → 29.6 (the last-overlapping placement deepens the layering at depth 20), accumulate 13.7 → 14.5, blend 47 → 57; at realistic depth (90 grid) unchanged within noise.
  - **Tab edge "bleed" (user observation) confirmed and traced to a shape-pipe bug, fixed (`…-jbr25-windows6`, `JBR-10654 Skia: antialiased fills are not normalized`, with `AnalyticShapeEdgeTest`, red 105–234 px per round-rect/ellipse fill → green):** in the 200 % captures the pixel right of the active tab's border is `1e283a` on every Skia arm vs background `191a1c` in software (light: `f1f5ff` vs `ffffff`): exactly 50 % of the tab's *fill* colour, on the right and bottom edges only; top and left are identical. The analytic shape pipe snapped the edges of antialiased *fills* to pixel centres (`floor + 0.5`, the stroke rule) while Java normalizes strokes only (`AAShapePipe.renderPath`: `adjust = bs != null && !PURE`); the measured software rule for AA fills is "no snapping at all" (a fractional edge covers the outer pixel by its fraction, NORMALIZE or PURE). Plain rects were unaffected (parallelogram pipe). Residual, not fixed: stroked analytic ovals differ from Marlin's cubic-ellipse stroke by up to 18 levels (70 px at 2×) and stroked rects by 28 levels at 8 join pixels — Q8/Q12 class, the test allows 32 for strokes. The render matrix is unchanged (974 / 178 / 0 vs the Mac's analytic image, which carries the same bug; the Mac's software column should move on its next run).

  - **Analytic strokes, three routes measured (2×, 1 px stroke, max levels vs the loops):** analytic `drawRRect`/`drawOval` stroke (in tree) rrect 14–55 by radius, oval 60×36 up to 80; the Skia path route for strokes (`sktemp.noAnalyticStroke`) worse, 66–110; Java's own stroker (`createStrokedShape`) filled by Skia rrect 21–37, oval 33 — the best fidelity but a Java stroker pass per draw. Both experiments reverted; decision open (§13). The committed test now also has a 56×36 "bigellipse" (stroke max 20–22 at 2×, 3–4 at 1×), stroke tolerances unchanged.
  - **Light 2× clusters: no new rendering class.** Every top cluster (toolbar run widget, window title text, status-bar warning icon, wavy inspection underlines, error-stripe marks, the progress spinner) is the software arm captured while the inspection pass was still running: the runner waited for `exit dumb mode` + 15 s, the analysis and the run widget come later. Runner settle after dumb mode 15 → 60 s. The terminal scene now runs a fixed `echo` (the `dir` listing carried timestamps). The 100 % matrix (A0/A1/A2 × light/dark) is recapturing with the fixed build.

  - **Curved-edge class quantified (`build/text-quality/probes/ArcCoverageProbe.java`, filled circle r = 100 at a quarter-pixel offset, 1×, Dawn vs loops):** max 19 levels; per 15° sector the mean over differing pixels is 7–8 on the diagonals (30–60°, 210–240°) and 4 on the axes; the single largest pixels (16–19) sit at the apexes (75–105°, 255–285°), where the edge is nearly tangent to a scan line and Marlin's 8 sub-scanlines quantize coverage in eighths. So the class has two inherent sources, one per renderer: Skia's distance-based coverage on diagonal edges, Marlin's sub-scanline quantization on near-tangent edges. Neither is a bug of the backend; the fill tolerance 8 in `AnalyticShapeEdgeTest` holds because the test's shapes are small (41×20, 56×36). The same 1 px stroked circle at 1× is pixel-identical (max 0).

  - **§3 luminance probe (`dwref --probe`, Segoe UI 13, per unit contrast): DirectWrite's weight depends on the text colour's luminance only** (black 62.8k on white and on mid-gray, white 72.4k on black and on mid-gray, mid-gray 70.8k/71.4k on white/black; monotone in luminance, white ≈ 15 % heavier than black in grayscale, 27 % in ClearType) — the same shape as CoreGraphics (text luminance, not paper), a different curve (DW removes ink from dark text and adds to light text relative to its own gamma-1 mode; CG adds for all). **GDI grayscale depends on the polarity only** (two groups: dark-on-light 63.2–63.7k, light-on-dark 91.1–91.8k, +44 %; mid-gray text falls into one group on white and the other on black); GDI ClearType polarity-dependent too, milder (≈ 10 %). **Consequence:** a per-luminance mask (the `SKPlatform_SmoothedGlyphMask` contract) reproduces DirectWrite by construction; GDI is a blend rule against the destination and needs a pipe-side gamma blend (what the JDK's LCD loops and the Metal shader are). Which look is "right" on Windows is a product question: DirectWrite = every modern app (and what Skia's own DirectWrite scaler context models), GDI = where the JDK's Windows LCD glyphs come from today.
  - **Native sheets:** 48 (DirectWrite via Direct2D on an opaque WIC bitmap at 96·scale dpi; GDI into a DIB), 20 rows, no kerning/ligatures, whole-pixel rows at Java's rounded advances in GDI-classic measuring mode. **Java arms on the VM:** `software`, `opengl-platform` (WGL works on the VM; D3D9 falls back to software, no Vulkan), `skia-platform`, `software-skia-scaler`, `skia-scaler-{coverage,darkened,smoothed}` — 9 arms × 24 sheets, viewer built (`opengl-platform`, `directwrite`, `gdi` added to `descriptions.json`).

So on Windows the Skia scaler's `coverage` route is **not plain coverage**: Skia's DirectWrite scaler context hands out masks 47 % heavier than FreeType's and 20 % heavier than DirectWrite's own grayscale (its luminance pre-blend at the default luminance, presumably); `smoothed` lands 9 % under DirectWrite. **Pixel for pixel against DirectWrite (gray/whole-pixel/1×, positions identical, best shift 0/0):** software 16.5k px beyond 32 / mean 58.6; skia-scaler-coverage 25.4k / 79.6; smoothed 21.5k / 57.8; GDI 24.4k / 96 — no Java arm is near the native rendering (the macOS `cg` route reached max 8); the Windows analogue is still to build.

Fractional advances agree to 1e-3.
