- **2026-10-23 (VM, morning) — follow-ups on the Windows text reference (user questions: the JDK's DirectWrite path as arms; why Skia's glyphs look shorter; system settings).**
  - **Where Windows LCD glyphs come from, measured:** for every font the JDK knows as installed (Segoe UI, Consolas; in the IDE also the bundled Inter and JetBrains Mono, which JBR registers with GDI), `FileFontStrike.useNatives` is true for LCD strikes at 3–100 px and the glyph images come from **GDI ClearType (`lcdglyph.c`), whatever scaler is selected** — the LCD ink of Segoe UI / Consolas is identical (148.7k / 137.4k) in `software`, `skia-platform`, `software-skia-scaler` and `skia-scaler-coverage`, fractional and whole-pixel alike. Only fonts created with `Font.createFont` (the sheets' Inter and JetBrains Mono files) reach the scaler for LCD: there Skia's LCD is 12 % heavier than FreeType's for Inter (177k vs 158k) and 17 % lighter for JetBrains Mono (151k vs 182k). Consequence: on Windows `sun.java2d.font.scaler=skia` changes LCD text of installed fonts through metrics only; its rasterization shows in grayscale text and in created fonts.
  - **JBR's opt-in DirectWrite glyph path (`-Ddirectwrite.font.rendering=true`, `lcdglyphDW.cpp`, options `measuring:rendering:clearTypeLevel:contrast:gamma:geometry`) as arms `software-dwlcd`, `skia-platform-dwlcd`, `software-dwlcd-gdiclassic`: broken on this image** — created fonts are unaffected (same as FreeType: it also needs `useNatives`), installed fonts (Segoe UI, Consolas) come out **smeared and overlapping** (ink 2.4× the GDI path's, 360k vs 149k; glyph images of wrong width or stride), with the default options and with GDI-classic measuring alike. Pre-existing JBR code, untested on this JDK 28 arm64 image; needs a look before it can serve as a reference arm (and it would be the natural home of a per-luminance DirectWrite mask route).
  - **"Skia's glyphs are one pixel shorter" — not in the sheets:** vertical ink profiles (ink per scan line of the Inter 13 and Segoe UI 13 regular rows, gray and LCD, 1× and 2×) show **the same extent in every arm** (lines 9–21 of the 28-px cell at 1×, 18–39 at 2×); the Skia scaler's rows are heavier per line (×1.4–1.5 in gray), not shorter. In the IDE the impression comes from the metrics finding: the scaler's leading 1 vs 0 and the Arial advance differences move text by a pixel, and the heavier masks change the apparent weight; hinting is the same everywhere in that all Java arms are unhinted vertically (`SkFontHinting::kNone`, FreeType light/no hinting for these fonts), while DirectWrite's natural mode snaps vertically — yet its extent matches too at these sizes.
  - **Knobs:** the scaler sets `kNone` hinting, linear metrics, sub-pixel positioning = fractional metrics, and rasterizes with `SkSurfaceProps` gamma 1.0 / contrast 0 (linear coverage). Two temporary environment knobs were added on the VM for the experiment (uncommitted `sktemp`): `SKIA_TEXT_HINTING=none|slight|normal|full` and `SKIA_TEXT_GAMMA=skia` (Skia's default contrast 0.5 / sRGB on the mask); results follow.
  - **System settings:** Java2D reads `SPI_GETFONTSMOOTHING/TYPE/CONTRAST/ORIENTATION` (`awt_DesktopProperties.cpp` → `awt.font.desktophints`: the default AA hint, LCD vs gray, RGB vs BGR) and `lcdglyph.c` linearises GDI's output with an inverse-gamma table from the system's FontSmoothingContrast; the LCD *blend* contrast (`KEY_TEXT_LCD_CONTRAST`, 140) is the JDK's own. The JBR DirectWrite path takes `IDWriteFactory::CreateRenderingParams()` (system gamma 1.8 / contrast 0.5 / level 1 / geometry) unless overridden. Skia's DirectWrite host asks DirectWrite for the recommended rendering mode and pixel geometry with the system parameters, but applies its own gamma/contrast model (the pre-blend from Skia's compile-time defaults, or none when the caller requests linear coverage as `SKFontScaler` does) — the system's gamma/contrast are **not** what Skia blends with; wiring them through would mean reading the rendering params and feeding them to the mask (or the pipes) ourselves.
  - **Experiment results (arms `skia-scaler-hint-slight`, `-hint-normal`, `-gamma-skia`, gray/fractional/1× Inter / JetBrains Mono / Segoe UI / Consolas ink):** hinting `slight` and `normal` are identical to each other (Skia's DirectWrite context maps both to the natural rendering mode) and change nothing for Inter (CFF outlines: 257.8k in all three), a little for the TrueType fonts (JetBrains Mono 235k → 248k, Segoe 190k → 191k, Consolas 176k → 173k); the vertical ink profiles are identical to `kNone` line for line. **`SKIA_TEXT_GAMMA=skia` changes not a single pixel**: the `SkSurfaceProps` gamma/contrast have no effect on the DirectWrite host's A8 masks in this path. So the 47 % extra weight over FreeType and 20 % over DirectWrite's own output is **in the alpha texture DirectWrite hands to Skia**: Skia's DirectWrite scaler context takes `IDWriteGlyphRunAnalysis::CreateAlphaTexture` with the factory's default rendering parameters (system gamma 1.8, enhanced contrast 0.5), i.e. a texture already weighted for DirectWrite's own blend, and we composite it as if it were linear coverage. **Path forward on Windows, concrete:** rasterize through DirectWrite ourselves behind the existing `SKPlatform_SmoothedGlyphMask`/coverage contract — `CreateGlyphRunAnalysis` with custom rendering parameters: gamma 1.0 / contrast 0 / level 0 for a true coverage route (what the pipes expect), and the system parameters plus DirectWrite's `GetAlphaBlendParams` ramp for the text luminance for the native-look route (the analogue of `cg`). Both are small (`dwref.cpp` already does the former in `--probe`), neither needs the pipes to change, and the second answers the hand-over's §3 by construction. Until then the Skia scaler's grayscale on Windows should not be judged against anything.
  - Viewer rebuilt over 15 arms (`text-reference-win/out/2026-10-22/viewer`); the temporary knobs are reverted in the tree (the VM image still carries them).
  - **Correction (2026-10-23, after the user's objection): hinting DOES change the rendered glyph height.** The scan-line *extent* is the same, the x-height and cap-height *edges* are not: with `kNone` the top of x and z straddles two lines at half coverage (JetBrains Mono 13: line 12 = 21, line 13 = 44 of 46 max), with `kSlight`/`kNormal` (DirectWrite natural mode) the edge is snapped to a full line one pixel higher (line 12 = 45) — the same snapping DirectWrite's own sheets show (its profiles match the hinted arm line for line), while FreeType in the JDK puts the edge lower still (line 12 = 6, line 13 = 38). So "Skia's glyphs are a pixel shorter than DirectWrite's" is right: DirectWrite snaps x-heights up, Skia with `kNone` does not, FreeType rounds them down. Cap heights snap the same way (E/H top line 10: 29 → 42). `XHeight.java` (VM, `/tmp/xh`) measures it; the earlier "same extent" statement in this entry is withdrawn.
  - **JBR DirectWrite glyph path, root cause of the "smear": DPI.** `lcdglyphDW.cpp` renders into an `IDWriteBitmapRenderTarget` from the GDI interop and never calls `SetPixelsPerDip`; the target's default pixels-per-DIP is the system DPI scale (2.0 on this 200 % display), so the glyph is rasterized at twice the requested pixel size into a bitmap sized for once, and the JDK places it at 1× advances. A one-line fix (`target->SetPixelsPerDip(1.0f)`, the JDK already passes device pixels as the em size) plus a jtreg test that renders with `directwrite.font.rendering=true` under a scaled display and compares glyph bounds with the GDI path. Pre-existing JBR bug on every HiDPI Windows display, not specific to our branch.
- **2026-10-23 (VM, midday) — JBR DirectWrite glyph path fixed (test first), and two corrections.** `…-windows8` + `JBR-10654 Windows: the DirectWrite glyph path renders at the strike's pixel size on scaled displays` (`lcdglyphDW.cpp`: `IDWriteBitmapRenderTarget::SetPixelsPerDip(1.0f)`; new `DirectWriteGlyphScaleTest`, red on the 200 % VM — H at 13 px came out 32×16 px for 25×9 — green after; the GDI run line guards the default path). Pre-existing JBR bug (JRE-11, 2024) on every scaled display.
  - **Correction 2: the leading difference is not rounding.** On Windows the FreeType scaler takes ascent/descent from the OS/2 `usWinAscent`/`usWinDescent` and sets height to their sum (`freetypeScaler.c`, `#if defined(_WIN32)`), so **leading is zero by construction** — GDI's convention (`tmExternalLeading` lives elsewhere); Skia's DirectWrite host reports the font's line gap (hhea/typo). Metrics are floats in both, no pixel rounding. The whole-pixel advance difference stands: FreeType rounds the *hinted* advance (`FT26Dot6ToIntRound(ftglyph->advance.x)` when fractional metrics are off), the Skia scaler rounds the linear one.
  - **Motivations found in the sources, for the user's questions:** `lcdglyph.c` header: GDI glyphs are used for LCD "for rendering fidelity for the most common cases, with no impact on the 2D rendering pipelines" (JDK 6, 2006: the JDK's own LCD rasterization and the system's ClearType tuning did not match what users saw in native apps); the `useNatives` conditions: unrotated, 3–100 px, installed TrueType, fractional metrics or the small-size "image with advance" path (the JDK overwrites GDI's integer advance with its own, see the comment in `FileFontStrike`). JBR added `freetype.font.rendering` (JBR-6671, 2024) to opt out, and the DirectWrite path (JRE-11, 2024) as an opt-in alternative; `JBR-1245` adjusted letter spacing in controls. None of these take the system's gamma/contrast beyond GDI's linearisation table and the desktop hints.
- **2026-10-23 (VM, afternoon) — the GDI override becomes a choice (user: "no implicit GDI override anymore; make the choices matter").** `…-windows8` + `JBR-10654 Windows: the source of LCD glyph images is a stated choice, not an override of the scaler` (`FileFontStrike`, `FontScaler`, new `LcdGlyphSourceTest` with seven run lines, three red before): **`sun.java2d.font.lcdGlyphs = gdi | directwrite | scaler`**; default `gdi` on the JDK's scaler (unchanged shipping look), **`scaler` whenever `sun.java2d.font.scaler=skia`**; `freetype.font.rendering` and `directwrite.font.rendering` stay as aliases; an unavailable Skia scaler is now a logged warning. Consequence for the record: **every IDE scene and every installed-font LCD sheet of 2026-10-22 measured GDI glyphs under the Skia scaler's name**; the sheets are re-rendered under `text-reference/out/2026-10-23` with every arm stating its source (new arms `software-scaler`, `skia-platform-scaler` = FreeType LCD glyphs for installed fonts), and the scenes A0 / T1 / T2 are recaptured. Also noted from `FileFontStrike`'s comments: JBR's DirectWrite glyph path deliberately asks for **gamma 1.0, enhanced contrast 0, natural-symmetric mode** so that the pipes apply the gamma — it is already the "DirectWrite as linear coverage" route of the plan, for LCD strikes of installed fonts.

The residual follows **`java2d.font.subpixelResolution`**: at the JBR default 4×1, Segoe UI / Consolas rows differ from native by max 37 (mean 10–17) while Inter matches (max 4); at **8×1 Segoe UI and Consolas match native within max 5 (mean 3)** while Inter moves to max 64; 16×1 is mixed; 1×1 is bad everywhere (max 115). The glyph masks are the platform's for all four families; what remains is **where** each glyph lands: Java quantizes glyph origins to 1/N px variants, DirectWrite's layout positions them on its own grid, and which N happens to coincide depends on the accumulated fractional positions of the row. This is the "pixel for pixel where positions coincide" caveat of the macOS report, now measured on Windows. Options: match DirectWrite's positioning grid (it quantizes sub-pixel positions itself; the grid to confirm), or accept ≤ 1/8 px placement noise. **Rasterization parity of the `platform` route is therefore established for all four families.** Experiment knobs reverted from the tree (the VM image still carries them until the next build).

  - **The 2× style-row residual is not the route: Direct2D renders some glyphs *inside a multi-glyph run* differently from the same glyph alone, from a display scale of 1.8 on.** The rows are real faces (Inter Italic / SemiBold / SemiBold Italic, JetBrains Mono Bold Italic files), not synthesized. Java's and DirectWrite's glyph origins are identical to 4 decimals (`Positions` probe vs `dwref --positions`); the pipe's variant pick is nearest, as DirectWrite's (`VariantPick`); the route's isolated glyph equals Direct2D's isolated glyph at the same fraction (`GlyphDump` vs `dwref --glyph`: 'c' italic at x.4375 identical). `dwref --runs` / `--prefix`: the layout's run (and a hand-built run with design advances) against one run per glyph at the same positions differ by exactly the sheet's pixels (Inter Italic 236 px, SemiBold 34, JetBrains Mono Bold Italic 49; Inter Regular and JetBrains Mono Regular 0) — in-run glyphs 'i c k f o x j u 5 7 8 } [ ] ( )' of the italic match **no** single-glyph rendering at any horizontal eighth-pixel offset in ±1 px nor any vertical one in ±0.5 px, the differing pixels are **not** where a neighbour's ink overlaps (0 of 19–27 per glyph), a two-glyph 'l l' run positions its second glyph by nearest rounding like a single glyph, and at 96 dpi the same runs differ by max 2–14 only. So Direct2D rasterizes certain glyphs of slanted faces differently as part of a run in the coarse regime; the mechanism is not identified. Java draws glyph by glyph and matches Direct2D's glyph-by-glyph output exactly; replicating a run-internal Direct2D effect would need run-level rasterization. **Left as a documented residual: 1–2 coverage steps (max 60–124) on 2–27 pixels per affected glyph, slanted faces, display scale ≥ 1.8, text drawn as runs by native applications.**

The JDK's own LCD sub-pixel positioning (`subPixPos`, `rowBytesOffset` in `DrawGlyphList.c` and the Skia pipe) shifts the glyph image by whole sub-pixels — 3 positions per pixel, no re-rasterization — which is why the LCD sheets never matched (mean 57–64 at every scale).   - On the question of a 3-step (sub-pixel-boundary) placement grid for LCD: that is exactly the JDK's existing LCD scheme (channel shift by thirds), and DirectWrite uses 6 (the boundaries and their midpoints); measured here against native, 3 steps cost mean 57–64, 6 steps give mean 3.
  - **2026-10-23 (VM, 23:30) — the 2× run-internal residual explained: Direct2D's overlap handling.** `dwref --runanalysis`: DirectWrite's own glyph run analysis of the whole italic run is **byte-identical** to one analysis per glyph (every glyph 0), so the rasterizer is not involved; it is Direct2D. `dwref --pairgap` (a two-glyph run against the two glyphs drawn singly, the advance stepping in half pixels): the difference exists **exactly while the first glyph's ink box reaches into the second glyph's** (Inter Italic 'fo': ink of 'f' ends 11.04 px right of its origin, 'o' starts 0.61 px right of its own; differences at advances up to 10.4 px, none from 10.9 on; 'ic', 'ck' likewise; Inter Regular 'fo' overlaps only below 7.7 px and its design advance is 9.38, hence zero in real text), and only at every other half-pixel phase of the second glyph (124, 0, 124, 0 …); at 96 dpi the same overlaps give max 3–63 only at the tightest spacings. So **Direct2D renders glyphs whose boxes overlap within a run through a different path (the one that keeps overlapping antialiased glyphs from blending twice), and in the coarse high-scale regime that path puts the glyph on the other half-pixel phase.** Italic faces overlap their neighbours' boxes by design; upright ones rarely. Java draws glyph by glyph, matches Direct2D's single-glyph output, and does not reproduce this; it is an artefact of Direct2D's overlap path, not a target. Closed.
  - **Sub-pixel variant ties (the JetBrains Mono 1.25× LCD residual):** `VariantPick` on both pipes: at an exact half-step (x.25 and x.75 on the sixth grid at 125 %) the software loops took x.25 **down** to x.1667 while the Skia pipe took it up to x.3333; DirectWrite's boundaries sit exactly on the half-steps and the half-step takes the upper position (ties up). The pipes' arithmetic (`coord + 0.5/res − 0.5`, then truncation) left exact ties to float rounding noise. Fix: a nudge of 1/4096 px in `ADJUST_SUBPIXEL_GLYPH_POSITION` (`DrawGlyphList.c`, `OGLTextRenderer.c`) and `adjustSubpixel` (`SKBackend.cpp`), so ties round up deterministically in every pipe. Red test first: new `SubpixelVariantTieTest` (every half-step of 4 gray steps at 1× and 6 LCD steps at 1× and 1.25×, through a BufferedImage and a VolatileImage, must render like the upper step). Green after the fix (commit `JBR-10654 Skia: a sub-pixel position halfway between two variants rounds up in every pipe`, `…-windows8`); all other tests green. The two pipes now render identically; JetBrains Mono at 1.25× LCD went from ~350 px > 32 (Skia pipe) to 70–81 px per row, max 49 (one sixth-step) — the rest are, most likely, DirectWrite's own float ties: its positions accumulate in DIPs (7.8 DIP per glyph, not representable), so an exact device-pixel tie is decided by noise on its side too. Sheets `out/2026-10-23g/`; 1× and 2× unchanged (gray 11.7 / 22.4, LCD 9.1 / 12.5 whole-sheet means, underlines included).

## 22. Text rendering configuration across the three platforms — the map and a design (2026-11-02, draft for the user's review)

**Why.** The knobs grew platform by platform and borrowed macOS words: `sun.java2d.skia.text.smoothing` means stem dilation on macOS, would mean grid fitting on Windows and hinting on Linux; `text.gray=cg|smoothed|darkened|coverage|platform|platformcoverage` mixes *who rasterizes* with *how heavy the stems are* and with two emulations. The VM's `SKPlatform_FontHinting()` (`210bc044d7de`, Windows `kNormal` so DirectWrite decides) and the Mac's `SKPlatform_FontSmoothingEnabled()` (`2958023cd952`) are the first two platform-default hooks; the design below generalizes them.

**22.1 What exists.** *Public Java2D:* `KEY_TEXT_ANTIALIASING` (OFF, ON, LCD_HRGB/HBGR/VRGB/VBGR, GASP, DEFAULT), `KEY_FRACTIONALMETRICS`, `KEY_TEXT_LCD_CONTRAST`; the desktop hints `awt.font.desktophints` (`awt.useSystemAAFontSettings`): Windows from `SPI_GETFONTSMOOTHING / TYPE / CONTRAST / ORIENTATION` (antialiasing mode, LCD order, contrast), Linux from fontconfig / Xft (`Antialias`, `RGBA` → the antialiasing mode and LCD order; `FC_HINT_STYLE` / `FC_AUTOHINT` per font reach only the JDK's own FreeType scaler, `setupLoadRenderFlags`: none → `FT_LOAD_NO_HINTING`, slight → `TARGET_LIGHT`, else the fontconfig load flags, autohint forced when set), macOS nothing (grayscale always; an LCD hint yields neutral masks). *Scaler:* `sun.java2d.font.scaler=jdk|skia`. *Ours (`sun.java2d.skia.*`):* `text` = glyphs | skia | shaper | paragraph (which layout and which drawing path), `text.gray` = coverage | smoothed | darkened | cg / platform | platformcoverage (the glyph source for grayscale, plus two emulations of macOS weight), `text.grayDarkening` (the emulation's em fraction), `text.smoothing` (Skia's text path asks its host for the platform's smoothing — `kNormal` on the CoreText host), `text.luminanceClasses`, `text.cgLuminance` (how platform masks depend on the text's brightness), `text.lcdColorBits` (Windows: LCD masks keyed by the text colour), `lcd` = twopass | accumulate | … (how LCD coverage is blended on the GPU), `lcdOrder` = system | rgb | bgr (the surfaces' sub-pixel geometry), and macOS's `metal.textGammaCorrection`. *Hooks:* `SKPlatform_FontHinting()`, `SKPlatform_FontSmoothingEnabled()`, `SKPlatform_LcdOrderIsBGR()`, `SKPlatform_SmoothedGlyphMask()`, `SKPlatform_SyntheticStyle()`. *Skia's own knobs (SkFont):* hinting none | slight | normal | full, edging alias | AA | subpixel AA, sub-pixel positioning, linear metrics, force autohinting, embedded bitmaps, embolden; `SkSurfaceProps` geometry and the text gamma / contrast.

**22.2 What one Skia hinting level does per host — the root of the confusion.** FreeType (Linux; the JDK's scaler everywhere but macOS): a *geometry* control — none: unhinted outlines; slight: the autohinter, vertical only; normal: the font's bytecode or the autohinter, both axes; full: the same with LCD targets; stem darkening is a separate FreeType property (off by default for TrueType). DirectWrite (Windows): also geometry, but coarser — none / slight: grid fitting *forced off*, symmetric natural mode; normal / full: the font's recommended rendering mode (the gasp table at the size) with the default grid-fit mode, i.e. DirectWrite's own per-font decision, plus ClearType's contrast enhancement from the rendering parameters. CoreText (macOS): no geometry control at all — any level above none is read by Skia's host as "font smoothing on", a *stem dilation* (≈ 0.013 em), which is what `AppleFontSmoothing` toggles for AppKit. So "hinting" is snapping on two platforms and weight on the third, and "smoothing" (macOS) is the cousin of FreeType's stem darkening and DirectWrite's contrast enhancement — a weight dimension, not an antialiasing one.

**22.3 Revision (user: "glyphs" and "platform" are unclear — what platform?).** Looking for better names showed the "source" dimension was wrong. On every platform *every* route renders glyph masks with the operating system's text engine: CoreGraphics on macOS (the JDK's canvas through JRS, our per-luminance context, Skia's CoreText host), DirectWrite on Windows for the Skia scaler's routes (our per-colour analysis and Skia's DirectWrite host alike), FreeType on Linux; Skia's own path rasterizer draws only the `darkened` emulation, and the JDK's FreeType scaler is the one real alternative engine on Windows. What differed between "cg / platform" and "coverage" was never *who* rasterizes but *how the engine was asked*: smoothing on or off, grid fitting on or off, rendered for the text's colour or as colour-independent coverage, gamma applied by the engine or by the pipe. Those are the weight, hinting and blend dimensions already in the model; and the Java-glyph-images-versus-Skia's-strike difference is the path dimension that exists (`text=glyphs|skia|shaper|paragraph`). The word "platform" was carrying two meanings, "the operating system's engine" and "the way Java2D's default route asks it"; both go.

**22.3b Second revision (user: "path" is generic — is it shaping?; FreeType on Windows is neither consistent nor worth exposing; "linear" collides with linear-light blending).** *Path* bundled three choices: who turns characters into positioned glyphs (Java's layout — its own mapping plus HarfBuzz through `SunLayoutEngine` — or Skia's shaper, HarfBuzz through SkShaper with Skia's Unicode module and font fallback), who lays out rich text (`TextLayout` / `LineBreakMeasurer` or SkParagraph), and whose strike makes, caches and composites the glyph masks (Java's `FontStrike` images through the pipes' atlases, or Skia's `SkStrike` through Skia's atlas and canvas — both stacks call it a strike). The old values were the diagonal of that cube: `glyphs` = all Java, `skia` = Skia's strike, `shaper` = + Skia's shaping, `paragraph` = + Skia's paragraphs. *Engine on Windows*: today's default is not one engine — grayscale through FreeType for every font, LCD through GDI for installed fonts and FreeType for file fonts (`sun.java2d.font.lcdGlyphs` is the GDI switch) — and the Skia scaler is the first configuration that is one engine (DirectWrite) consistently; the engine follows `sun.java2d.font.scaler` and is not a dimension to expose. *Linear*: Java2D's `gamma` blend is not linear-light blending — it is a contrast curve on the coverage (`KEY_TEXT_LCD_CONTRAST`, Metal's text gamma, Skia's text gamma / contrast tables are the same kind), and the "plain" blend interpolates sRGB-encoded values; neither is linear in light, so "linear" names nothing accurately.

**22.3c The model, third form.**

| dimension | question | values | `system` per platform |
|---|---|---|---|
| **strike** | whose strike makes, caches and composites the glyph masks | `java2d` · `skia` | — (a route choice) |
| **shaping** | who maps characters to positioned glyphs, including fallback fonts | `java2d` · `skia` (needs strike = skia) | — |
| **paragraphs** | who lays out attributed text | `java2d` · `skia` (needs strike = skia) | — |
| **antialiasing** | edges and LCD order | the Java hint: none · grayscale · lcd, plus `lcdOrder` | the desktop hints, as today |
| **hinting** | whether outlines snap to the grid | `system` · `none` · `slight` · `normal` · `full` | Linux fontconfig's hint style per font · Windows DirectWrite's recommended mode · macOS not applicable |
| **weight** | whether stems are thickened beyond the outline's coverage | `system` · `none` · `emulated` (catalog only) | macOS `AppleFontSmoothing` · Windows ClearType's contrast enhancement · Linux FreeType's stem darkening (off unless set) |
| **blend** | how coverage meets the text colour and the background | `system` · `plain` · `corrected` | masks rendered for the text colour where the engine does that (CoreGraphics per luminance, DirectWrite per colour); the pipes' correction curve where Java2D always applied one |

`blend=plain`: coverage interpolates the sRGB-encoded colour and background as stored (today's "plain"). `blend=corrected`: the coverage goes through a contrast or gamma curve first — Java2D's LCD contrast, Metal's text gamma correction, Skia's text gamma and contrast — an approximation of linear-light blending, not the thing itself. `blend=system`: no blending correction in the pipe because the engine already rendered the mask for that colour (the luminance classes and `lcdColorBits` tune how many colours). `weight=none`: the outline's coverage with no stem adjustment (what was called "coverage"). The engine is CoreGraphics on macOS, DirectWrite on Windows and FreeType on Linux whenever `sun.java2d.font.scaler=skia`; the JDK scaler's mixed engines on Windows stay the legacy configuration, not a property of this design.

Today's routes in these words: the JDK's macOS text = strike java2d, weight on, blend plain; "cg / platform" = weight system, blend system; "coverage" = weight none, blend corrected; "platformcoverage" = weight none, blend plain; `text.smoothing` = weight system with strike skia; `text=paragraph` = strike, shaping and paragraphs skia.

**22.4 Mapping onto Skia per engine (internal).** `hinting` → `SkFont::setHinting` on FreeType and DirectWrite; on CoreGraphics the flag is commandeered by `weight` (the only switch Skia offers for CoreGraphics' smoothing) and `hinting` is reported as not applicable. `weight` → `CGContextSetShouldSmoothFonts` (our contexts) or the hinting flag (Skia's CoreText host); DirectWrite's rendering parameters (`none` needs parameters without contrast enhancement — to verify on the VM); FreeType's `no-stem-darkening` property. `antialiasing` → edging and `SkSurfaceProps` geometry as now. `blend=corrected` on the skia strike is Skia's own text gamma and contrast; on the java2d strike the pipes' curves. **Linux caveat:** Skia's fontconfig manager applies fontconfig's settings only to fonts it matches; file fonts (the IDE's bundled ones) need the fontconfig query the JDK's scaler already does (`FontConfigInfo`) applied to the SkFont by us.

**22.5 Properties (third proposal).** `sun.java2d.skia.text.strike=java2d|skia`, `sun.java2d.skia.text.shaping=java2d|skia`, `sun.java2d.skia.text.paragraphs=java2d|skia` (the three replacing `text=glyphs|skia|shaper|paragraph`; a skia shaping or paragraphs value implies strike skia), `sun.java2d.skia.text.hinting=system|none|slight|normal|full`, `sun.java2d.skia.text.weight=system|none|emulated`, `sun.java2d.skia.text.blend=system|plain|corrected` (tuning: `luminanceClasses`, `lcdColorBits`), `sun.java2d.skia.text.lcdOrder=system|rgb|bgr`. Retired: `text`, `text.gray`, `text.smoothing`, `text.grayDarkening` (aliases for one release if wanted). The hooks fold into `SKPlatform_TextDefaults()` — the engine's hinting, weight and LCD order for this user, read once per process. Defaults: the three route choices as the shipping decision sets them, everything else `system`.

**22.3d Third revision (user: what does `corrected` cover; rename `weight`; drop the emulations).** *Blend `corrected` is an intent with three implementations today:* the software loops' LCD LUTs built from `lcdTextContrast` (default 140 unless the engine renders per colour, then 100; grayscale in the loops is plain), Metal's text shader's curve (grayscale and LCD; `sun.java2d.metal.textGammaCorrection`), and Skia's own text gamma and contrast tables on the skia strike (`SkSurfaceProps` without text gamma / contrast means Skia's defaults; measured 21 % lighter than the loops' glyph images on the same masks). The dimension says *that* a curve is applied, not which; aligning the three to one definition (the Java2D contrast exponent, implemented in each pipe and handed to Skia as its surface text gamma / contrast) is implementation work the design calls for but does not assume. *Weight → `darkening`*: the common effect across CoreGraphics' font smoothing (a stem dilation), DirectWrite's enhanced contrast (a coverage boost) and FreeType's stem darkening (an outline emboldening) is heavier stems than the outline's coverage; "contrast" is taken by the blend curve (Java's `KEY_TEXT_LCD_CONTRAST`), so `darkening = system | none`. *The emulations go*: `text.gray=smoothed` (Skia's LCD edging averaged to gray) and `darkened` (the outline widened by `grayDarkening` em and filled by Skia's path rasterizer) were steps toward macOS' look before the cg route rendered through CoreGraphics itself; nothing ships them, the catalog's arms that used them become retired arms (captures stay). Removes `GRAY_SMOOTHED`, `GRAY_DARKENED`, `grayDarkening` and their branches in `SKFontScaler.cpp` / `.java`.

**22.3e The model, fourth form (current).**

| dimension | values | `system` means |
|---|---|---|
| strike | java2d · skia | — |
| shaping | java2d · skia (needs strike skia) | — |
| paragraphs | java2d · skia (needs strike skia) | — |
| antialiasing | the Java hint + `lcdOrder` | the desktop hints |
| hinting | system · none · slight · normal · full | fontconfig per font · DirectWrite's recommended mode · not applicable on macOS |
| darkening | system · none | AppleFontSmoothing · ClearType's enhanced contrast · FreeType's stem darkening |
| blend | system · plain · corrected | per-colour masks where the engine renders them · the pipe's correction curve where Java2D applied one |

Properties: `sun.java2d.skia.text.{strike,shaping,paragraphs,hinting,darkening,blend,lcdOrder}`; retired `text`, `text.gray`, `text.smoothing`, `text.grayDarkening`.

**22.6 What the catalog's dimensions become.** `scaler` → the engine named in the arm (CoreGraphics, DirectWrite, FreeType), `gray` → weight × blend, `blend` → blend (plain / corrected / system), `layout` → shaping × paragraphs, and a new `strike` column (java2d / skia); `renderer` stays. Arm descriptions name the engine and the `system` preference each platform honours, never "platform".

**22.7 Open before implementing.** (1) Windows `weight=plain`: can Skia's DirectWrite host be given rendering parameters without contrast enhancement (needed only for the catalog's plain-coverage arm, not for shipping)? (2) Linux: whether the IDE's file fonts get fontconfig's hint style through Skia today (expected: no) — the Linux chunk's first measurement. (3) Whether `fitting` deserves a user-visible property at all on macOS (no effect) — probably reported as "not applicable" rather than silently ignored. (4) Re-reading preferences while the process runs (AppKit and GTK do on notification; the desktop hints do for antialiasing) — a later refinement.

**22.8 Implemented (2026-11-02, night; §12).** Properties as in 22.3e/22.5; `weight` shipped as `darkening`; the retired spellings are aliases, the emulations are deleted. Hooks: `SKPlatform_TextDefaults()` (one struct per process) and `SKPlatform_FontHinting(request, darkening)`; `SKPlatform_SmoothedGlyphMask` takes the darkening flag. One rule added beyond the design: darkening rides on Skia's text path's font and on the platform mask, never on the strike's own host coverage (the CoreText host's smoothed A8 is a pre-blended luminance mask, not coverage — see the §12 entry). Of 22.7: (1) still open (Windows blend plain without contrast enhancement — the catalog's arm only); (2) the Linux chunk's first measurement; (3) resolved: `hinting` is accepted everywhere and documented as not applicable on macOS; (4) still a later refinement. New: the Windows VM should verify the DirectWrite host under the default (blend system, hinting request −1 = kNormal) and `hinting=none|slight|full` through `text.hinting`; the user's stated interest is to bring the full Skia route (strike skia) closer to the platform's rendering rather than align the three correction curves.
